# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1989

## Introduction by Nareśvara dāsa

What is significance of the yearly Vyāsa-pūjā book? For most temple presidents and other leaders in the Kṛṣṇa consciousness society, it can mean a hard time of wracking one’s brain trying to put thoughts and glorification of Śrīla Prabhupāda down on paper to meet the production deadline. For the devotees in general it is something we look forward to receiving every year around the time of Vyāsa-pūjā. But for all it is a sharing of realizations about Śrīla Prabhupāda, a glorification that helps us remember and meditate upon him.

To be sure, others on the periphery of ISKCON may view this yearly effort to glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda as a rather expensive exercise in sentimentality; they may, of course, appreciate the devotee’s loyalty and commitment to a venerable religious teacher of the modern age – but then, there have been so many saints down through the ages. What makes Śrīla Prabhupāda so special?

To answer this question, we need to look back at the history of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In *Śrī Navadvīpa-dhāma-mahātmya,* Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura describes how Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu appeared to the founder*-ācāryas* of the four *sampradāyas* – namely Śrīpāda Madhvācārya, Śrīpāda Rāmānujācārya, Śrīpāda Viṣṇu Svāmī, and Śrīpāda Nimbakācārya – and gave each the order to preach and establish particular tenets of pure Vaiṣṇava *dharma* in preparation for His descent. Thus Madhva taught the tenets of refutation of Māyāvāda philosophy and the transcendental service of the *mūrti* of Śrī Kṛṣṇa; Rāmānuja taught the tenets of *bhakti* unpolluted by *karma* and *jñāna,* and service to the Vaiṣṇavas; Viṣṇu Svāmī taught exclusive dependence upon Kṛṣṇa, and the path of *rāga-bhakti*; and Nimbarka taught the process of taking shelter of the lotus feet of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and the love of the *gopīs* as the highest spiritual attainment.

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu descended to preach *nāma-saṅkīrtana* and pure love of Kṛṣṇa, and He incorporated the above tenets into His own *saṅkīrtana* movement in order to make them available to one and all. Lord Caitanya and His associates demonstrated the supreme efficacy of the *yukta-vairāgya* principle by spreading His intensely ecstatic *hari-nāma saṅkīrtana* movement all over India, overwhelming all varieties of materialistic philosophies and sectarian social designations in a tidal wave of pure love of Godhead.

Moreover, Lord Caitanya desired that His *saṅkīrtana* mission be carried to every town and village on earth. In Śrī Caitanya-maṅgala, by Locana dāsa Ṭhākura, Lord Caitanya speaks of a *senā-pati*, a great general who would come after Him to accomplish this very mission. Thus we can understand that Lord Caitanya’s extraction of the essence of the *Vedas* and Vaiṣṇava *dharma*, and His rendering of that essence into an easily consumable form, prepared the way for a later preacher personally empowered by Him to launch His *saṅkīrtana* movement on a global scale.

In “Nityānanda Suryodoy,” an article in his magazine *Sajjana-toṣaṇī,* Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura expresses his yearning to see the day when fortunate non-Indian Vaiṣṇavas would perform *hari-nāma saṅkīrtana* in the cities of England, France, Russia, Germany, and America. In this article he announced that Lord Caitanya’s prediction would shortly be fulfilled. Though his son, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, sent preachers to foreign countries, he did not see this vision fulfilled during his time on earth. But he spoke of a self-effulgent *ācārya* from among his disciples who would preach on a grand scale.

Furthermore, this *ācārya* would finally establish a spiritual edifice in which all the other Vaiṣṇava *sampradāyas* would find shelter, as foreseen by Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura. As Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta writes in *Bhaktivinoda-vāṇī-vaibhava,* Ch. 6, “In a short time, there will be only one *sampradāya*, or devotional line. That will be the Brahma-sampradāya. All the other *sampradāyas* will find their conclusions in the Brahma-sampradāya.”

Who is the founder*-ācārya* of such a worldwide Vaiṣṇava movement, that fulfills these predictions? The answer is obvious: His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda 108 Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the founder*-ācārya* of the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness.

The following verses from the *Prapannāmṛta-tarpaṇa* (62.47-51) glorify Rāmānujācārya as the founder of his movement, but they apply equally well to Śrīla Prabhupāda:

*tebhyas sarva-jana-trātā*  
*sarva-śreṣṭhatamo dhruvam*  
*dīnābhaya pradānārthaṁ*  
*viṣadī-kṛtya bhū-tale*

**”Among all** ***ācāryas*****, certainly he who is the deliverer of all people, who appears in this world for the purpose of removing the fears of the fallen souls (*****dīnābhaya*****), is the most perfect one.”**

*gītācāryokta-tatyārtha-*  
*caramārtha-prakāśanam*  
*ṛṣi-prokta-pramāraṇaṁ*  
*kṛtvā vyākhyānam uttmam*

**“He shed light on the ultimate meaning of the philosophical conclusion spoken by Kṛṣṇa, who is the speaker of** ***Bhagavad-gītā.*** **He also gave perfect purports to the evidences given by the** ***ṛṣis.”***

*divya-prabandhān lokebhya*  
*śatakopādibhiḥ kṛtān*  
*arthopadeśa-paryantaṁ*  
*vidhāyitvā ca śāśvataḥ*

**”He propagated everywhere the meanings and purports of the songs of the previous Vaiṣṇava** ***ācāryas*****.”**

*nāthāryādi-gurūṇāṁ ca*  
*śatakkopādi-yoginām*  
*āhṛtya vyāsa-sūtrāṇāṁ*  
*arthān loka-hitāya ca*

*śrībhāṣyādi-mahā-granthaṁ*  
*vidhāya karuṇārnaya*  
*nirjitya sarva-samasyān*  
*vādena śataśo ‘śubhān*

**”For the welfare of the people, he, an ocean of mercy, contributed great books that explained the meaning of Vedānta strictly in line with the previous Vaiṣṇava** ***ācāryas*** **and devotees. In this way he conquered hundreds of inauspicious philosophies with his arguments.”**

Having grasped the unique position of Śrīla Prabhupāda, one can easily understand the significance of this Vyāsa-pūjā book. It is an annual outpouring of love and devotion from those who have taken shelter of the movement that he established, which is the fully potent manifestation of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement in the modern age. In actual fact, the Vyāsa-pūjā book is not really glorification enough of Śrīla Prabhupāda. If we continue to publish this book annually for the next ten thousand years, we will still not be able to complete the glorification of this personality, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who so kindly graced us with his presence.

Nareśvara dāsa  
with thanks to the  
Bhaktivedanta Academy  
at Śrīdhāma Māyāpura

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Kavicandra Swami

When I was asked to write this offering, my first thought was that I don’t really understand “the meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā,” so such an assignment would be quite difficult. As with anything, the meaning must be different to different people. *Munis* could argue over various meanings of the words and over various interpretations. Certainly a non-devotee, after making a thorough investigation of the occasion from his external perspective, would understand it quite differently that the devotees. Even among the devotees, we all have our individual relationships with Śrīla Prabhupāda. So there must be unlimited meanings.

After considering these factors, I decided to compile some of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s statements of the issue. His authority must be superior, and his explanations are always pleasing to all. I don’t think that the following comprises all he spoke on the subject, but at least here are a few of the things he said directly addressing the matter:

”Please offer my Vyas puja blessings to every one of you. I am so much pleased with the book put together by you. It will be read tomorrow in the meeting. Similar publication was done by the ISKCON Press. So I thank you very much, all of you, for appreciating my humble service which I am trying to render as a matter of duty ordered by my Guru Maharaja. I request all my disciples to work cooperatively and I am sure our mission will advance without any doubt.” (letter to Tamāl Krishna, Aug. 14, 1971)

”In the Mayavadi Sampradaya they observe the Guru Purnima day to offer respect to the Spiritual Master. This system of offering respect to the Spiritual Master is current in all sections of Vedic followers, but so far we the Gaudiya Sampradaya is concerned, we offer on the Appearance Day of the Spiritual Master our humble homage, and this particular auspicious moment is called Vyas Puja.” (letter to Bali Mardan, Aug. 25, 1970)

”Yesterday we held Vyas Puja Ceremony, and it was very nice. I shall take the opportunity now to thank you for the nice booklet you have printed and for the part in this booklet written by you. After all of the reading was finished, I explained how service and prayers are accepted by Krishna through the medium of the disciplic succession. It is something like electricity: if one is in touch with his Spiritual Master, then in this way an offering is automatically transferred to Krishna. Just as the mercy of Krishna is coming down through the disciplic succession, so the service of the devotee is offered up to Krishna through the disciplic succession.” (letter to Brahmānanda, Sept. 5, 1969)

”Today they will observe Vyas Puja ceremony (my Birthday Anniversary), so from this day, I will be stepping on the 73rdyear. I hope the remaining days of my life may be utilized to serve you all Western devotees of Krishna. Please pray to Krishna that He may give me the necessary strength to discharge the duty entrusted by my Spiritual Master.” (letter to Hayagrīva, Aug. 17, 1968)

”I am in due receipt of your Vyas Puja offering dated August 22, 1974, received just now, and I am very glad to receive it; better late than never. I thank you very much for your sentiments. Satisfaction of the spiritual master is the secret of advancement in spiritual life. The Lord is the original Spiritual Master, and a person in the disciplic succession can convey the message of the Lord as it is to his sincere disciple. We cannot manufacture our own process; therefore mental speculation does not at all help us in spiritual life. One simply has to surrender himself to his guru, and everything will be revealed to him.” (letter to Śrījī-devī dāsī, Nov. 7, 1974)

”Of course, that time was just the moment when Kali, the predominating deity of the present age, peeping to infiltrate in the worldly affairs and later on it was complete through the exigency of an upstart brahmana boy. So Vyasadeva was a real person accepted by all authorities, and anyone can judge how wonderful he was to compile the Vedic literatures. He is therefore known as Mahamuni. Muni means thoughtful or great thinker or great poet, and Maha means still greater. So there is no comparison of Vyasadeva with any writer or thinker or philosopher. Nobody can estimate the scholarly importance of Srila Vyasadeva. He composed many millions of Sanskrit verses, and we are just trying to receive a fragmental knowledge out of them by our tiny efforts only. Srila Vyasadeva therefore summarized the whole Vedic knowledge in the shape of Srimad Bhagavatam, which is known as the ripened fruit of the desire tree known as Vedic knowledge. The ripened fruit is received hand to hand through disciplic succession from Srila Vyasadeva, and as such the bona fide Spiritual Master’s Appearance day is worshipped as Vyas Puja. Not only that, the exalted seat on which the Spiritual Master sits is also called Vyasasan.” (letter to Bali Mardan, Aug. 25, 1970)

One Indian scholar, being overwhelmed by Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books, declared that he must be the incarnation of Vyāsadeva. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s reply was, “Vyāsa-prasāda, the mercy of Vyāsadeva.”

”We are observing Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony, the birth anniversary of our Guru Mahārāja. Why? We cannot understand Kṛṣṇa without the spiritual master. That is bogus. If anyone wants to understand Kṛṣṇa while jumping over the spiritual master, then immediately he becomes a bogus. Therefore, Caitanya Mahāprabhu says, *guru-kṛṣṇa kṛpāya pāya bhakti-latā bīja.* That is the Vedic injunction: *tad vidhi praṇipātena paripraśnena sevayā.* Nobody can understand Kṛṣṇa without going through His most confidential servant.

”This is the meaning of this Vyāsa-pūjā. You cannot surpass. If you think that you have become so very learned and advanced that now you can avoid the spiritual master and understand Kṛṣṇa, that is bogus. That is the meaning of this Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony. We should always pray, *yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādaḥ. Yasya prasāda:* only by the grace of the spiritual master can we achieve the grace or mercy of Kṛṣṇa. This is the meaning of this Vyāsa-pūjā – offering obeisances by the *paramparā* system.” (lecture)

”So today is a very auspicious day, Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda’s birthday. Here is the picture of Ṭhākura Sac-cid-ānanda Bhaktivinoda. He was one of the ācāryas of this disciplic succession from Kṛṣṇa. We have got a succession table from Kṛṣṇa, a genealogical table. There are two kinds of genealogical tables. The first one is by semina – father, his son, his son, like that. That is the material genealogical table. And there is one spiritual genealogical table, the disciplic succession, coming down from Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa, the original father, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, spoke the Vedic knowledge to Lord Brahmā, Brahmā spoke to Nārada, Nārada spoke to Vyāsa, Vyāsa spoke to Madhvācārya. So in this disciplic succession is Lord Caitanya. From Lord Caitanya came the six Gosvāmīs, and similarly, coming down, down to Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, then Gaura-kiśora dāsa Bābājī Mahārāja, then to my spiritual master, then we are next generation, my disciples. So there is a disciplic succession.” (lecture in London, Sept. 23, 1969)

”So in front of Caitanya Mahāprabhu you are chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra,* and you are taking part in it very seriously. So my Guru Mahārāja will be very, very much pleased upon you and bless you with all benefits. So he wanted this, and it is not... it is not that he is dead and gone. That is not spiritual understanding. Even an ordinary living being, he does not die: *na hanyate hanyamāne śarīre*. And what to speak of such an exalted, authorized personality as Bhaktisiddhānta? He is seeing. I never feel that I am alone. Of course, I came to your country without any friends, without any means. Practically just like a vagabond I came. But I had full faith that ‘My Guru Mahārāja is with me.’ I never lost this faith, and that is a fact.” (lecture on Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura’s appearance day, March 2, 1975)

”The spiritual master is not a new invention. It is simply following the orders of the spiritual master. So all my students present here who are feeling so much obliged, I am also obliged to them because they are helping me in this missionary work. At he same time, I shall request them all to become spiritual master. Every one of you should be spiritual master next. And what is their duty? Whatever you are hearing from me, whatever you are learning from me, you have to repeat the same in toto, without any addition or alteration. Then all of you can become spiritual master. That is the science of becoming spiritual master.” (Vyāsa-pūjā address, Hamburg, Sept. 5, 1969)

”Personally, I have no hope for any direct service for the coming crores of births of the sojourn of my life, but I am confident that some day or other I shall be delivered from this mire of delusion in which I am at present so deeply sunk. Therefore let me with all my earnestness pray at the lotus feet of my divine master to allow me to suffer the lot for which I am destined due to my past misdoings, but to let me have this power of recollection: that I am nothing but a tiny servant of the Almighty Absolute Godhead, realized through the unflinching mercy of my divine master. Let me therefore bow down at his lotus feet with all the humility at my command.” (address on the appearance day of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, Bombay, Feb. 1936)

And in a poem that was greatly appreciated by his spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda summarized the issue:

The oldest of all,  
But in new dress.  
Miracle done,  
Your Divine Grace.

Kavicandra Swami

## Śrī Vyāsa-Pūjā homages from the G.B.C.

## Agrāṇi Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Thank you very much for the wonderful life of Kṛṣṇa consciousness that you have given to me. Who could have guessed that this hapless and lowly fool would meet with such great good fortune? You picked me up from the depths of ignorance and despair and placed me on the splendid path of devotional service. I find great satisfaction in executing all devotional activities, especially chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. Because of you, life is now free and happy, and the future is as brilliant as the sun. To me, your appearance in this world is the most auspicious event in the history of creation.

Thinking of your kindness and the unique opportunity it affords us, who could help but sing and dance in exaltation? The supreme perfection, eternal association with the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Kṛṣṇa, in full knowledge and transcendental bliss, is within reach of us all. This is your inconceivable mercy. You have given us everything. For what more is there to aspire or ask?

There is something more, however, though not due to any deficiency on your part. And to attain it I again must beg your mercy. Like a dependent child, I have been “simply taking, taking, taking.” I have taken so much, but have given very little in return. Spiritually I am still very immature and beset with many material desires. I heartily enjoy the many wonderful blessings you kindly bestow upon me, but being selfish and forgetful, I do not consider how I may serve you in return. Thus even while enjoying the blissful life of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, I feel a personal dissatisfaction because of my indifference to pleasing you. My mind’s selfish conditioning is overwhelmingly strong and obstinate. Though I wish it would simply go away or disappear, it does not yield. I deeply regret this condition.

I want to become your pure servant, and there is so far yet to go. True, your mercy has already carried me over many formidable obstacles, but I am ever dependent on it. To overcome this most formidable of all obstacles – *ahaṁ-mameti*, egocentric selfishness – I require your special mercy. Kindly help me to completely submit to your divine will. I want that my every thought, word, and action, my heart and my life, be engaged in assisting you in your glorious mission of benedicting the world with Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Agrāṇi Swami

## Badari-nārāyaṇa dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

When I was a child, I read a story about a young boy who found a magic lamp. Upon rubbing it, a genie came out and offered the boy three wishes. I remember thinking, “What would I wish for if I had three wishes?” Well, I would wish to be rich. But what if I got sick? So I’d wish for good health. But what if I died young? So I’d wish for a long life. But what about my family and friends? How will I be happy unless they are also rich and healthy and have long lives? What if my family, my friends, and I are all rich and healthy and have long lives, but there is a war or natural disaster? And what about the suffering of others? But I’ve already used up my three wishes. In this way my small child’s mind would contemplate and become bewildered. I concluded that it was best to wish for unlimited wishes on my first wish, and then I could cover all the problems and possibilities. One wish would enable me to fulfill all of my desires.

Years later I had the great good fortune to hear Your Divine Grace debunk my childish proposal. You told the story of a wood-carpenter who was chopping wood in the forest. It was a hot day and the work was hard, so the woodsman sat under a shady tree to rest. Musing to himself, he said, “I wish this wood would just stack itself.” Lo and behold, the wood began to stack itself! The woodsman was struck with wonder, but he gathered his wits and wished again: “Axe, chop wood by yourself.” Sure enough, the axe began to chop wood on its own. The woodsman was thrilled as he thought, I must be sitting under a desire tree, and it is fulfilling all my demands.” He then wished for an elegant silk tent, fine music, delicious foods, and beautiful women. In this way, swept away by his newfound opulence, the woodsman failed to notice how the whole day passed. Suddenly noticing the fading rays of the sun, the woodsman thought, “Oh, no, I’m out in the forest at night! Certainly a tiger will come and eat me.” You laughed, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as you expertly delivered the punch line: “... and sure enough, because the woodsman had said it, immediately a tiger came and ate him.” You explained that material desires “burn like fire and are never satisfied,” and that even those that are temporarily satiated breed suffering for the conditioned souls. Your purport was that we shouldn’t stop desiring, but that we should learn the art of desiring properly.

Now I know what to wish for. I need only one wish, which will actually satisfy me and be the best service to my family, friends, and all mankind in general. Please always engage me in your pure devotional service. Your saṅkīrtana movement is spreading the rays of Lord Caitanya’s benediction moon, which soothes, relieves, and nourishes all living entities. I am dull and full of faults, but I do have some small glimmer of faith in the power of your association via service. My only hope is that by seeing my feeble attempts to assist your devotees and your ISKCON movement, you will tolerate my presence until the power of the holy name can cleanse my heart and I become a genuine disciple and faithful son. Please fulfill my desire to always be engaged in your service.

Your mercy is all I am made of.  
Badarī-nārāyaṇa dāsa

## Bhakti-bhūṣaṇa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisance at the dust of your holy lotus feet.

Because I am ungrateful and bereft of all good qualities, it is very difficult for me to write a heartfelt offering to you. My heart is steel-framed, and everything I can think of is empty words. There is no way to accomplish this task in my usual whimsical way. It is a very sobering experience, as by your causeless mercy I can perceive my own shortcomings very clearly and feel a sense of complete helplessness.

In my desperation I have been reading some of the offerings of your followers from last year, and I’m very impressed by their deep realizations and their sublime love for you. How will I ever be able to come up to their standard? I’m unworthy of being in their company, and I can see it is your mercy alone that has kept me from falling away all this time. It is through your sincere disciples and grand disciples that you have guided me over the years and pushed me to accept the burden of love more and more. This burden has become much too heavy for me, as is obvious to everybody. I know that if I don’t learn how to surrender to you fully, I could easily be crushed under it.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please force me to fix my attention on your lotus feet. I want to be your insignificant servant and nothing more. Please give me the intelligence to realize a drop of the truth contained in this verse from the *Śikṣāṣṭaka:* “O almighty Lord, I have no desire to accumulate wealth, nor do I desire beautiful women, nor do I want any number of followers. I only want your causeless devotional service, birth after birth.”

*Your lowly servant,*  
Bhakti-bhūṣaṇa Swami

## Bhakti Charu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisance at your divine lotus feet.

Once again I am compelled to meditate upon you. Under the pretense of serving you, so often I drift away from your lotus feet, but your very merciful devotees always bring me back to your divine shelter.

So often I want to offer myself completely to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but helplessly I get deluded by *māyā* and forget my resolution.

Remember the day you gave me initiation. At that time I was your personal servant. I used to be with you all the time; therefore that day I could not go out to collect my *guru-dakṣiṇa* for you. I felt extremely bad that I did not collect anything to offer you. When everyone left the room and I was alone with you, I said, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave me dīkṣā, but I did not offer you any *dakṣiṇa* as yet.”

You smiled and replied, “You offer yourself; that is the best *dakṣiṇa.”*

Submerged in an ocean of ecstasy, I offered you obeisances and prayed silently, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am your forever; please accept me as your eternal servant. I offer you my ‘self’ as my *guru-dakṣiṇa.”*

When I got up, I found that your merciful eyes were fixed upon me and your face was bright with your usual radiant smile. I felt that you had heard my prayer and accepted my *guru-dakṣiṇa.*

You accepted me, but how much of myself did I offer you? When I met you, you first asked me to offer this life to Kṛṣṇa, and I resolved that I would. In your association, everything was so easy. In your presence, *māyā* could not spread her influence. You made us swim in an ocean of ecstasy. How wonderful were those days! All we wanted was just to be with you: to listen to you, to chant the holy name sitting close to you, or to massage your soft and pink lotus like feet. Will those days ever come back? Will you come back in our midst, Śrīla Prabhupāda? Or shall we be transported, due to some inconceivable good fortune, to wherever you are?

That day in Vṛndāvana, I asked you, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, should I serve the *prasādam*?” You were reluctant to eat, but seeing me standing there, you agreed at least to sit in front of the plate. I helped you walk to the table and you sat down, but you did not have any appetite or the strength to eat. You just sat there with your hands folded on your lap. At last you said in a tired voice, “You can see that I cannot eat, so why do you waste your time cooking for me, when you have so much to do? From tomorrow, you rather let someone else cook for me.”

I said, “When you get better, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I will let someone else do that, but now please let me cook for you.”

You replied, “I will get better only when I die.”

My heart ached at the thought that soon you might leave us, and tears came streaming down from my eyes. I tried to hide my face with my hands and bury it in my chest. In your deep, resonant voice, you kept on speaking: “Someday we all have to leave this world. So why lament? You are not this body, nor am I. So why lament for something that we are not? Didn’t I teach you that the soul doesn’t die?”

Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, now I realize that you did not leave us. It is a long way for us to go back to the spiritual world, where we can associate with you eternally, but even in our minds you are so much alive.

I can close my eyes and go back to those days in Vṛndāvana. In your room, I see myself sitting on the floor next to your bed. Haṁsaduta sings in a soft and sweet voice: *govindam ādi-puruṣaṁ tam ahaṁ bhajāmi.* You listen, lying on your bed with your eyes closed and your hands clasped on your chest. Your body shines with such a brilliant glow. The stupid doctor says it is due to an accumulation of salt in your body. He is so unfortunate; he does not know about your spiritual identity. He thinks you are just an ordinary patient.

I move close to you and softly place my hand on your shining chest. You respond by just saying "*Hmm*."

"Will you take some fruit juice, Śrīla Prabhupāda?"

You shake your head to express your unwillingness.

"Just take a little bit, please."

You open your mouth, and I pour some pomegranate juice very carefully into your mouth. You swallow it without any protest. With a handkerchief I wipe your mouth. Now Haṁsaduta sings saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka-. Bharadvāja walks into the room and, after offering obeisances to you, starts to play the harmonium. You whisper, "Tell him not to play the harmonium." I convey your wish to Bharadvāja. He immediately stops. Then you ask, "Who was that?"

"Bharadvāja Prabhu," I reply.

"Oh, he can play."

He sings *yaṣomatī-nandana brajavara-nāgara.* You listen to the sublime glorification of your Lord.

Someone comes in and tells me that your sister, our "Piśima," has arrived with her son Chandrababu. I rush out to receive her and bring her in. She stands close to your bed, and I tell you that she has come.

"Ask her whether she would like to cook for me." You are always so concerned to please everybody. You knew that she always liked to cook for you. Therefore, even though you cannot eat and have not eaten for two and a half months, still you encourage her to cook for you.

Piśima asks me, "What will he like to eat?"

You respond, "Ask her what will she like to cook."

She insists that you suggest, and finally you submit: "She can cook some *kicharī* for me."

She walks out of the room like affection personified, and I follow her to help her in the kitchen.

You do not like to lie on a bed. That is your divine restlessness. You decide to go on Govardhana *parikrama,* and Lokanātha Mahārāja arranges a bullock cart. I do not want you to go, because I know that if you go, you will not come back.

I think I will ask Bābāji Mahārāja to stop you when he comes in the evening. But when he comes, I am not there, and you already have told him about your plan. He likes the idea and agrees to join you. When I come in, I find the two of you planning the trip. My last hope to stop you has been shattered, but still I do not give up. In the privacy of the next room, I tell Bābāji Mahārāja about your plan to leave us behind in that bullock cart ride. So he walks up to you and says, "I cannot let you go."

You surrender: "If you say so, then I will not."

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we tried to prevent you from leaving us, but your will is supreme because you act according to Kṛṣṇa's desire. Kṛṣṇa wanted you to go back to Him. Out of your causeless mercy, you came to remind us about Kṛṣṇa and kindle the desire in our hearts to go back to Him. But you are so dear to Him that He could not bear your separation for too long, so He came and took you back. And we, your helpless children, cry in speechless anguish.

Although I know that you are always with us, still I cannot see you, touch you, or speak to you. Sometimes it seems that you are testing our eagerness to be with you. If we really want you, then we have to prove our love for you. You showered your love upon us; now it is our turn to reciprocate. We have to put all the feelings of our hearts at your lotus feet. Very mercifully, you allowed me to associate with your vapu. Now let me serve your vāṇī. Let me realize that your instructions are nondifferent from you, your devotees are nondifferent from you. Now let me see you through all that you have left behind to flood the world with the love of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

If you really want to go, I can always see you, but it is more important that you see me - that I become aware that you are constantly watching me. I can feel the warmth of your merciful glance. I can hear your profound assurance: "I am always with you. Just keep on rendering your service to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu." I know that if I just turn around, I will see you standing there just beside me. All I have to do is open my spiritual eyes.

Desiring to remain engaged in the service of your lotus feet eternally,  
*Your most insignificant servant,*  
Bhakti Caru Swami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

Most worshipable Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya*  
*jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena*  
*tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

For the treatment of this temporary body, I am now staying in the Ārya Vaidyaṣālā, an Ayurvedic nursing home in South India, and for the first time I am recalling some of your divine instructions regarding your deep concern for the care of one's physical health.

Whenever I came to have the darśana of Your Divine Grace after some interval of separation, your first question was always "How is your health?" On one morning walk on Venice Beach, I did not bring enough warm cloth. Whey you saw me like that, you were so upset, and like a concerned father, you chastised me heavily: "Why do you come like that? Why are you so careless about your health?" So immediately Śukadeva Prabhu gave me one of his jackets for the morning walk. Your compassionate eyes showed signs of satisfaction and appreciation at Śukadeva Prabhu's timely gesture. In Vṛndāvana during your last days, my duty to attend Your Divine Grace fell during odd hours, from 9:00 p.m. to 1: a.m. You usually were awake at midnight. At that time you would speak in a low voice in Bengali, recalling many past events. On many of these occasions you felt that I had not had enough rest, since you always saw me at midnight, by the side of Your Divine Grace. You would ask me, "Are you taking enough rest? Take care of your health." Śrīla Prabhupāda, even at that difficult time you forgot your own physical condition and were more concerned about the well being of your insignificant and unworthy servant.

At this stage of our society, we do not have the proper consciousness to look after someone when he or she gets really sick. Śrīla Prabhupāda, whey you were physically present, you showed so much care and concern for all of us. But now... ? A few years ago, Bhāvabhūti Prabhu, who built the ISKCON Guwahati temple, wrote a letter to the GBC body in Māyāpur. He wrote in the letter that he had given his life to ISKCON, and that now he needed substantial financial help for his treatment. Would the GBC - the ISKCON society - do anything for him? I do not know how many unreported cases there are like that. At such a time, a devotee needs not only financial help but also psychological support from the Society. I do not know how Satsvarūpa Mahārāja felt when he was seriously ill, or Harikeśa Mahārāja. They probably depend on their good disciples for their many needs. I am fortunate to have been taken care of by Dr. Sukumaran (Śrīman Sanandana-kumāra dāsa) and his good wife while staying here. I am grateful to them. But how many can afford to be in that category? It seems that we have to do something in this area. Some great souls should come up with and implement a good proposal.

On this most auspicious day, Śrīla Prabhupāda, kindly bless me so that I can serve you better.

*You insignificant servant,*  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhakti-tīrtha Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We, your disciples and grand disciples, surely at heart all want to be able to immensely please you. These days, when it comes to details, practically no two leaders share the same ideas on more than two or three or so major issues. This is very unfortunate, because it divides us so much as a family and at the same time has its way of making the devotees scream out to you for your help, guidance, and Kṛṣṇa's intervention more than ever before.

No one can hide in this movement anymore behind position or even structure. Unity within diversity is a healthy sign of maturation, but obviously if or when we minimize the essence or begin to see each other as enemies, all hell breaks loose and everyone suffers, the devotees as well as the karmīs, who we are meant to help. Kṛṣṇa consciousness being such a spiritual science, anyone who is not really holding on to your lotus feet will be exposed or has already been exposed. We seem to all be making a few mistakes here and there, but the significant factor is how you are evaluating such incoherence. It is obvious that if we are highly displeasing you, our fever of sense gratification will be rising very rapidly, and if you and Kṛṣṇa are somewhat pleased (an indication that we are engaging in proper devotional service), then one will be acquiring causeless knowledge and detachment:

*vāsudeve bhagavati*  
*bhakti-yogaḥ prayojitaḥ*  
*janayaty āśu vairāgyaṁ*  
*jñānaṁ ca yad ahaitukam*

**"By rendering devotional service unto the Personality of Godhead Śrī Kṛṣṇa, one immediately acquires causeless knowledge and detachment from the world." (*****Bhāg.*** **1.2.7)**

It may not hurt for each devotee to evaluate his fever. In this way we can see if it is rising or falling. If we choose not to do this, it does not matter so much because if one is not properly connected, his resistance will be insufficient to handle the accelerated attacks of *māyā,* and this can be a barometer to let him know how he must immediately upgrade his devotional character before *māyā* totally devastates him.

These times of such incoherence in the material and spiritual settings are wonderful opportunities for devotees to obtain all necessary credentials for selfless service and empowerment.

In spite of the many difficulties, by your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, everywhere I am beginning to see such wonderfulness amongst the Vaiṣṇava community. By your mercy, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have made all of our fortunes. You have given us the holy name and have informed us how Kṛṣṇa is nondifferent from His name. You have given us such wonderful books, which contain teachings that can give us answers to any problems. You have created a society where devotees and nondevotees can participate in spiritual culture, but most important, you have given us yourself. You lived and died for us, and now we must die to live for you. We must all help each other to give up the unnecessary body, i.e., the *anarthas* and the improper subtle body, we can give you the biggest offering - ourselves in full surrender. After all, if we don't establish a sufficient internal connection with you and the other great pure devotees, we will have only ourselves to blame as we lie on the lap of *māyā,* wondering how we have once again become such sense gratifiers. However, unless you keep forgiving us and reaching out more to help us, how will we survive?

I, for one, take this auspicious day to beg forgiveness for any disturbances I caused to my loving Godbrothers and nieces and nephews, for they are your pride. I also beg forgiveness for being such an immature representative for your mission. However, I will not rest a second until I can be a truly surrendered disciple. Today I vow to die to live for you. We love you dearly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we pray that one day we will be able to show you our true love by doing the thing that will please you the most, which is to become pure lovers of Kṛṣṇa, free of any ulterior motives and free from all of the three modes of nature, so as to be able to engage in unconditional loving services to Kṛṣṇa.

*Your little son,*  
Bhakti-tīrtha Swami

## Gaura Govinda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto 'pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**"By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement. Therefore, I should always remember and praise the spiritual master. At least three times a day I should offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master."**

O my ever-worshipable eternal spiritual master, O lord of my heart, His Divine Grace Abhay Charan Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, kindly accept my prostrated daṇḍavat praṇāmas at the holy dust of your divine lotus feet on this occasion of your sacred appearance day.

How, being such a tiny, insignificant, unworthy creature, am I able to glorify the unending and infinite glory of such a great and dearmost devotee of Kṛṣṇa as Your Divine Grace? It is an impossible and perhaps self-deceptive job for this useless and most unworthy servant of yours. Your Divine Grace far transcends what you appeared to be. The glories of Your Divine Grace know no bounds of time, place or distance. They can even eclipse many a polar star and surpass the height of many millions and billions of Himalayan mountains.

I am taken back to the far-off days of January-February 1977, when Your Divine Grace, out of your causeless mercy, placed your holy feet on the long-cherished soil of Bhubaneswar, the capital of Orissa, the land of Lord Jagannātha and of the sacred pastimes of Śrīmān Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu. Demonstrating the ideal quality of Vaiṣṇava simplicity, you preferred to remain for sixteen days in a mud hut that had been erected on the land donated to you here in Bhubaneswar instead of at the nearby, well-built and well-arranged State Guest House of the government. By your very divine presence, those then out-of-the-way outskirts of Bhubaneswar were delightfully surcharged with the pure transcendental energy of the unalloyed, nectarean sweet love of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa. Thus that otherwise usual site was turned into a rare spiritual sight and a memorable pilgrimage for many fortunate aspirants and sincere seekers of your divine *darśana*.

O my beloved spiritual master, more than twelve long years have now passed since the day that you, with your divine hand, laid the foundation stone for the temple of Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma on the holy appearance day of Lord Nityānanda Prabhu. At that time you made the holy declaration that this last-founded project of yours would become one of the world’s best Vaiṣṇava centers. Still, unworthy, most wretched, and degraded as I am, the construction of Their Lordships’ temple stagnated very painfully for a long time, and even after the recent resumption of construction, the rate of progress on this long-cherished project of yours is still very slow. This really gives great pain to this worthless servant of yours. There is some insignificant consolation, however, in the fact that by the strength of your causeless mercy, the translation of your priceless nectarean spiritual publications into the Oriyan language is somehow continuing, although not nearly as fasts as would give you satisfaction.

Yes, my dear spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, although I am a great offender, an unworthy, weak, and low disciple of yours, I beg your kind permission to offer this prayer at the lotus feet of Your Divine Grace:

I cannot, I cannot, I cannot…  
But I am quite hopeful at heart,  
Only by the perennial source of  
the mighty strength of your  
Divine causeless mercy.

Conditioned soul that I am, I am full of faults.  
But if you won’t take those away, where do I go?

In the *śāstra* it is said the spiritual master is *guru karṇa-dhāra,* the captain of the ship to take the sincere disciple across the ocean of birth and death.

Please catch hold of my unworthy ear,  
O my revered spiritual master,  
and bring me to the path, lest I lose all hope.

You are my only hope.  
You are my only source.  
You are my only shelter.  
O my dear Gurudeva, kindly accept me as a  
speck of the holy dust of your lotus feet  
and thus qualify me to engage in your sacred service.

*Your eternal but unworthy servant,*  
Gaura-Govinda Swami

## Girirāja Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace and your numerous servants.

We enact this drama for our purification and your glorification.

**Back Home**

**I. Your Presence**

**Devotee:** Śrīla Prabhupāda, nobody will accept me.  
**Prabhupāda:**That’s all right. I’ll take you. Just come to me.

**II. Your Anxiety**

**Prabhupāda:** Where is that rascal? I am calling him. Why he is not coming?

**III. Your Call**

**Prabhupāda:**Come back! Come back!! Come back!!! I will still take you.

**IV. Your Servants’ Mercy**

**Devotee:** Śrīla Prabhupāda, I’m back.

**V. Your Mercy**

**Prabhupāda:** Thank you very much. We were waiting for you. There is service for you.

\* \* \*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you know everything; still you have asked us to express our realizations in writing, so we explain.

The drama can be understood in different ways in different contexts. In one, you are in Goloka and we are here. You call us, but we do not go. Due to our material attachments, we are stuck. Due to your devotees’ mercy, we become liberated.

In another reading, you are wherever the devotee is, and the devotee is estranged from ISKCON. You are always available to guide us and accept our service. Yet when we lose the favor of the Vaiṣṇavas, or when we are not properly engaged or encouraged, serving you becomes difficult. You call us, but because we have become weak, offensive, we do not respond. But when your servants encourage us, instruct us, and help us practically, we regain our strength and come back.

In one other reading, you are in Bombay and the devotee is me. “Girirāja can never leave Bombay.” But when I could not enter Bombay, I concluded that it was Kṛṣṇa’s arrangement. Maybe it was our desire.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we need the devotees and the order of the *guru.* If we neglect either one of them, we create havoc.

Still, by the efforts of your servants, I may yet return, “back home, back to Bombay.”

*Your fallen servant,*  
Girirāja Swami

## Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day of your appearance on this planet, please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Every year when I sit down to write my Vyāsa-pūjā offering, I realize how unqualified I am in attempting to describe your transcendental glories. Your appearance on this planet, along with the appearance of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, is the most significant development in Kali-yuga. You appeared in order to help realize Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s prediction that His holy name would spread to every town and village. If it were not for your unlimited mercy, I and millions of others would still be rotting in *māyā*.

In your dealings, you manifested all the qualities of an ideal Vaiṣṇava. Your every thought, word, and example was exactly according to the śāstras. In spite of thousands of followers, comfortable living conditions, receiving donations, etc., you always remained the humble and pure devotee of Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Your Bhaktivedanta Purports will continue to illuminate the dense darkness of ignorance in this age for the next ten thousand years. In your powerful purports, you have destroyed the atheistic propaganda and impersonalistic views. The message of the Vedic texts, which for thousands of years was locked up in Sanskrit and known only to a few in India, has been made available in almost every major language of the world.

Your poured mercy just like the rain cloud pours torrents of rain on this dry earth. Like an inexhaustible well of clear, pure water, you quenched the thirst of the conditioned souls for the nectar of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You opened “spiritual hospitals” all over the world so that people of all races could become enlightened about the mission of human life. These spiritual hospitals, or temples, are like the embassies of the Supreme Lord.

You did not let any obstacle obstruct your preaching mission. To you, the word *impossible* exists in the fool’s dictionary. Even old age did not slow down your preaching and translating activities. Your determination to preach and transcend all material impediments reminds us of the mood of Grandfather Bhīṣma, who remained in perfect meditation on Lord Kṛṣṇa even while pierced by so many arrows.

Your divine efforts have altered the course of history from a path of illusion and bewilderment to one of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and happiness. In dedicating the First Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* to your spiritual master, you have said that “He lives forever by his divine instructions, and the followers live with him.” Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura has said, “He reasons ill who says that Vaiṣṇavas die, when thou are living still in sound.”

In your letters, *darśanas*, and purports, you have made it clear that there is no alternative to devotional service. As each day passes, Kali-yuga is becoming more and more intense. We will be able to save ourselves, as well as others, by constantly meditating on your instructions, just as Arjuna reflected on Kṛṣṇa’s teachings after the Lord departed from the planet. This material ocean is an ocean of faults, and no doubt this ship of ISKCON will have to face the storms. But as you have built a strong ship and prepared precise navigational charts (your books), we will definitely be able to sail across the material ocean.

In conclusion, on this most auspicious day, I just want to beg your mercy so that I may be able to keep serving you. Please bless me to remain in your service. I have come to the realization that I am like a spider carrying my little grains of sand compared to your other disciples, who are lifting huge boulders and accomplishing great things for you.

*Your servant,*  
Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

## Guru Gaurāṅga dāsa

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet.

Today is the most auspicious day of the entire Vaiṣṇava calendar. It is the appearance day of our beloved spiritual master, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. I count myself among the most fortunate as I stand before the vyāsāsana to glorify Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your superhuman efforts you spread the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement all over the world. You traveled far and wide in obedience to your *guru-mahārāja’s* order to preach the eternal truth of *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. As Sanātana Gosvāmī exchanged his new quilt for an old, ragged one, you willingly exchanged bodily comforts for the rigors of worldwide preaching. In plain and simple terms, you taught me that I am not this material body but a pure spirit soul, an eternal part and parcel of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. You explained how my forgetfulness of Śrī Kṛṣṇa has caused me to languish in the material world, a brutish captive of the cycle of repeated birth and death.

Most importantly, you showed me how I can break free from that morbid cycle. You taught me to hear and chant the Lord’s Holy Names. Your revealed the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* as the surest means of going back home, back to Godhead – Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

You gave me the most precious gift, and I am eternally indebted to you. I wish to repay you but know that this is not really possible. Still, as the coin of the transcendental realm is chanting and hearing, I know that I can please you by chanting the Lord’s holy names and remaining faithful to your pure example. Please bless me with a fraction of your unflagging determination. Please engage me always in your devotional service.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your menial servant,*  
Guru Gaurāṅga dāsa

## Guru-prasāda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most fallen obeisance in the dust off your lotus feet. Just as Kṛṣṇa has unlimited potencies, you manifest limitless spiritual opulence in being a perfect manifestation of the Servitor Personality of Godhead. Kṛṣṇa made you famous by your selfless service continuously offered to Him. You never took credit for anything, always giving it to your *guru-mahārāja* or Kṛṣṇa, or even to your disciples. But no one could be as famous as you, and your fame has only begun to surface.

We could perceive so much beauty in your smile or in your kind words of appreciation, even though we were not very qualified to receive them; however, we knew that Kṛṣṇa was pleased by the mercy of His pure devotee. And I still cannot fathom how powerful you were. Defeating nescience at every opportunity, you tirelessly injected Kṛṣṇa consciousness into the dull brains of those who tried to challenge the authority of Kṛṣṇa. You circled the globe at an indefatigable pace to serve Kṛṣṇa, and we are exhausted after a short trip. Previously*, kṣatriyas* had the strength of ten thousand elephants, but that doesn’t compare to the endurance you demonstrated by founding, forming, and empowering with your teachings the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

Your knowledge took the vast writings of Vyāsadeva, the whole Vedic conclusion, expanded them, and established their relevance to this world we live in – without losing one drop of transcendental potency. To the contrary, you have brought the *māyāvādīs* to their knees, dwarfed the greatest *yogīs*, and utterly smashed the materialistic scientists and empiricists. No wonder your books are the guiding beacon for all humanity for the next ten thousand years. Anyone who thinks otherwise should try to fathom the miracle that you performed.

Kṛṣṇa gave you wealth that the most powerful magnates in the world could not imagine. Thousands and thousands of devotees offering their lives to you; millions and millions of books distributed, and more still coming by your preaching potency, seeds which you have planted that will capture the entire world, interlaced between their transcendental sound vibrations. And you never saw any of this as your own. The power of complete submission offered to you and millions of dollars could not affect you in the slightest because you have the greatest wealth: Kṛṣṇa fully manifest within your heart at all times. You are so dear to Kṛṣṇa that He took you from our visible midst to join His eternal *līlā*, which you actually never left.

And actually you never left us, either. Your books, lectures, tapes, and teachings are touching and purifying our hearts more and more, manifesting your glories daily. That is the opulence of the pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa.

Please allow this lowly servant to continue his attempt to serve your lotus feet. Never let me forget that whatever tiny service you award me is your mercy, not my creation. Let us always remember how you are making our service qualified to please Kṛṣṇa; otherwise we have no claim. All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your humble servant,*  
Guru-prasāda Swami

## Harikeṣa Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisance thrown in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you on this most auspicious day.

As fire comes from wood, as heat and light come from fire, as pearls come from oysters, as milk comes from cows, as good progeny come from good parents, as diamonds come from the earth, as knowledge comes from the *śāstra,* as life comes from the sun, as medicinal herbs come from the forest, as desirable things come from the Vedic *yajñas,* as soothing rays come from the benediction moon, so all desired results in ISKCON come from your books.

Please bless me to always produce, print, and distribute your transcendental literatures without any deviation for the rest of my life. Please bless us, your disciples and grand disciples, to never lose track of this primal source of all spiritual inspiration.

Everything comes from your books, for they are the basis of everything. We glorify you best by simply serving your book distribution mission. You are still present in these books, ever fresh and vibrant. Therefore by serving the *saṅkīrtana* mission, we are serving you powerfully and directly.

Your books shine with a youthful vigor which is eternally beyond the contamination of the material world. In fact, you did not really leave us when you left your physical form; you remain here still through your books. Who wouldn’t want to be part of your great *saṅkīrtana* mission?

Thank you.

I know this offering will find you well and happy, for you are always engaged in the transcendental mission of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu wherever you may be.

*Your servant,*  
Harikeśa Swami

## Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto 'pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaṣas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

As the devotees of ISKCON continue to chant their daily sixteen rounds and follow the four regulative principles, and as they continue to faithfully serve your divine mission, the devotees gradually advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, moving steadily toward their full maturity in pure love of Godhead. As the devotees grow up in spiritual life, they can more and more appreciate your unique transcendental qualities, your extraordinary degree of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

You are fully qualified to be the founder*-ācārya* of ISKCON, since you are situated on the highest platform of spiritual understanding. In countless ways, you set a perfect example for all humanity. By following you, all people can advance directly toward the perfection of their lives, pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness, for there is no obstacle, no mistake or imperfection in your path.

How fortunate is ISKCON to work under your direct shelter! How blessed is service unto your lotus feet! You have demonstrated the truth that you are indeed nondifferent from your Society, and that Lord Kṛṣṇa will always protect and guide your faithful followers, since you yourself are the most faithful follower of the previous *ācāryas*. On the blessed day of your divine descent into this world, we humbly pray that we may remain forever at the shelter of the lotus feet of the faithful servants of your lotus feet. Please manifest the glory of Kṛṣṇa here on earth so that all conditioned souls may discover their real, eternal life in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Falling in the dust of your feet, I beg to remain,

*Your tiny servant*,  
Hridayānanda das Goswami

## Jayapatāka Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I beg you to offer my respectful obeisance unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who has inundated the Western world with the transcendental flood of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu's *saṅkīrtana* movement!

Your Divine Grace so kindly brought us Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the Western world. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu states in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* that unless one has the transcendental mercy of Lord Kṛṣṇa he cannot spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu: *kṛṣṇa-kṛpā binā tār pravartana*. You are undoubtedly the embodiment of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu's mercy and are empowered to deliver the whole universe with Lord Caitanya's flood of love of Godhead! On the occasion of Your Divine Grace's Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, I reconfirm my surrender and dedication to serving your lotus feet, even life after life, and pray for your causeless mercy and beg forgiveness for any offenses committed.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Jayapātaka Swami

\* \* \*

In the *Caitanya-maṅgala,* by Locana dāsa Ṭhākura, a book referred to in the purports of the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* it is explained how Nārada Muni visited Goloka Vṛndāvana and met Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Nārada Muni found Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu being personally served by Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī and Her associates on one side, and by Rukmiṇī Mahārāṇī and her Dvārakā female companions on the other. A *mahā-abhiṣeka* bathing ceremony was being given to Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu by the *śaktis*. When Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu learned of Nārada Muni's anxiety for the conditioned souls, who were forgetting their eternal loving relationship with Lord Kṛṣṇa in the universe of the four-headed Brahmā, the Lord called all of the *śaktis* around and made a declaration. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu said He was going to descend into the material world and personally flood everyone with love of Godhead. Even the dogs and other animals would get the mercy, what to speak of the human beings. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu mentioned that He would personally preach and give out love of Kṛṣṇa, but even those places where he would send His *senāpati-bhakta* ("commanding general devotee"), who would preach His *saṅkīrtana* movement in other, distant places in the world and inundate those places with love of Godhead, delivering the remaining sinful persons.

Doesn't Śrīla Prabhupāda fit the description of the *senāpati bhakta* referred to by Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu? This realization struck me, giving yet another, deeper appreciation for the unique position Śrīla Prabhupāda holds in our disciplic succession. His Divine Grace's appearance was not a coincidence, but part of the great plan of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu to flood the world with love of Godhead by the transcendental chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*. Śrīla Prabhupāda visited all the distant continents of the world and went to the fallen souls of those places, delivering them from sinful activities by giving them the taste of the eternal nectar of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Śrīla Prabhupāda is specifically empowered by Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu to lead the modern world back to Godhead. It is imperative that everyone follows in his lotus footsteps.

His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda's founding of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness was for the express purpose of satisfying the desires of His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Ṭhākura and other previous *ācāryas*. ISKCON is a living expression of Śrīla Prabhupāda's personal ecstasy of devotional service offered to the lotus feet of Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga. ISKCON is described in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* as one of the branches on Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu's divine tree. Since the Caitanya tree is transcendental, even one branch has the capacity to spread and cover the entire universe. Śrīla Prabhupāda certainly desired that the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement spread everywhere and establish Lord Caitanya's *saṅkīrtana* movement in every town and village all over the world.

It is proper to remember the most unfortunate situation that Śrīla Prabhupāda is delivering the conditioned souls from by connecting them to Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s disciplic succession. There would have been no connection without the causeless mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda. Śrīla Prabhupāda realized the instructions of His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura and the previous *ācāryas*, as well as the practical method of implementing them. The failure of others to fully represent the previous *ācāryas* is expressed by Śrīla Prabhupāda in his purports to the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā*.For the International Society for Krishna Consciousness (ISKCON), His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda is the founder*-ācārya.* The unique relationship Śrīla Prabhupāda has with ISKCON and its devotees is no less important and significant to ISKCON than the relationship the entire disciplic succession has with the succession’s founder*-ācāryas*, such as Rāmānujācārya and Madhvācārya. It is Śrīla Prabhupāda who is our founding connection with the disciplic succession. He will continue to be the guiding inspiration, force, and shelter of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. In the *Harināma-cintāmaṇi,* His Divine Grace Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura has explained the position of a succession’s *ādi-guru*, or founder*-ācārya*, in relation to that succession. Although ISKCON is a branch of an older disciplic succession, since Śrīla Prabhupāda is the *ādi-guru*, or founder*-ācārya*, of this specific branch and the empowered representative of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu, it is appropriate for those devotees of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu following Śrīla Prabhupāda to offer him the respect, loyalty, and devotion due an original *guru* of a succession. As explained below, the honor Śrīla Prabhupāda’s followers show him should be unique in comparison to that shown to other *gurus* of the disciplic succession:

*sampradāyer ādi-gurur śikṣā avalambana kariye ācaraṇ karibe:*

*sādhu-sampradāye, ācārya-sakala,*  
*śikṣā-guru-pratiṣṭhita*  
*ādyācārya jini, guru-śiromaṇi,*  
*pūji tān’re yathosite*

*tān’ra susiddhānta, anugato ho’ye*  
*na mānibo anya śikṣā*  
*tāṅhāra ādeśa, pālibo yatane,*  
*no loibe anye dīkṣā*

**”In the disciplic succession of Vaiṣṇavas, the previous** ***ācāryas*** **are established respectfully as** ***śikṣa-gurus*** **for the** ***sampradāya*****. However, the founder*****-ācārya,*** **or original** ***guru*** **of a disciplic succession, is appropriately worshiped and respected as the crown jewel \[topmost\] of the spiritual masters. His perfect philosophical conclusions are to be followed by all in the** ***sampradāya;*** **any contrary instructions will not be accepted. His orders are to be carefully carried out, and initiation is not to be taken from another succession.” (*****Harināma-cintāmaṇi*****)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda instructed that every letterhead, invitation, and piece of literature should contain His Divine Grace’s name as founder*-ācārya* of ISKCON. His Divine Grace established a spiritual and administrative tradition with the GBC (Governing Body Commission) of ISKCON as his representative and the ultimate managing authority of ISKCON. Mūrtis of Śrīla Prabhupāda and His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati Ṭhākura were established on the altars of the Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Temple in Vṛndāvana and other temples. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books form the basis of ISKCON’s study and preaching programs. His Divine Grace’s instructions for developing the holy *dhāmas* and expanding Kṛṣṇa consciousness everywhere are the inspiration. It is not possible to express all the ways Śrīla Prabhupāda’s relationship and position in ISKCON are unique, but these are some examples. Once taking shelter of Śrīla Prabhupāda, one should accept any and all initiations and instructions from true representatives of Śrīla Prabhupāda coming in his disciplic succession. This obligation to the founder*-ācārya* is confirmed by the *ācāryas* and Vedic literatures.

Śrīla Prabhupāda instructed that even a branch of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s tree may become most prominent. Everything is possible by Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s inconceivable potency. It is the special responsibility of the followers of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda to at least preserve his instructions and contributions and to preach so that they may grow and endure for at least the next 9,988 years.

OṀ TAT SAT

## Kavicandra Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our most humble obeisance.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Each year, as the magnitude of your preaching manifests itself, it seems more and more difficult to find the proper words to glorify you. As we sing every day, “the *Vedas* sing of your glories.” But in spite of our inability, you kindly give us the chance to try. Just thinking of any of your qualities and activities is ecstatically purifying. Please be kind upon this fallen soul and allow me to always remember you and to become your true follower. You prefaced your translation of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* with this profound instruction: “To Śrīla Prabhupāda Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Goswami Mahārāja, my Spiritual Master, on the 26thAnnual Ceremony of His Disappearance Day: He lives forever by his divine instructions, and the follower lives with him. You told us, “I had full faith that ‘my *guru-mahārāja* is with me.’ I never lost this faith, and that is a fact.”

By your unflinching faith in your spiritual master, and by your undeviating execution of his instructions, you performed miracles. You turned numberless *mlecchas* into Vaiṣṇavas. And your books, which contain all the instructions of the *paramparā,* are still doing that, with ever-increasing potencies. Furthermore, they will continue to expand your name and fame for the next ten thousand years, at least. You are eternally glorified by your books. Your followers and your temples also glorify you. No one can repeat your accomplishments, for you have already spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world.

You gave the book *bhāgavata* to the world. But more important, you acted as the perfect “person *bhāgavata,”* making it possible for anyone to understand the book *bhāgavata,* which otherwise be inaccessible. You kindly moved about all over this planet and interacted with so many people, making possible so many memories of your sublime nature. This gives us some understanding of what faith really is, although it is so sublime and beyond the vision of those “who have lost their vision due to the dense darkness of the Kali-yuga.” As one policeman in London once noted, you were “glowing.” You showed that there is something beyond this world and misery.

Lord Ṛṣabhadeva instructed His sons, “O My sons, you should accept a highly elevated *paramahaṁsa*, a spiritually advanced spiritual master. In this way, you should place your faith and love in Me, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. You should detest sense gratification and tolerate the duality of pleasure and pain, which are like the seasonal changes of summer and winter. Try to realize the miserable condition of the living entities, who are miserable even in the higher planetary systems. Philosophically inquire about the truth. Then undergo all kinds of austerities and penances for the sake of devotional service. Give up the endeavor for sense enjoyment and engage in the service of the Lord. Listen to discussions about the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and always associate with devotees. Chant about and glorify the Supreme Lord, and look upon everyone equally on the spiritual platform. Give up enmity and subdue anger and lamentation. Abandon identifying the self with the body and the home, and practice reading the revealed scriptures. Live in a secluded place and practice the process by which you can completely control your life air, mind, and senses. Have full faith in the revealed scriptures, the Vedic literatures, and always observe celibacy. Perform your prescribed duties and avoid unnecessary talks. Always thinking of *bhakti-yoga,* you will patiently and enthusiastically be elevated in knowledge and will be able to give up the false ego.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 5.5.10-13)

Many volumes have been written, and many more will come, describing how you have perfectly followed all of these instructions. Please allow me to always hear these wonderful narrations and to be able to always attempt to be your follower. If I can even hope that in some future lifetime I could be with you, then my life will be successful.

*Your aspiring follower and servant,*  
Kavicandra Swami

## Kīrtirāja dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisance at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace and your causeless mercy, which you are bestowing upon us all.

Well, Śrīla Prabhupāda, here I am again, late as usual. I’m sorry, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that I have not written to you sooner, but I have been neglectful again. I can tell you what I have been doing, but you already know that. I can try to make my excuses for being late, but I’ve been doing that since I was a child. If there is ever a chance that I may go back to Godhead, I’m afraid I may be late for that, also. Please forgive me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for being late once again.

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ*  
*paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande*  
*śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, this verse clarifies your position and everyone else’s relationship to you. Śrīla Prabhupāda, without your mercy, there is no hope for anyone in this material world, as we are all spiritually dumb, lame, and blind. And even if someone has many so-called “good qualifications,” they mean nothing without your mercy. They are like so many zeroes without the one in front of them. That “one” is you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have come to realize very clearly that it is not possible to do anything without your mercy, and I have also come to realize that by your mercy ANYTHING is possible.

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ‘pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is not possible to make any advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness without your mercy. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we would have no idea of what the words “Kṛṣṇa consciousness” even meant if it had not been for you. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have made the term “Kṛṣṇa consciousness” a common word in countries throughout the world. For example, even the Uttar Pradesh Government Office of Tourism welcomes visitors to Vṛndāvana with the greeting “Welcome to the Land of Shree Krishna Consciousness.” They would not have used this term had they not heard it from you. You have gotten the most unlikely candidates to chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra*. And one of the MOST unlikely candidates has even become your disciple.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you continue to reveal to us daily what Kṛṣṇa consciousness is, along with the depths of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. However, sometimes, like young children, we are afraid of the deep water and only want to play in the sand, building our “sand castles,” only to have them washed away by the next big wave of *māyā*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are doing everything. Your potency is revealed by how, even after your physical disappearance from our association, you are personally pushing on your mission by inspiring not only your disciples and grand disciples but even ordinary persons to serve Lord Kṛṣṇa and the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu in some way or other.

Once, in New York City, when I was driving you in the car to the old Brooklyn temple, a taxi driver pulled up alongside you and turned to me and said, “Even an ordinary person can understand when he sees a saintly person.” So, even ordinary persons can understand your extraordinary spiritual position, although certainly not in full. And some of them can even understand the spiritual quality and importance of your message and mission – again, not in full, of course, but they can understand something, and some even want to help you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I think that all of us, even the dullest of us all, myself, have understood that you are still present with us and directing this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Otherwise, there would be no question of this movement going on. You always wanted to be freed of ISKCON management so that your translation work could go on undisturbed. However, due to our shortcomings, this was never possible, and you had to continue to manage, even in seeming petty affairs. I guess that you had to force us to grow up and take responsibility for your mission and your movement.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, daily when I offer *pūjā*, I pray to you that you will always keep me in the shelter of your ISKCON movement and under the shade of your lotus feet. I pray that you will never let me leave your ISKCON movement, your association and service, and the association and service of your sincere servants. I pray that you may engage me as you see fit in your mission and the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and that you will always guide me so that I may become a pure devotee of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī and Lord Kṛṣṇa and of Their dearmost servant, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. And I pray that you will please allow me and empower me to be your instrument.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, this material world, as you have pointed out so clearly to us, is a place of suffering. The suffering is unlimited; it knows no end. Once, on a morning walk in Māyāpura, I was telling you about the preaching in Poland, and I said that without Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the people of these eastern European countries would be “finished.” You immediately responded by saying that there was no question of being finished, but that without Kṛṣṇa consciousness their suffering would go on. Śrīla Prabhupāda, only by your grace is it possible for one’s suffering to finished. This suffering condition will come to an end only when we take complete shelter of your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I used to think (somehow or other) that I did not mind coming back to this material world to serve you lifetime after lifetime. I knew that this is the attitude of the great souls, but somehow or other I thought like this. I guess I was just pretending. But now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I don’t want to stay in this material world – unless, of course, you are somewhere in this material world, engaged in your preaching mission. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to join you; I want to have your personal association again.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I’m a child in Kṛṣṇa consciousness; I need your association and instructions constantly. I feel this material nature to be like the big bully on the block. I want and need to hang on to my father for dear life. I’m completely overpowered by the material nature, and you are the only one who can protect me. I know, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that I am an ungrateful child, but I pray that you will please never let me break free of your loving and protective grasp, no matter how much I may try or how much someone else may try to wrench me away from my father’s protection to beat me to a pulp.

Maybe someday, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I may honestly have the realization that I don’t mind being in this material world lifetime after lifetime if I can only serve you. But for now, I’m fed up with this material world and only want to be in your association again. Of course, I know that we are always in your association if we strictly follow your instructions. My attempt to follow your instructions is feeble, so I’m hoping, praying, and begging for your association and mercy. However, by your unlimited kindness, despite all my disqualifications, you shower your mercy on my head. It is the only thing that keeps me going. I pray and beg, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that that shower will never cease but rather turn into a downpour. I want to drown in the ocean of your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, instead of only floating.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, while trying to work hard for you, I also want to remember you. I don’t want to forget you and your instructions. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, help me to become worthy of being your disciple.

I beg for your continued mercy and engagement, and I hope that I may become and forever stay.

*Your faithful servant,*  
Kīrtirāja dāsa
