## Loka-śākṣī dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, please accept my most humble obeisance at your lotus feet, which are worshiped all over the universe. All glories unto you.

In the Fourth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (4.28.51purport), you write, “As the Supreme Personality of Godhead comes down upon this earth to reestablish religious principles. It is the duty of the disciples to take charge of the mission of the spiritual master and execute the will of the spiritual master, the disciple should be prepared to lay down his life and abandon all personal considerations.”

This instruction very clearly describes the sign of a real and sincere follower of yours. It describes real chastity and surrender on the part of the disciple. And this is possible only in the association of those who are real devotees of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Your Kṛṣṇa consciousness society was established solely for the purpose of creating a society of real and pure devotees. As you write, “Transcendental devotional service cannot be complete and cannot be relishable without the association of devotees. We have therefore established the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness. Anyone who is trying to be aloof from this Kṛṣṇa consciousness society and yet engage in Kṛṣṇa consciousness is living a great hallucination, for this is not possible… It is clear that unless one is associated with devotees, his devotional service does not mature; it does not become distinct from material activities.”

With this understanding, and also understanding my fallen and useless condition, I beg to remain the humblest of the servants of the real servants of your ISKCON.

Loka-śākṣī dāsa

## Mukunda Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**”I offer my respectful obeisance unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.”**

**”Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Goswami. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

You said once in a lecture that “Arjuna was always associating with Kṛṣṇa. He did not know anything else – Kṛṣṇa.”

You too, Śrīla Prabhupāda, know nothing other than Kṛṣṇa. *Vyavasāyātmikā-buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana / bahū-śākhā hy anantās ca buddhayo ‘vyvasāyinām.* Throughout the broad range of your “practical” activities – dictating letters, teaching devotees accounting, instructing disciples on Vedic standards of hygiene, solving complex ISKCON management problems, respecting *prasādam,* resting a mere four hours a day or less, counseling disciples on many personal matters, and even negotiating exhaustively with lawyers when your Bombay project was at risk, you never forgot Kṛṣṇa, not for a second.

We always remember how you tirelessly served the order of your Guru Mahārāja, how your mind was always fixed on that directive.

In the autumn of 1977 in Vṛndāvana, you prepared to depart from our mundane vision. You were in a situation that creates utter chaos and confusion in the minds of conditioned souls, but your intellect remained perfectly fixed in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Even at that time you were enthusiastically executing your spiritual master’s order – writing books for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the English-speaking world. Earlier that year, in New York, you told us that you wanted to die “with my boots on, like Arjuna.”

Your unrelenting spiritual energy showed us what was possible. Your prodigious literary output demonstrated how great *ācāryas* in our line, like Ṣrīla Bhaktivinoda, Sanātana and Rūpa Gosvāmīs, and Vyāsadeva, could produce volume after volume of transcendental literature through continuous absorption in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

You explained that if we always chant purely, associate with devotees properly and constantly, and study your books regularly, our minds could be controlled. Above all, you stressed distributing pure, transcendental knowledge and by your actions always showed that preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness was your life’s mission.

You directed us to devote our lives, as you devoted yours, to making the world Kṛṣṇa conscious, but to remain unattached to the results. You said that if only one person became fully Kṛṣṇa conscious, that was also success. In this way you frequently emphasized quality over quantity.

You encouraged us by recounting how in the Sixth Chapter of the *Bhagavad-gītā* even Arjuna complains to Kṛṣṇa how hard the mind is to control. You assured us that one who tries to do so without Kṛṣṇa consciousness will fail.

In Bombay in 1975 you explained why controlling the mind is extremely difficult. But you kindly enlightened us, “The best policy to control the mind is to desire how to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This is the best… this brain taxation… how to convince people about Kṛṣṇa, how to take them to Kṛṣṇa’s desires: *sarva-dharmān parityajya*. In this way, if you go on making plans for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, then your mind is controlled. This is the way.”

I pray for your mercy that I may become increasingly pure during these fleeting moments in this material body. Please give me the strength to control my mind by submerging it always and completely in the sweet ocean of your life’s mission. And kindly give me the benediction to carry out my duty to serve you more and more purely, better and better, more and more, day by day.

*I beg to remain your eternal servant,*  
Mukunda Goswami

## Nirañjana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Each year, at the time of preparing a Vyāsa-pūjā offering for your pleasure, I usually begin by reflecting on all the wonderful things that you’ve done to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. I should be thinking about all the wonderful things that you’re doing, but due to my dull-headedness and lack of surrender, I tend to forget that you’re still present with us. Although my deficient realizations still prevail, this year I would like to make a humble attempt to speak about what you’re now doing, and not just what you’ve done.

Just as all of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s unlimited potencies act perfectly according to His will, so your potencies, manifest as your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, continue to act according to your desire. Granted, we’ve seen manifestations of misused independence in your movement, and therefore, there have been actions not representative of your divine will. But that will always be there by Lord Kṛṣṇa’s arrangement. As the Lord in everyone’s heart, He allows us the freedom to choose, and even in your physical presence we saw many fall pray to māyā’s allurements, although they had once accepted the shelter of your instructions. Of course, we know that, due to your mercy, having tasted the nectar of your lotus feet, they will never forget them. In due course of time, they will be drawn, once again, under the shade of your lotus feet.

But we can also see many who have not fallen away. By remaining in your ISKCON society, and serving your mission, these devotees are increasingly displaying the symptoms of living representatives of your unlimited potencies. They can be recognized by their ability to attract forgetful souls to become dedicated to your mission. Just as in your physical presence you attracted so many thousands of lost souls to the degree that we allow you to act through us, we can continue to attract them all the way back to Godhead.

This is another one of your magical feats. Even though some of us are not completely transparent, the attractive potency is still there. It would seem as though this could not be possible, but somehow we can understand that because you’re still watching over us like a protective parent, you continue to provide all that we’re lacking, until we can become more submissive to you.

Perhaps the most profound manifestation of your active potencies is found in the way your books still ignite the desire for devotional service within the hearts of nondevotees. We have constant, practical experience of how persons who received one or more of your books came into the association of devotees with the desire to learn how to render devotional service to the Lord. From experiences such as these, it’s clear that your lucid purports remain perfect instructions, according to time, place and circumstance, to eradicate the gross misconceptions which dominate the minds of our modern society. You had already convinced them that they must associate with devotees to learn more, and thus you are placing the responsibility on us to show them an ideal example to follow. In this way you’re simultaneously attracting them and pushing us to come up to the standard described in your books.

What to speak of the nondevotees, your devotees as well are deriving their daily sustenance from your books. Without them we could never endure the onslaught of material nature. By relishing your intimate association, our strength increases, and we feel fully nourished to push on with our service.

We also have personal experience of how, just by talking and thinking about you, you become personally present, purifying our hearts by your association. Just as Lord Kṛṣṇa states that He is present wherever His devotees are discussing His intimate pastimes and activities, similarly, we can see how you manifest yourself within such transcendental discussions about you. This Vyāsa-pūjā homage is just one of numerous examples that enable me to understand how this is possible.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as you can see, my realizations are meager. There are countless examples of your presence before us, but I must become more surrendered to have the eyes to see you everywhere. Therefore, on this most auspicious day of your divine appearance in this world, I stand before you and pray that your presence in my life becomes increasingly prominent so that I will never forget you for a moment. I pray that my thoughts become forever filled with your instructions, eternal pastimes, and service. I pray that my words become your words, and my acts become your acts. Although I’m still unsubmissive, I pray that you will please use me as you like so that next year, and each subsequent year for eternity, I will never again forget that it is you who’s attracting everyone to Kṛṣṇa, and I am simply

*Your eternal servant,*  
Nirañjana Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisance at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

It is many years now since you physically departed from the planet, yet you remain ever present with us in so many ways. Indeed, one can definitely say with conviction that you are the life and soul of ISKCON. Since your departure, we have seen various kinds of changes take place within your society, which have affected different devotees in different ways. In fact, there are even persons who claim that you are no longer present within ISKCON. Using this faulty judgment as an excuse, such persons have departed in a variety of different directions, and many of them are now falsely claiming that they are “real ISKCON.” Fortunately, the main body of devotees remains within ISKCON, working cooperatively under the guidance of the Governing Body Commission, as you have instructed us to do. I’m sure the majority of devotees who attended this year’s Māyāpura-Vṛndāvana Festival will attest to the fact that a very inspiring mood is developing within your society – a mood based on serious hearing and chanting about Kṛṣṇa, with a strong desire to unitedly push on with the preaching work.

Of course, there have been many problems over the years, but that is no excuse to break away from your society. Such action only aggravates the problems still further. These problems are merely growing pains, so we should rather remain patient and tolerant and pray to Your Divine Grace to give us strength to unitedly overcome them. Certainly, it is the duty of the serious disciple to point out any deviation he may observe creeping into the movement, but that should be done in a constructive and considerate fashion, making every endeavor to avoid committing *vaiṣṇava-aparādha.* Preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness, you have said, is a declaration of war against māyā, who is expert at retaliating in newer and newer ways. Hence, there have always been problems, and most likely there always will be. You were always vigilant to guard against any deviation, cautioning us that the movement could not be destroyed from without, only from within, yet you dealt with each “problem case” with patience and affection: “hard as a thunderbolt, yet soft as a rose.” Your aim was always to keep everyone working unitedly within ISKCON and thus pressing on effectively with the real work – preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness while simultaneously developing in within us.

Therefore, if we detect some problem we should not simply go on criticizing and criticizing till we totally discourage everyone else and ourselves into the bargain. Rather, we should remain optimistic about the future of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, and try to set a good example ourselves by keeping strict and regulated *sādhana*, seriously studying your books, and enthusiastically endeavoring to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Such exemplary behavior will surely inspire others in spiritual life and create an atmosphere in which Kṛṣṇa consciousness can expand naturally. Furthermore, we should see any difficulty as an opportunity to depend more fully on Kṛṣṇa, just as Queen Kuntī prayed to the Lord:

*vipadaḥ santu tāḥ śaśvat*  
*tatra tatra jagad-guro*  
*bhavato darśanaṁ yat syād*  
*apunar-bhava-darśanam*

**”I wish all those calamities would happen again and again, so that we could see You again and again, for seeing You means that we will no longer see repeated births and deaths.” (*****Bhāg.*** **1.8.25)**

Life in ISKCON means associating at close quarters with so many devotees of different natures. To remain Kṛṣṇa conscious in such a situation means we have to control the mind and senses, subdue the false ego, and be humble. The false ego is always ready to flare up at the slightest instigation and throw us into a confused state of consciousness. Therefore, it has to be strictly disciplined by absorption in devotional service to Kṛṣṇa. So please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, kindly help me to serve your servants within this ISKCON movement by following your example and instructions very carefully and remaining fully engaged in your devotional service at every moment.

I beg to remain always the shelter of your lotus feet,  
*Your unworthy servant,*  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most fallen *daṇḍavats* at the dust of your lotus feet. Please be merciful and grant me the highest benediction I can imagine: to become your unalloyed servant.

I open your books, and you talk to me, giving me confidential instruction and personal inspiration.

Whenever I want you, whenever I need your association, whenever I require your transcendental intelligence – I open your books, and you talk to me.

You answer my questions, dispel my doubts, allay my fears, correct my errors, restore my faith. Over and over, you pick me up and console me and set me straight with your latest advice.

Not long ago, difficulties overcame me, and I faltered before the demands of your service; but, fortunately for me, you were here,\* as ever, and I went to you, and once again you explained everything clearly, and told me in confidence how to be:

”A perfectly cleansed person never quits the devotional service of the Lord,” you said. “Sometimes a representative of the Lord engaged in preaching work meets various so-called difficulties,” you pointed out, and cited as examples the sufferings of Lord Nityānanda, Lord Jesus, and Prahlāda Mahārāja. But you said that in spite of such tribulations none of them gave up their service because “a pure devotee of the Lord is so purified in his heart that he cannot leave the shelter of Lord Kṛṣṇa in any circumstances.”

”There is no self-interest in such service,” you continued. While the *jṇānīs* ultimately give up their speculating, and the *yogīs* their gymnastics, you said, “a devotee of the Lord cannot give up the service of the Lord because” (and here I realized you were making your main point) “he is ordered by his spiritual master. Pure devotees like Nārada and Nityānanda Prabhu take up the order of the spiritual master as the sustenance of life. They do not mind what becomes of the future of their lives.”

The last sentence hit me like a shot. As you said it, I saw you climbing the gangplank of the *Jaladuta* in your seventieth year. You also “did not mind.” That is why you can utter words like these, say them so that they reverberate like a bell in our hearts, say them so that they return to us again and again and compel our attention, our respect, and finally our worship. What knowledge and realization are in that awesome sentence: “They do not mind what becomes of the future of their lives.”

I know that I owe life to your having not minded what would become of the future of your life.

I know that generations upon generations will indeed be as indebted as I am.

And I realize that I owe you the same surrender that you gave; and moreover, I gladly, joyfully want to give it, because I know in this way I will never lose your company.

I do not want to care what becomes of the future of my life, for in this freedom under your order is the eternal refreshment of your association and the association of your dearly beloved Govinda.

Thank you so much for extending to me this relationship with you, which is proven to be unaffected by mere mortality.

Thank you so much for your books, the ever-inviting, always-open meeting place you have arranged for all of us – past, present, and future.

Please keep on instructing, rebuking, and most of all inspiring me to become, more and more,  
*Your servant,*  
Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

## Śivarāma Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisance. All glories to Your Divine Grace, and all glories to your International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness.

Vyāsa-pūjā is a unique opportunity for your followers to glorify you in countless ways. As crystal splits light into different colors, so do you, through your unlimited grace, reveal the multifaceted elements of Kṛṣṇa consciousness that form the eternal activities of *bhakti.*

According to your individual relationship with each devotee, you reveal yourself to them, and they reveal you to the world. The collective essence of such revelation is our vision of who is “Śrīla Prabhupāda.”

Today I would like to relish one of the limbs of devotional service through which you have revealed yourself to me. It is your gift, and you are its personification.

In the *Nectar of Instruction* you state, “The International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness has been established to facilitate these six kinds of loving exchanges between devotees... The life of the Kṛṣṇa Consciousness Society is nourished by these six types of loving exchange among the members.” It is this association of devotees that I would like to eulogize.

*hari haraye namaḥ kṛṣṇa yādavāya namaḥ*  
*yādavāya mādhavāya keśavāya namaḥ*  
*gopāla govinda rāma śrī-madhusūdana*  
*giridhārī gopīnātha madana-mohana*

**”O Lord Hari, O Lord Kṛṣṇa, I offer my obeisance to You, who are known as Yādava, Hari, Mādhava, and Keśava. O Gopāla, Govinda, Rāma, Śrī Madhusūdana, Giridhārī, Gopīnātha, Madana-mohana!”**

The past seems far away, but there was such a time as ”before.” Before I came to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, I, like so many flounders, was struggling in the ocean of material existence, tossed by the waves of happiness and distress. I was told I was a “social anima.” Thus in trying to establish my identity, it was imperative that I exchange with the cohabiters of my environment.

But I couldn’t.

Although I had many acquaintances, I didn’t know anyone. True, we shared many desires and sins, but relationships seemed to last as long as common *anarthas*; it was a business, give and take. I had no real friends. Well, maybe just a few. There was a little green turtle (whose name was Sulli), a budgie, and a crocodile. The turtle disappeared in the backyard, and the budgie died of old age. The crocodile got restless one hot summer afternoon and was last reported hunting in the sewers of Montreal. That left me with Angus – a German Shepherd dog – and my wife. Unfortunately, I had equal difficulty communicating with both. It became apparent that neither humans nor animals would be able to fulfill my real need for loving relationships. Generally, all exchange boiled down to sex, money, and politics. Within, I was hankering for some tangible communication. Externally, I was becoming increasingly bitter.

Somehow I came across the holy name of the Lord, and its constant vibration became my only solace. Although my relationship with the holy name was tangible, I very much hankered for some personified means of Kṛṣṇa consciousness which would assist me in my search for the ultimate reality.

*śrī-caitanya nityānanda śrī-advaita sītā*  
*hari guru vaiṣṇava bhāgavata gītā*

**”All glories to Śrī Caitanya and Nityānanda. All glories to Śrī Advaita Ācārya and His consort, Śrī Sītā Ṭhākurāṇī. All glories to Lord Hari, the spiritual master, the Vaiṣṇavas, the** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **and** ***Śrīmad Bhāgavad-gītā*****.”**

Then in 1972 I began visiting the Montreal temple. It was a converted bowling alley on the third floor, just above Pine’s Pizza. It took about four visits for me to notice the altar, and another few to see the figures who were there. Even when I did see Lord Jagannātha, Lord Balarāma, and Lady Subhadrā, I had difficulty understanding just who they were.

Gokulānanda Prabhu was the first devotee I met (besides the one who took all my money on Park Avenue for some BTG’s). He explained to me the ABC’s of Kṛṣṇa conscious philosophy. He encouraged me to take *prasādam* and would always eat while speaking. He was humble. I had never met anyone who was humble before, and it struck me. Sometimes he even offered obeisance to me, even though I was just a “*karmī*.” Candra told me I was crazy if I could not remember the date. And someone told me I was in māyā for washing my hands with warm water. On the whole I felt drawn to the selfless attitude and spiritual exchanges of devotees.

One Sunday I was told the devotees were going to New York to meet you. I was a fool; I left after the feast. Next Sunday I heard about the visit. Then I became aware of you.

It took some preaching, but soon I found myself singing in *maṅgala-ārati: vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam.* The realization came in quite a dramatic way that May of 1973: “such a spiritual master” was “here,” and I rediscovered you, my eternal guru, by the mercy of the Vaiṣṇavas and Kṛṣṇa. I chanted again and again: *vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam.* “I offer my respectful obeisance unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.”

By your mercy I received the Holy name. By your mercy I came in contact with devotees. Now by your mercy I am reading *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, and a whole new understanding is being revealed to me by your grace.

*śrī-rūpa sanātana bhaṭṭa-raghunāth*  
*śrī-jīva gopāla-bhaṭṭa dāsa-raghunāth*  
*ei chay gosāir kori caraṇa vandan*  
*jāhā hoite bighna-nāś abhīṣṭa-pūraṇ*

**”All glories to Śrī Rūpa Gosvāmī, Sanātana Gosvāmī, Raghunātha Bhaṭṭa Gosvāmī, Śrī Jīva Gosvāmī, Gopāla Bhaṭṭa Gosvāmī and Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī. I offer my obeisance to the feet of these six Gosvāmīs. By my offering them my obeisance, all obstacles to devotion are destroyed and all spiritual desires are fulfilled.”**

We are not a personality cult. Accepting you means to accept the disciplic succession and the conclusion of the disciplic succession.

*evaṁ paramparā-prāptam*  
*imaṁ rājarṣayo viduḥ*  
*sa kāleneha mahatā*  
*yogo naṣṭaḥ parantapa*

**“This supreme science was thus received through the chain of disciplic succession, and the saintly kings understood it in that way. But in course of time the succession was broken, and therefore the science as it is appears to be lost.”**

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda; Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura; Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura; Gaurakiśora dāsa Bābāji; the six Gosvāmīs; Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu... I find that I have become a member of a family lineage that originates with Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself. It is a new life, new friends, new father, and new family, and it is all real, not a product of māyā. What I was seeking, I have found. A real society, not for cultured animals but for humans. And within that society is the means for attaining the ultimate perfection of the human form.

At this point, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I would like to take your permission to describe and glorify some of the devotees who have been instrumental in my spiritual life. Although it is stated that one should not glorify others before the spiritual master, glorification of the Vaiṣṇavas is glorification of you, as they are all your representatives. It is by their association that I am tasting your instructions in devotional service. By remembering them, I am remembering you.

There was Viśvakarma, who reeled me in from the material world. He saved me from material life and also convinced me to leave my wife.

Uttama-śloka talked to me in a very kind and personal way. That was new, since everyone else who talked to me wanted something, while he just wanted to help me.

Śukra was my first obstacle in relationships in the temple. To overcome it, we became friends.

Śrīpati gave me responsibility and depended on me. He inspired me: “ISKCON is in its pioneering days; become a pioneer and open a new frontier in the history of the world.”

Jagadīśa upstaged my established plans. I had just finished cutting a hole in the roof and constructing a greenhouse for Tulasī-devī when he sent me to Winnipeg, with all my belongings in a garbage bag, to become temple president.

In Minneapolis, Satsvarūpa Mahārāja talked to me about you as he sat in my room one rainy afternoon. He was the first person who gave me personal contact with you through *śabda*.

*ei chay gosāi jār – mui tār dās*  
*tā-sabāra pada-reṇu mora pañca-grās*

**”I am the servant of that person who is a servant of these six Gosvāmīs. The dust of their holy feet is my five kinds of foodstuffs.”**

One night Priya-bandhu and I went about the *brahmacārī āśrama* washing devotees’ feet as they slept. Then we drank the water, feeling very clever.

After my initiation ceremony, Ayodhyāpati asked me how I liked the *samosās*. “What’s a *samosā*?” I asked. He explained in detail as he fed me one after another.

You explained in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that one serves the six Gosvāmīs by serving the spiritual master. Similarly, all of those who are serving you are servants of those Gosvāmīs. I therefore relish the nectar of their association. Verily their foot-dust is our life and soul.

Manusuta taught me to distribute books. I was a bit withdrawn; he made me be bold in your service.

Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja taught me what it was to be a puppet. Despite disagreements and separation, he taught me that Vaiṣṇava association transcends history, and he made me taste the *rasa* of relationships.

Then there were Jayatīrtha and Bhagavān, wherever they may be, who, among other things, showed me the pitfalls of spiritual life.

Bhakti-cāru Mahārāja taught me etiquette. Vaiṣṇavas were not “guys” but *prabhus*. Getting things done did not obviate “how” they should be done.

In England I have found another home in the home of ISKCON.

Akhaṇḍadhī Prabhu inspired me to take up the fighting spirit that you had always inculcated in your followers. He challenged me, “The GBC should be leading the campaign to save the Manor.” Through his dedication I found service to Rādhā-Gokulānanda.

Bhāgavatāśraya is my well-wishing critic, always careful for my *sannyāsa.* He took responsibility for Rādhā-Londonīśvara and expanded book distribution, whilst adjusting to the advice of his own well-meaning critics.

Balabhadra insisted that the real relationships were more binding than rules and formalities. He offered his perspective with simple logic while showing how to worship the Lord with devotion.

Keśava Bhārati is my friend, although some think I favor him unnecessarily. Through him I have seen what it means to stick to ISKCON despite personal problems.

There are God brothers and God sisters too numerous to mention here. Some chastise me, others criticize, and a few encourage. Then is the ensuing generation of devotees, as well as disciples, and *in toto* they comprise my family of Vaiṣṇava relations. Whatever the exchange, critical or loving, angry or submissive, it is all sweet. In these last years I have learned that connection with devotees is the essence, in whatever form it may take, for it is you who are chastising, correcting, testing, and giving reward.

*tāṅdera caraṇa sebi bhakta-sane bās*  
*janame janame hoy ei abhilāṣ*

**”This is my desire: birth after birth may I live with those devotees who serve the lotus feet of these six Gosvāmīs.”**

At the Ratha-yātrā of 1987, Kṛpamaya led a long *kīrtana* well into the night. It was one of those *kīrtanas* that made my heart dance. In the midst of countless Vaiṣṇavas we danced in ecstasy, with our arms above our heads, to the sound of the holy name. Young devotees, old devotees – all designations and disagreements dissolved as together we tasted the Holy names with smiles on our faces. No words needed to be exchanged; everything was clear in the brilliant sunlight of the *kīrtana*. This is the essence of all relationships. Dancing and chanting in the ecstasy of the Holy name. When we sang *“Nāma-saṅkīrtana”* my heart echoed incessantly. I could think of nothing else. “Certainly,” I thought, “I will always live in the association of Vaiṣṇavas.”

*ei chay gosāi jabe braje koilā bās*  
*rādhā-kṛṣṇa-nitya-līlā korilā prakāś*

**”When these six Gosvāmīs lived in Vraja, they revealed and explained the eternal pastimes of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have revealed the Absolute Truth, but it is not dry or dogmatic, a set of formulas or rules to follow. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is such a unique experience, and the means of relishing it – namely, glorifying Kṛṣṇa in the association of Vaiṣṇavas – is a lure too irresistible to ignore and too sweet to resist.

I wanted to glorify you, but it came out this way. Śrīla Prabhupāda, all these Vaiṣṇavas are your representatives. Through them I have learnt from you. As they are great, you are the greatest, for you have created them all. As such you are the real Vaiṣṇava whom we all try to emulate, and you are the real friend that we are searching for. In searching you out after your disappearance, I found your devotees, and through them I am learning one way of appreciating you.

*ānande bolo hari bhaja bṛndabān*  
*śrī-guru-vaiṣṇaba-pade majāiyā man*  
*śrī-guru-vaiṣṇaba-pāda-padma kori āś*  
*nāma-saṅkīrtana kohe narottama dās*

**”Absorbing your mind in meditation upon the divine feet of the spiritual master and the holy Vaiṣṇavas, chant the names of Lord Hari in ecstasy, and worship the transcendental realm of Vṛndāvana. Desiring the lotus feet of Śrī Guru and the Vaiṣṇavas, Narottama dāsa sings the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **of the Holy name.”**

Now assembled worldwide, all of these devotees are worshiping you. Collectively they are called the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness. Thank you for this kindness – the worldwide organization of pure devotees. If I have made a little advancement in my fifteen years, I can see it in my love for your ISKCON.

These servants of yours are more dear to me than my life. By serving them, I feel that I am serving you. Whether I am in this world or some other, material or spiritual, is not of great concern. Śrīla Prabhupāda, since you are the most munificent Vaiṣṇava, please offer me your blessings. By your well-wishes, all obstacles in spiritual life may be overcome. I am unqualified by my service; still I humbly place two requests at your lotus feet: First, please never let me leave association of all those devotees whom I have learned to love. By whatever means required, please keep me as a servant in any capacity in your wonderful ISKCON. Second, sometimes in the course of preaching, some things are said or done by me which displease others. In my efforts to please you, please protect me from my wicked mind and false ego so that as I discharge my responsibilities, I may avoid committing offenses at the lotus feet of any devotee.

*tāṅdera caraṇa sebi bhakta-sane bās*  
*janame janame hoy ei abhilāṣ*

**”This is my desire: birth after birth may I live with those devotees who serve the lotus feet of these six Gosvāmīs.”**

I hope this offering meets with your favor. I have many more things to say, but I will end here.

*Your servant,*  
Śivarāma Swami

## Tamāl Krishna Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisance at Your Divine Grace’s lotus feet.

We were in the habit of always writing to Your Divine Grace, but due to your departure we have been deprived of corresponding with you in this type of circumstance for more than ten years. Now for the first time we are again writing, encouraged by the recent close contact we are having with you.

The scriptures inform us that association with the spiritual master is of two types: vapur and vāṇī. Because vāṇī is always available, it is considered the most important. Yet there is a special sweetness to service rendered in the guru’s physical presence. Disciples treasure even a moment’s association with their guru, remembering it throughout their lives, and eagerly listen as those who were fortunate to have had more extensive association describe their experiences. Thinking vapuḥ-sevā was possible only in the form of such memories, we could never have imagined to find ourselves engaged in your personal service again. But that is precisely what has happened.

Suddenly we have been cast in the very same roles in which we served you in the last year of your presence: Tamāl Krishna Goswami, your secretary; Śatadhanya dāsa, your attendant; Gopāl Krishna Goswami, you GBC for Bombay; Ādityā dāsī, your office overseer; and B.V. Nārāyaṇa Mahārāja, ISKCON’s devoted well-wisher – all acting in the same capacity, with the same fixed intensity of service. What caused this remarkable transformation? Your Last Will and Testament. As faithful disciples, we desired to implement your will and other final instructions, yet we now find ourselves checked by a legal blockade engineered by your eldest son from your gṛhastha āśrama. He has challenged both the authenticity of your will and your preeminent spiritual position.

We are at least glad that the confrontation should take place in Bombay, specifically in Hare Krishna Land and the Bombay High Court, for it was at Hare Krishna Land, that place you called battling, when you finally achieved victory, it was here that you said with much satisfaction, “This was the best fight!” Now once again we are being attacked, but this time on a much greater scale. Your former son is claiming all the temples of ISKCON and all of your books as his personal, inherited property!

Due to India’s snarled legal system, it has taken nearly eleven years for this case to be heard. Now that it has begun, we feel the same constant anxiety we experienced in the last year of your presence. From the moment we wake up until we fall exhausted to sleep late at night, you alone are in our minds at every moment. Protection of your Society and Book Trust is our only concern; apprehension haunts us constantly.

No longer do we take darśana of the Deities of Gaura-Nitāi, Rādhā-Rasabihārī, and Sītā-Rāma only to appreciate Their loveliness. Rather, we beseech Them for intelligence and strength to succeed in the daily court battle. Our morning sādhana is abbreviated, our meals are rushed, as every moment is spent preparing for the day’s hearing. Your letters, your signature, your bank accounts – these are no longer museum pieces, but have again become living instruments with which to serve you. Day after day, week after week, the battle is continuing, and there is no immediate end in sight.

We are not the only devotees engaged in such struggle. In other parts of the world, like Los Angeles, London, and Paris, they are also fighting to safeguard your interests, and they must also be experiencing a similar anxiety. But there is a unique feature to our fight: Here in Bombay the debate is solely concerned with Your Divine Grace. We must establish without a doubt your role as the founder-ācārya of ISKCON and the BBT. We must prove that as a *sannyāsī* you exhibited detachment from all material affairs, never claiming anything as your personal property despite the limitless wealth and facility that Lord Kṛṣṇa placed at your disposal. And we have to present the true picture of your final pastimes, in which you exhibited superhuman activities, such as translating the third volume of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam’s* Tenth Canto despite your failing health, never losing consciousness until your departure for Goloka. Your Kṛṣṇa consciousness, your motivations, your activities throughout the years – these are the very substance of this case.

We therefore feel ourselves to be very fortunate. Devotees alone can appreciate that our distress is actually a welcome boon, for it is a great impetus to our developing firm faith in and attachment to your divine service. Therefore we are unable to measure the extent of your mercy in again manifesting yourself before us and allowing us the nectar of such personal association. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Thank you again and again. Your service is our *sādhana*, it is our joy, and the very goal of our existence.

*Eternally at your lotus feet,*  
Tamāl Krishna Goswami

## Toṣaṇa Kṛṣṇa dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I offer my prostrated obeisance at your lotus feet. All glories unto you. I, like a dying, unconscious man in the desert, was given the life-saving spiritual water of your personal mercy.

You did not have to take the long journey from India to New York by freighter ship, all alone, at age seventy, across stormy seas, but you did, and thus I was saved.

You did not have to vigorously and uncompromisingly preach the message of Lord Caitanya amidst all forms of degradation in a world unworthy to hear – but you did, and thus I was saved.

You did not have to translate into the night and painstakingly publish scores of Vaiṣṇava literatures describing the transcendental nature of the Absolute Truth, strictly within the teachings of the predecessor ācāryas – but you did, and thus I was saved.

You did not have to associate with the nasty fools like us living in a fool’s paradise. You had no business here but to give your mercy to fools like me; and indeed you did, and thus I was saved.

I pray that you never let me forget all you have done with no desire for any personal benefit. Never let me forget your utterly causeless nature extending itself beyond the measure of this world – gushing over with the only real love there is.

If as a servant I meditate on your deeds, qualities, and desires, then I will be forever saved by following your footsteps – the ultimate refuge.

*Your very fallen servant,*  
Toṣaṇa Kṛṣṇa dāsa

## Vāsudeva dāsa

**Śrīla Prabhupāda – ISKCON’s Original Touchstone**

Vyāsa-pūjā is a glorious occasion when we have the opportunity to once again churn our memories in appreciation of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mercy. Śrīla Prabhupāda is a “touchstone” and has made many others into “touchstones” like him who can influence others’ lives and set them on the path of devotional service.

Personally, I did not receive much association with His Divine Grace. Nevertheless, whatever contact I did have keeps me sustained in his service. On this occasion I wish to narrate the glorious incident of how I came into contact with Śrīla Prabhupāda.

In 1974, one night at about 10 p.m., someone knocked on my door. I opened the door to see a devotee standing there. I asked him what he wanted. He explained to me that he needed a place to stay and that someone had directed him to my place. My father was a very pious and devotional person, and he welcomed all kinds of *sādhus* and holy men. So naturally people directed such persons to us. We inquired about each other. He told me that his name was Vijaya-dhvaja dāsa. He asked what I did, how long I worked, at what time I returned home, etc. After we had become a little acquainted, I gave him the facility to stay with us.

Every morning after I left for work, he would also go out and then return about half an hour before I would and wait in the lounge. On my return from work at 5 p.m., he would take the opportunity to preach to me. He would seat me nicely under a cool, shady tree and read *Bhagavad-gītā,* explain the importance of the holy name, and instruct me how to chant on the *japa* beads. Sometimes we would also go for a swim in the ocean.

Vijaya-dhvaja was very tactful in dealing with a tough businessmen like me. I still appreciate his exceptional ability to cunningly capture me in his devotional program. I had social friends who would come every day to take me to after-work gatherings, but seeing me with a holy man made them uncomfortable, and without requesting me to accompany them, they would go away. After some time they stopped coming altogether.

Vijaya-dhvaja Prabhu always prepared food, made an offering to Kṛṣṇa, shared it with me and my family, and even distributed it to the many neighbors around the house. In his preaching, he often emphasized that I should do some service. So one day I asked him, “You always say, ‘Do some service; do some service.” But what should I do?” He told me that to preach he needed transport, and he requested me to provide something. So the next day we went into Lautoka and collected a few hundred dollars from each shopkeeper. I gave the money to him to purchase a van. That was my first service, apart from accommodating him.

Right afterward, I had to go to Australia on a business trip. Vijaya-dhvaja Prabhu gave me a *kīrtana* tape of Śrīla Prabhupāda to take with me. I liked hearing this *kīrtana* tape very much. While in Sydney, I would lock up my room from 9 p.m. to about 1 p.m. and loudly play the tape and sing along with Śrīla Prabhupāda. Sometimes the tenant upstairs would stomp on the floor and shout at me to stop it.

Then one day I saw an unusual scene: devotees singing in Sydney. I was very much impressed by their Vaiṣṇava attire – *dhotīs, kūrtas, tilaka* – and the singing of the holy names to the accompaniment of drums and *karatālas*. When I mentioned this to a friend of mine, he told me that they were all drug addicts swaying under the influence of intoxication. I heard these disappointing words but decided to check the devotees out for myself. So I went to the temple and stayed for two nights with them. I personally saw them following all the religious principles – rising early, taking a cold-water bath in freezing winter, and seriously worshiping Kṛṣṇa. I became very much convinced and desired to know who had brought about this wonderful change in them. I repeatedly expressed my desire to the devotees that I wanted to meet this person. I was told that Śrīla Prabhupāda would arrive in Sydney within the next few days. However, my business trip finished before Śrīla Prabhupāda’s arrival, and I had to return to Fiji.

While in Fiji, I heard that Śrīla Prabhupāda was in Sydney and that he was to go to Hawaii afterwards. Then I found out that flights from Sydney to Hawaii stopped over in Fiji for some time. Having arranged for permission from the Airport Authority, I went to meet Śrīla Prabhupāda in the transit lounge at Nandi Airport. There were three of us – myself, my wife (now Bhāgavatī-devī dāsī) and Vijaya-dhvaja Prabhu. We took garlands, and also *prasādam* prepared by Vijaya-dhvaja Prabhu, which consisted of *sabjī, purīs,* tomato chutney, and sweets. Śrīla Prabhupāda was the last passenger to disembark and enter the transit lounge. I was most anxious to meet him. First I eagerly looked at him for some time, and then I offered my *daṇḍavats* from a distance. After comfortably seating Śrīla Prabhupāda, we offered him garlands and the *prasādam*. Speaking in Hindi, Śrīla Prabhupāda immediately asked, “Is there any Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa *mandira* here?”

I replied, “No.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda ordered, “Make a *sundar baḍia* Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa *mandira.”*

I immediately agreed and said, “Yes, I will make a beautiful *mandira.”*

Śrīla Prabhupāda added, “Chant sixteen rounds of the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra every day on *japa-mālā* and make a *mandira.”*

After this we had some light conversation. I requested Śrīla Prabhupāda to come to Fiji again, and he agreed, saying, “There will be a temple, so I will certainly come.”

When the boarding call came, I again offered full *daṇḍavats* to Śrīla Prabhupāda and touched his feet. Śrīla Prabhupāda did not say anything but bent down and touched my head. I felt his mercy and an unusual feeling pass through my body. I felt light, as if lifetime after lifetime of my sins had been washed away in an instant.

This is how I came in contact with “ISKCON’s original touchstone,” Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*I remain your servant,*  
Vāsudeva dāsa

## Śrī Vyāsa-Pūjā homages from Sannyāsīs

## Ānanda Svarūpa Swami

*kṛṣṇaika-cetta mada-moha-vināśa-kārin*  
*mad-dṛṣṭi-gocara prabho prabhupāda-svāmin*  
*doṣābhivṛtti-paridūśita-manda-buddheḥ*  
*sañcintayāmi caraṇau tava bhakti-hetoḥ*

**”My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, your mind is always fixed on Kṛṣṇa. My dear Prabhupāda, kindly shower me with your mercy. My intelligence is full of faults and my mind is crazy. Therefore, my dear Prabhupāda, I surrender completely unto you.”**

*namas te namas te kṛpā-pūrṇa-dṛṣṭi*  
*namas te namas te mahānanda-dhātrī*  
*namāmo namāmaḥ punar rakṣa rakja*  
*vidhehi dayāṁ pāhibo dakṣa dakṣa*

**”O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are a shower of mercy, by which you deliver great transcendental happiness. I offer my repeated obeisance unto you. By your expert kindness, please protect me again and again.”**

*akṣṇoḥ phalaṁ tvādṛśa-darśanaṁ hi*  
*tanoḥ phalaṁ tvādṛśa-gātra-saṅghaḥ*  
*jihvā-phalaṁ tvādṛśa-kīrtanaṁ hi*  
*sudurlabhā bhāgavatā hi loke*

**”O Śrīla Prabhupāda, the purpose of my eyes is to see you, of my body to touch you, and of my tongue to celebrate you, for in this world, where is a pure personality such as you to be found?”**

O my Guru Mahārāja, it is the custom to offer thanks to you on this day. In my case, however, even that ability is utterly dependent on you, who are the maintainer of my tenuous position in spiritual life.

It has been some years since I have been able to write a public offering on your Vyāsa-pūjā day. Our long separation has helped me realize your eternal presence within my heart, for, like Śukadeva Gosvāmī, you can enter the hearts of all. Therefore, I find it unnecessary to justify my ineptitude.

I am unable to serve you, to be worthy of you, or to be a deserving disciple, for I am a tested and reliable representative of the demoniac class.

Still, O Mahāśaya, O Ṭhākura, that very inability and unworthiness may be my only qualification, and my demoniac birth my sole inspiration, for did not Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura write (for the benefit of one such as me):

*gopināth, āmi ki doṣe doṣī*  
*asura sakala, pāilo caraṇa,*  
*vinoda thākilo bosi’*

**”O Gopīnātha! Just see my wretched situation! I am so unfortunate, such a mine of faults, so fallen, forlorn, and forsaken, that even though so many, many demons have attained Your lotus feet, I am still languishing here!”**

This (the Vyāsa-pūjā book) is a dangerous forum to express inner premonitions. Not the least because I’m still unsure of the constancy of what is in my heart. Nevertheless, I am compelled to speak, to bare the insidiously ominous, too-persistent-for-comfort feeling, that things are not as they should be.

I hear some GBC’s congratulating themselves, anticipating the golden era for our movement just around the corner. And it sounds so right, up there in their rarefied atmosphere. Yet when I tour India I see much disquieting me. And why just India? Elsewhere is no less uninspiring.

Of course, I know something of the feel of India’s pulse, having spent so many years here, one of your rare white elephants. Others, although saintly, remain atypically silent; and around the world the God brothers slip and slide, carp and curse. Are they all irrevocably in *māyā?*

What’s worrying me is not definite, concrete, or cogently explainable; therefore I am appealing to you to come into my heart and reveal the nature of my inquietude or make me free from doubt.

ISKCON convention has rarely used Vyāsa-pūjā as an opportunity to polemicize; still, I take a literal leaf from your book by remembering the Vaiśiṣṭakas that you offered at the feet of your Guru Mahārāja in 1961. In those writings I read your eloquent, fearless prayer to him to resolve the problems facing the institution he created for the well-being of all living entities.

Twenty-eight years later your words leap from the page, outraged; they mock our simpering, stereotyped ideas of Vyāsa-pūjā; they incite, browbeat, plead, and propel to action!

*”Agun jvalbe.”* There will be fire!

Today your house of hope is vulnerable, and, as I am living in it (albeit somewhat uncomfortably), it would be a tragedy if it ever fell.

Pessimistic? No. Pragmatic? Definitely.

In 1961, in the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple, under the protection of Rūpa Gosvāmī, Jīva Gosvāmī, and Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja, you cried in anguish, unable to bear the separation from your *guru*.

And if indeed a catastrophe is about to be unleashed upon our not-always-so-suspecting heads, please do not let me be a silent bystander.

O Prabhupāda, your lotus feet are more resplendent than the sun, the moon and the stars, for has the sun not shed its life-giving brilliance and the moon and the stars their soothing light upon my head for countless births without penetrating the deep darkness of my heart?

O my lord, my master, forgive my interminable offenses, which swell like a limitless ocean without shore. Please engage me in your service so that my intelligence may remain constantly refreshed. I appeal to your immeasurable compassion, for are you not the directly designated emissary of Lord Caitanya?

O ocean of consideration, charity, indulgence, and courtesy! O reservoir of generosity, kindness, and elegant grace! All glory unto you! All glory to Your Divine Grace!

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Ānanda-svarūpa Swami

## Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisance at your lotus feet.

From any angle one may consider you, you are a giant, because your appearance in this world has brought about gigantic benefits.

Your preaching work has no equal in quality and quantity. Your Bhaktivedanta purports enlighten and awaken uninterruptedly the consciousness of millions of beings. The distribution of your books, reaching the 200 million mark, expands unrestrainedly throughout the world.

The world is in need of peace, and you give the ideal formula. The world is in need of freedom, and you deliver the necessary knowledge. The world is in need of practical solutions, and you furnish them by the thousands. The world is in need of hope, and you guarantee it by means of the *paramparā* system. The world is in need of happiness, and you dispense the *mahā-mantra*. The world is in need of faith, and you present your example. The world is in need of help, and you provide your movement.

You are the virtuous saint, the protecting angel, the irreplaceable friend and kind benefactor who indiscriminatingly helps all who suffer. You are the magic doctor and the father in whom everyone seeks to place their trust. You are the transcendental guru who can save all.

Bhakti Bikash Swami  
Prabhupāda-caraṇa-reṇu

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the tide is turning. Your (and our) ISKCON is again becoming more and more ecstatic every day. The most amazing news is that the apparently impenetrable bastions of the Communist powers have accepted your order. That which we dreamt of happening in some distant future is with us now: Kṛṣṇa consciousness is being practiced openly in countries which only three years ago persecuted devotees. This is proof that the inconceivable potency by which Kṛṣṇa spreads the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is still very much alive in ISKCON.

In the West, the preaching is continuing, and devotees are simultaneously looking inwards toward their cultural roots. Many are coming to Vṛndāvana for intensive month-long programs of studying your books. This year’s Māyāpura Festival also marked a continued return to sanity, based on *śravaṇaṁ kīrtanaṁ viṣṇoḥ smaraṇaṁ pāda-sevanam.* All this is in pursuance of your order: “I am building these temples in Māyāpura and Vṛndāvana so that my Western disciples can come to India to learn about Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” An annual Vraja-maṇḍala Parikrama has been started by your disciples, and a Gaura-maṇḍala Parikrama will begin next year.

All in all, life in ISKCON has steadied out. Problems are still there and always will be, but the worldwide atmosphere is becoming favorable. The next requirement is a big push on book distribution and preaching, coupled with a commitment to dissolve the *anarthas* which have entered your society. Then if we can just stay on the right track this time, we’ll all be set up for dancing our way back to Godhead. It really is all your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

The history of ISKCON since your disappearance from our vision less than twelve years ago is a demonstration of the stanza you quoted too often:

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ‘pi*

As you demonstrated in your life, even in one’s darkest hours, when all seems impossible, if a devotee simply remains fixed on the principle of satisfying the spiritual master, all success is assured. On the other hand, despite having a big title, many properties, and thousands of followers, if one cannot satisfy the spiritual master, one cannot make any progress in spiritual life.

The dictum is so clear as to be frightening. The seductive witches of material desire, the attractively dressed *viṣa-kanyās* of prestige, position and glory, are whispering us away from you to a lonely spot where they can kill us with their kisses. Śrīla Prabhupāda, save us! Fix us in the dust of your lotus feet by the strong attachment of selfless service. Somehow or other, in all that we are doing, in our *sādhana*, in our preaching, in our struggles, if we can just attain the position of an atom in the dust of your lotus feet, that will be our success, that will be our reward. We have nothing else to aspire to.

Your soft lotus feet are traveling from universe to universe, picking up millions of conditioned souls, who cling to your lotus feet as their only salvation. The members of ISKCON are all dust particles of your lotus feet and are therefore worshipable by me. Please give me an eternal place among them.

Now, by the mercy of your disciples, you have given me the order of *sannyāsa*. Please bless me to make good use of it in your service and not to become abominable by using it for comfort and false prestige.

Always desiring to bathe in the dust of your lotus feet, Praying for the mercy to have your feet placed on my head life after life (damn my puffed-up eloquence – give me one drop of sincerity),

*Your aspiring servant,*

Bhakti Vikash Swami  
(still your Ilāpati dāsa)

## Bhakti Rāghava Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisance at your divine lotus feet. I was born in the darkness of ignorance, and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with the torchlight of knowledge, have kindly come to deliver this fallen conditioned soul from this deep, dark well of *māyā’s* illusory energy. May I remain ever grateful and indebted unto you for having bestowed your causeless mercy upon me.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the perfect master, the perfect guide. Your books, your lectures, your letters, your conversations, are all saturated with such sublime instructions. Simply I have to learn these very nicely and endeavor to make them available to others. By coming in contact with you, so many conditioned souls are being awakened to their real, constitutional position as eternal servants of the Lord. Such is the power of the pure devotee. Because of your firm faith in the orders of your Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, and due to your strong determination to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the English-speaking world, you have gained the favor of our Vaiṣṇava *guru-paramparā ācāryas* and have been showered with the full mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and the Pañca-tattva*: kali-kālera dharma – kṛṣṇa-nā*ma-*saṅkīrtana! kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana:* “The fundamental religious system in the age of Kali is the chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Unless empowered by Kṛṣṇa one cannot propagate the *saṅkīrtana* movement.” (*C.c. Antya-līlā* 7.11)

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have no desire for personal gain or fame, but I do nurture a strong desire to see you more and more glorified. As Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura himself so virtuously chants in his Śaraṇāgati, *kṛṣṇa se tomāra, kṛṣṇa dite paro, tomāra śakati āche:* “Kṛṣṇa is yours. You are able to give Him to me, for such is your power.” You have so wonderfully inherited this power to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to so many people. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am but a fool, but if you kindly bestow but one drop of your mercy, I may engage in your service relentlessly.

*I beg to remain you humble servant,*  
Bhakti Rāghava Swami

## Bir Krishna dāsa Goswami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisance at your lotus feet.

I am begging that you will always allow me to remember that the only access that I have to the Supreme Personality of Godhead and pure spiritual knowledge is you. *Śāstra-cakṣus,* for me, means to see through your words, to accept your words as the standard by which all other evidence is judged. If I think that I can approach the Vedas, the Vaiṣṇava histories, or other types of evidence and in this way achieve an understanding of Kṛṣṇa consciousness apart from your vāṇī, I will surely be bewildered in my attempt. You state in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* “In his book *Sarva-saṁvādinī,* Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī has noted that although there are ten kinds of evidence – direct perception, the Vedic version, historical reference, hypothesis, and so on – and although they are all generally accepted as evidence, the person presenting a hypothesis, reading the Vedic version, perceiving, or interpreting by his experience is certain to be imperfect in four ways. That is, he is subject to commit mistakes, to become illusioned, to cheat, and to have imperfect senses. Although the evidence may be correct, the person himself is in danger of being misled due to his material defects.”

You have stated that “everything we need is in your books,” Śrīla Prabhupāda. This is not an exaggeration. It is a fact. All other evidence, be it *śruti* or otherwise, should be seen only as supplementary to your words.

You have likened the disciple’s relationship with the guru to the relationship of a chaste wife with the husband. In other words, the wife knows only the husband, and her life is one hundred percent dedicated to the husband.

To apply this principle to my relationship with you, I accept something as absolute fact only if I have it from you (your books, tapes, lectures, letters, etc.), and I judge everything else by this standard.

I check all aspects of my *sādhana* with your words, as there is danger in adding or subtracting from your prescriptions. You state this very cogently in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta:* “A Vaiṣṇava is immediately purified, provided he follows the rules and regulations of his bona fide spiritual master. It is not necessary that the rules and regulations followed in India be exactly the same as those in Europe, America, and other Western countries. Simply imitating without effect is called *niyamāgraha*. Not following the regulative principles but instead living extravagantly is also called *niyamāgraha*. The word *niyama* means ‘regulative principles,’ and *āgraha* means ‘eagerness.’ The word agraha means ‘not to accept.’ We should not follow regulative principles without an effect, nor should we fail to accept the regulative principles. What is required is a special technique according to country, time, and candidate. Without the sanction of the spiritual master, we should not try to imitate. This principle is recommended here: *śuṣ*ka-*vairāgya-jñāna saba niṣedhila*. This is Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s liberal demonstration of the bhakti cult. We should not introduce anything whimsically, without the sanction of the bona fide spiritual master.”

In this way, I am sure that I will be protected by you from being cut by the “razor’s edge” that you have warned us about. Please allow me to always stick to this “straight and narrow” path that leads “back to home, back to Godhead.”

*Your servant,*  
Bir Krishna dāsa Goswami

## Dhanurdhara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisance. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

You wanted to accomplish so many seemingly impossible tasks. They weren’t impossible though, because the devotion of one on the platform of *prema-bhakti* can control even the Supreme Lord. Thus, the Lord is bound to fulfill the devotee’s desires and words.

We can therefore understand that as the history of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement unfolds, your desires will be fulfilled.

For example, this year we saw your desire for a strong ISKCON presence at the Kumbha-melā fructify into an ISKCON camp worthy of Prabhupāda, the master at whose feet all other masters sit. Our short association with the Soviet devotees proved to us your statement that the communists will make the best devotees. You wanted an American house in Vṛndāvana, a Vaiṣṇava institute where your followers could come and be trained as preachers. It is happening, and, in fact, whatever instructions you have given will certainly be fulfilled in time by the sincere souls, who, giving up sense gratification, make one of your orders their life and soul.

In Vṛndāvana you wanted an ideal Gosvāmī institute to train young boys as perfect *brāhmaṇas,* an institution you said would save human civilization. You told us to fill it with five hundred students, and you wanted children from high-class Indian families. You told us it would be difficult, but it would be successful. Śrīla Prabhupāda, this task can be done, because Lord Kṛṣṇa is bound by your words. Please inspire me to give up all desires for sense gratification, so that before I die I can be one of those devotees who acted as an instrument to fulfill one of your prophecies.

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Dhanurdhara Swami

## Dhanvantari Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this day of your appearance, I am praying to Kṛṣṇa to allow me to become your servant. I am an insignificant person who was born in this material world among the lowest of mankind. But as it is stated in the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,

*kirāta-hūṇāndhra-pulinda-pulkaśā*  
*ābhīra-śumbhā yavanāḥ khasādayaḥ*  
*ye ‘nye ca pāpā yad-apāśrayāśrayāḥ*  
*śudhyanti tasmai prabhaviṣṇave namaḥ*

**”Kirāta, Hūṇa, Āndhra, Pulinda, Pulkaśa, Ābhīra, Śumbha, Yavana, members of the Khasa races, and even others addicted to sinful acts can be purified by taking shelter of the devotees of the Lord, due to His being the supreme power. I beg to offer my respectful obeisance unto Him.”**

So I am hopeful that you will give me the shelter of your lotus feet, and in this way I will be able to become your one-hundred-percent servant.

Dhanvantari Swami

## Dyutidhara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful obeisance in the dust of your lotus feet. You have blessed this world with your divine appearance and are carrying the unfathomable mercy of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. As the Lord’s unalloyed representative, you are traveling in all the towns and villages, reclaiming those forlorn wretches who have all been paralyzed by *māyā’s* poison. Upon obtaining your *darśana,* we are again coming to the living platform. Gradually the intoxication of self-importance and universal arrogance are subsiding. You have come with the glorious banner of *hari-nāma saṅkīrtana,* and by this unprecedented inundation of *kṛṣṇa-prema,* our wooden like bodies have begun dancing and chanting in ecstasy.

This is not a sentimental occurrence but rather the result of your most exhaustive and authoritative presentation of *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. On the strength of these and other literatures, you have substantiated the Vedic culture, thus creating a renaissance of living spirituality. This flowering of transcendental culture is evident in hundreds of your ISKCON communities, wherein the Deities of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa are opulently worshiped with *prasādam, ārati,* and *kīrtana,* and where there exist the evolving models of *gurukula* and *varṇāśrama-dharma.* That millions of persons have been influenced by your literatures attests to the fact that you are a *śaktyāveśa avatāra* and that this preaching work is a manifestation of your devotional ecstasy.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are fully possessed of the quality of mercy, and your every aspiration is to benedict all the fallen souls with the taste for the nectar for which we are always anxious. Of the relationship between yourself and your disciples, you have said that “we are packed up together in Kṛṣṇa consciousness” and that when the disciple is always thinking of the *guru,* the *guru* is always thinking of the disciple. Please afford us the intimacy of rendering service to you in the crores of births that remain in this material world.

*Your fallen servant,*  
Dyutidhara Swami

## Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my fallen obeisance at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

You have taught us by precept and by experience that a disciple can realize the Lord’s association through the transparent via medium of the bona fide spiritual master. You have even personally stated, “I do not know Kṛṣṇa, but I only know my *guru-mahārāja.”* This humble expression is clearly meant for our instruction. Lord Kṛṣṇa is very distant from us, but we can glimpse His supreme personality by understanding your divine character. *Sarvair guṇais tatra samāsate surāḥ*: the Lord’s qualities are mirrored in the person of His pure devotee. How can this not be so? For Kṛṣṇa is dwelling in your heart: *svāntaḥ-sthena gadā-bhṛtā.*

Just as Kṛṣṇa manifests manifold divine forms to reciprocate the diverse devotional ecstasies of His *bhaktas* (*rūpa-bhedam avāpnute*), so you also demonstrate various aspects of your personality to reciprocate your disciples’ affectionate services. And just as we can appreciate the Lord’s presence in the glorification and remembrance of His transcendental *līlās* (*yatra gāyanti mad-bhaktāḥ*), so we can also soothe our separation from you by the memory of your transcendental qualities and pastimes.

**Śrī Daśāvatāra-śaktyāveśa-guṇa-stotra**

(1)

As Lord Brahmā was fast asleep  
during partial annihilation,  
Gauḍīya Matha lost in court battles deep  
– spiritual devastation.  
Like Hayagrīva, poisoned interpreters  
stole the Gītā’s knowledge away.  
But you, like Lord Matsya,  
”As It Is” – saved the day.

O Prabhupāda! O lord of the Vaiṣṇavas! All glories to you!

(2)

The devas and the asuras  
for nectar did they churn,  
Vāsukī’s head or Vāsukī’s tail –  
which one did they earn?  
Like Lord Kūrma, you could engage  
the pious and sinner each,  
To “scratch the itch upon your back”  
– by book distribution to preach.

O Prabhupāda! O lord of the Vaiṣṇavas! All glories to you!

(3)

Due to Hiraṇyākṣa’s  
exploitative motions,  
Mother earth had fallen  
into the Garbha Ocean  
And, like Lord Boar, had descended  
into the nether regions.  
Prabhupāda swooped upon the West  
steeped in abominations.

O Prabhupāda! O lord of the Vaiṣṇavas! All glories to you!

(4)

Just as Hiraṇyakaśipu  
submitted all to his rule,  
Impersonalism’s trapped the world –  
– Māyā’s last tool.  
Nīrviśeṣa śūnyavādī,  
Prabhupāda firmly crushed,  
His pointed words, like Nṛsiṁha’s nails,  
False logic hushed.

O Prabhupāda! O lord of the Vaiṣṇavas! All glories to you!

(5)

What cunning craft did Vāmana show  
to purify His devotee?  
So you have also caught us all  
by transcendental trickery.  
And just as the Ganges from His toe  
sanctifies the world,  
So all are cleansed of lust and greed  
– Bhaktivedanta purports unfurled.

O Prabhupāda! O lord of the Vaiṣṇavas! All glories to you!

(6)

A teacher or a cheater?  
– you’ve rightly discriminated,  
Leaders who themselves are fallen  
– severely interrogated.  
But while Paraśurāma only killed them 21 times  
with his keenly sharpened chopper,  
Your BBT books will act for 10,000 years  
– Kali-yuga’s hypocrisy-stopper.

O Prabhupāda! O lord of the Vaiṣṇavas! All glories to you!

(7)

Rāma-rājya! Rāma-rājya!  
– sentimental outcry.  
Whole world’s waiting for Lord Rāma’s rule;  
His absence is their alibi.  
But every faith and every race  
live happy in your house.  
Unless we lead by your example  
– again become a mouse!

O Prabhupāda! O lord of the Vaiṣṇavas! All glories to you!

(8)

Lord Balarāma, the Original guru,  
Kṛṣṇa’s servant did He act.  
So Prabhupāda as His representative  
served his disciples in fact.  
He said it was his duty  
in us some fault to find,  
As Yamunā surrendered to His plow,  
chastisement purified our minds.

O Prabhupāda! O lord of the Vaiṣṇavas! All glories to you!

(9)

Only Prabhupāda could hold a flower  
for two hours without jading,  
Or tell his disciple to preach to an ant  
because the soul is all-pervading.  
As Lord Buddha is hailed as  
the Compassionate One,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda will be known as  
the Merciful Sun.

O Prabhupāda! O lord of the Vaiṣṇavas! All glories to you!

(10)

As Kalki, the Supreme Chastiser,  
kills the wicked barbarian men,  
Prabhupāda destroyed our evil propensities  
and turned us into brāhmaṇas.  
With hearing, prasādam, and ārcana,  
and chanting the holy name  
He’s taken the dull, the jealous, and the madman  
and created someone sane.

O Prabhupāda! O lord of the Vaiṣṇavas! All glories to you!

As Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead Himself, exhibits a unique, unrivaled sweetness, which gives Him the nomenclature “all-attractive,” so we found you to be simply the most attractive person within this world. We pray that you will increasingly draw us nearer to your association in devotional service so that we may give up the service of the Lord’s material energy.

*Striving to be your eternal servant,*  
Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

## Guṇagrāhī dāsa Goswami

**”Your love for Me...”\***

Your Gurudeva arranged to exit early.  
You’ve said that his disciples were to blame.  
Then they later failed him in his mission:  
The GBC he’d asked for never came.

Coming West, you pleaded for assistance.  
Excuses came, though you were on the brink  
Of fulfilling Lord Caitanya’s prediction.  
Cooperation – again the missing link.

Then our turn came to pass the test of māyā.  
You built a house for us, and by your grace,  
As one we worked worldwide to serve your order,  
Your shelter keeping all things in their place.

And when you left, you said, “At least maintain...”  
”Of course,” we thought. “That’s the least we’ll do.”  
Puffed up with borrowed plumes, we then forgot you.  
And the house you built caught fire through and through.

And now that envy’s fire has subsided,  
The shelter of your vāṇī felt once more,  
We pray to not forget our past and others’,  
And fail to work together as before.

O Prabhupāda! Your love for us is boundless.  
You’ve given so much more than we deserve.  
And all you ask from us is to return it  
By loving one another as we serve.

\*”Your love for me will be shown by how much you cooperate to keep this institution together after I am gone.” (Śrīla Prabhupāda)

*Your servant,*  
Guṇagrāhī dāsa Goswami

## Indradyumna Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
*Today on this most auspicious occasion*  
*Of your appear*ance in this world,  
I am intensely feeling separation from you,  
Remembering you pastimes amongst us.

Just twenty years ago today  
You appeared within my life;  
An uninvited but most welcome guest,  
Who remains my eternal guide.

You gave me your mercy,  
I gave you my life,  
With little perception of the gifts you’d given,  
In the infancy of my spiritual life.

In the joy of my fortune, years passed as moments  
In service to your mission,  
When suddenly that fateful day,  
You passed away from my vision.

Quickly discovering you once again,  
In the shelter of your instructions,  
I found a solace that has now matured  
Into genuine inspiration.

O Prabhupāda! I miss you now, on days like this,  
Though I know you’re forever present;  
Those first special moments spent with you  
Remain an eternal treasure.

I miss the touch of your lotus hand,  
Like that one morning in Paris,  
When seeing my eagerness to touch your feet,  
You stopped and rubbed my head.

And oh! My good fortune, when your glance fell upon me  
That day I peeked into your room!  
And you motioned me forward, “All right, come on!”  
And gave me your own prasādam!

And where would I be, had you not slapped my back  
One evening at the temple in London,  
And told me life will be easier now  
That you’re taking me back to the spiritual kingdom?

O Prabhupāda! Precious moments with you  
Remain fresh within my vision,  
And give me the strength and ability  
To bear all trials and tribulations.

And I feel your presence, even today,  
As you counsel and advise;  
Sometimes praising, and even chastising,  
My foolish attempts to please you.

I hear you through your books,  
Full of perfect knowledge,  
That always seem to come to life  
When I apply them in your service.

I hear you through my God brothers, too,  
Who correct me when I’m wrong.  
Like Śyāmaāl Prabhu, who takes the time  
To remind me of your instructions.

The flame of your enthusiasm  
Still burns within our hearts.  
Just see my friend Praghoṣa,  
And how the books are going out!

And in kīrtana with Ayodhyāpati,  
I feel the presence of the holy name,  
O Prabhupāda! It’s just as if  
We were with you once again!

I see you too in Bombay,  
When Girirāja Swami speaks,  
And I see your sharp intelligence  
In the words Ātma-tattva repeats.

Your instructions are as precious to me  
As my very life.  
Indeed they are, and will always be,  
My eternal guiding light.

By your grace you guide my worship  
To my Lordships every day;  
Through your mercy They accept my service  
In Their pleasant smiling way!

Lakṣmī-Nṛsiṁha, Govardhana-śilā,  
And Nitāi-Nimāi-sundara too,  
My constant companions throughout this life  
And, I pray, forever more.

Now each year my service grows,  
For I’ve inherited your mission,  
And like a grateful son I try to help others  
By nurturing the seeds you’ve given.

Alas, this year even saw the pauper  
Clothed in royal dress!  
As disciples approached, and I accepted,  
It’s what you wanted, you said.

But you know my heart; what can I do  
But simply connect them to you?  
On your order I take up the burden of love;  
I’ve no qualifications, it’s true.

Whatever you ask I’ll try to do,  
But how can I repay my debt?  
For you have saved me from a hellish world  
That only led to death.

But now I have life, so let me preach  
Until my final breath!  
And then send me, please,  
To be with you, or where you feel it’s best.

To preach the glories of the holy name  
One more time around again,  
Or birth after birth, as you see fit,  
Until I’m home with you again.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Indradyumna Swami

## Īśvara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisance at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace and to your transcendental ISKCON. It is well known that we should address the spiritual master just to glorify him. Personally, I want to glorify you twenty-four hours a day because there is no limit to your divine glories.

Although I am here in this material world, suffering due to heavy misidentification with my false ego, still I rejoice when I hear you being praised. I enjoy every second and every word of it. One of the innumerable wonderful things you did was to lovingly teach us, sometimes almost by force, how to worship you. Worship of pure devotees is the highest worship because such great souls come to this world out of their mercy to instruct us. No one wants to accept a subordinate position in this world, but as soon as one submissively serves a spiritual master, he quickly advances in spiritual life. By serving you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are beginning to understand what spiritual life really is.

My heart is as hard as a stone, and I am suffering due to my heavy attraction to materialism and dry mental speculation. In this condition, how can I ever love anyone? Still, even for a hard-hearted person like me, there is something very special in you that gives me the highest pleasure when I serve you. I hope that someday my heart will become so soft that it will completely melt when hearing your glories. Until then, I will continue trying for that perfection, as I have been for the last several years.

One thing I would like to reveal to you is that even the first time I heard of you, although I was still very young, I felt a special kind of pleasure just understanding that I had to dedicate my life entirely to you. Almost twenty years have passed since then, and I still relish the same feeling of dedication. I can definitely say that I have never felt that I have been cheated. Although in the beginning I was too young to understand anything, you were so special to me that I knew I had to surrender.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, without your mercy and guidance I am nothing and am really afraid of going astray. Please do not kick me out, and know for certain that I am ready to be corrected at any time. You can do that as you’ve always done – by the direct, potent, and intense system you presented through your divine instructions, your divine books, and the divine association of your disciples.
