Like so many other disciples and followers of yours, I am trying to dedicate the most important years of my life to serving you. It is actually a sacrifice. Many have stepped back, preferring to wait and prepare themselves better for the future. As for myself, I cannot afford that much training and preparation. I feel this is a kind of urgent situation now, and I am forced to surrender because there is so much to do. I know that many of those who are just preparing themselves now will be wonderful preachers and renunciets in the future. But my realization is that something has to be done now, a sort of sacrifice for this urgent situation. But it is really nectar. I enjoy being used like that, and I hope I always will.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give me strength. Without you presence, spiritual life will be just like the gaping jaws of a monster. Guide me so that I will act only for your pleasure, and correct me if I deviate from the path. Please allow me to always serve you and all of your disciples and followers.

*I beg to remain your fallen servant,*  
Īśvara Swami

## Jaya Dhvaja Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisance unto your lotus feet.

Your glories on this auspicious day  
We glorify by surrender, and obey  
Your divine instructions,  
Your Divine Grace.

Loitering on the street, you came to the West,  
Taking shelter of the Holy Name.  
Kṛṣṇa is with you,  
Your Divine Grace.

Empowered by your guru,  
You came to the West,  
Fulfilling Mahāprabhu’s desires –  
Your Divine Grace.

To rescue the conditioned souls in the West,  
Kṛṣṇa sent you many candidates to help,  
To expand your mission.  
Your Divine Grace.

By your preaching, your authority stood;  
Māyāvādīs, yogīs, karmīs fled away,  
With no room for pretension and cheating –  
Your Divine Grace.

Ācārya means “heavy for Kṛṣṇa’s sake.”  
You engage everyone in Kṛṣṇa’s service  
By your purity and strength.  
Those who are not serious cannot stay  
In your ISKCON mission,  
Your Divine Grace.

In your plans and structure we have to engage  
Without concocting something new, and follow as it is,  
Spreading Kṛṣṇa’s glories in every town and village –  
Your Divine Grace.

“Distribute my books; it is my request.”  
This is most important to defeat the illusion of men.  
”I am this body; this is mine,” always they claim,  
And simply they struggle till the time of death.  
Your books are the only solution –  
Your Divine Grace.

Pride, anger, and lust are why we don’t obey  
Your guidance and advice and go astray.  
The waves of confusion carry us away;  
Then Māyā enchants us to take the old ways.  
Please keep us in the association of devotees,  
Your Divine Grace.

In the devotees’ association we can serve Kṛṣṇa and you  
And, by fitting into your plan, not fail into illusion.  
The weeds of Māyā you eradicate,  
From the sincere and meek who are ready to serve –  
Your Divine Grace.

Chanting sixteen rounds, you have recommended;  
This is our shelter, which will let us stay  
In your divine association.  
Maṅgala ārati, Guru-pūjā, and Bhāgavatam class  
We shall attend every day;  
That will show real love for you, keeping your mercy –  
Your Divine Grace.

On this day I’m praying that you keep me engaged  
Without letting me fall into Māyā’s way.  
Let me remain as your eternal servant,  
Your Divine Grace.

*Your fallen disciple,*  
Jaya Dhvaja Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

To offer obeisance to my spiritual master, I must first fall flat at his lotus feet, prostrating my body before him, forgetting whatever material thoughts are in my mind, dedicating my body, mind, and words to be used however he may be pleased to use them.

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.7.6) we find:

*anarthopaśamaṁ sākṣād*  
*bhakti-yogam adhokṣaje*  
*lokasyājānato vidvān*  
*cakre sātvata-saṁhitām*

**”The material miseries of the living entity, which are superfluous to him, can be directly mitigated by the linking process of devotional service. But the mass of people do not know this, and therefore the learned Vyāsadeva compiled this Vedic literature, which is in relation with the Supreme Truth.”**

The term anartha is mild. “That which is superfluous, meaningless, or worthless.” It skirts the depravity and wretchedness a soul lost in Kali-yuga calls “life.” From that kind of worthless life Śrīla Prabhupāda has kindly rescued us and given us the life of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, devotional service to Kṛṣṇa.

I would therefore like to address and expose a most obnoxious and impure idea. It is this:

Śrīla Prabhupāda gave us the basics of Kṛṣṇa consciousness – and especially the externals of the *saṅkīrtana* movement – but he didn’t give us the innermost nectar, the most confidential essence of devotional service: pure, ecstatic, spontaneous love of God.

Perhaps because of the kind of students he had, perhaps because he was preaching to the masses, because he was concentrating on basic necessities, or simply because his movement and his disciples were still new, he held back on giving out that most sweet nectar.

But now things have changed. Twelve years have gone by, and some of us, at least, can no longer be satisfied just to follow regulative principles and ring temple bells. We hanker for that higher taste, which is ultimately what Kṛṣṇa consciousness is all about.

Now, I really have no quarrel with hankering for advancement. What could be wrong with wanting to advance? But what irks me is the idea – fallacious to the core – that the way to find advancement is to go outside what Śrīla Prabhupāda gave us.

I’m not saying that sin corrupts anyone who dares appreciate any other devotee of the Lord. But those who minimize Śrīla Prabhupāda’s glories are diseased, and we should know it.

Śrīla Prabhupāda has in fact made everything in Kṛṣṇa consciousness available to us – all the way to the highest, most perfect stages – but to achieve it we must patiently follow the correct procedure.

Those who think they can jump over the spiritual master’s instructions and somersault directly into the forests of Goloka Vṛndāvana can only wind up spiritually crippled or dead.

To enter Goloka Vṛndāvana, even to proceed on the path of pure devotion, we must sincerely follow in the footsteps of the spiritual master and try to satisfy him by carrying out his orders and serving him.

*yasya deve parā bhaktir*  
*yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthā*  
*prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

When one sincerely devotes himself to the lotus feet of the spiritual master and the lotus feet of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, all the confidential nectar of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is revealed.

The spiritual master is the most confidential servant of the Lord, and the only way to satisfy the Lord is to satisfy the spiritual master.

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ‘pi*

Only by satisfying our spiritual master can we ever hope to satisfy Kṛṣṇa. And if our spiritual master is dissatisfied we are spiritually nowhere.

Mundane cheap devotees imagine themselves entering the confidential service of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. But the true devotee factually enters the service of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī by dedicating himself, heart and soul, to the manifest representative of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, the spiritual master.

*athāpi te deva-padāmbhuja-dvaya-*  
*prasāda-leśānugṛhīta eva hi*  
*jānāti tattvaṁ bhagavan-mahimno*  
*na cānya eko ‘pi ciraṁ vicinvan*

Speculations, concoctions, additions, and subtractions only block our way. If we want to know the true glories of the Lord – His most sublime glories as the original Personality of Godhead – we can do so only if we get at least a little mercy from the Lord.

”Revival of the dormant affection or love of Godhead,” Śrīla Prabhupāda writes, “does not depend on the mechanical system of hearing and chanting, but it solely and wholly depends on the causeless mercy of the Lord. When the Lord is fully satisfied with the sincere efforts of the devotee, He may endow him with His loving transcendental service.”

But for us there is no question of satisfying the Lord or getting his mercy except through the causeless mercy of the spiritual master.

Therefore, those who sincerely and seriously stick to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s service and follow his instructions will enter Goloka Vṛndāvana. Those who try to enter Goloka Vṛndāvana directly, not through him, will join the devotees of illusion. Their love will turn to passion, their nectar to poison, and instead of entering the forests of Vṛndāvana they will be lost in the material woods.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we should recognize, did give us the purest nectar of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, both by word and by deed.

The highest nectar is not to chant the holy name in a solitary place but to distribute the holy name all over the world. And the highest pleasure is not to enjoy the holy name but to serve the holy name, and serve its servants.

And this is precisely the example that Śrīla Prabhupāda showed. While others swerved, Śrīla Prabhupāda served.

Śrīla Prabhupāda taught us how to make the service of the spiritual master’s orders our life and soul. By Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mercy, those who faithfully stick to his service, following his instructions carefully, will steadily advance, step by step, and ultimately reach the highest perfection. They will be endowed with all the blessings the spiritual master reserves for a faithful and gentle disciple.

Let us therefore glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda, His Divine Grace, and dedicate our lives to His eternal service.

*Your servant,*  
Jayādvaita Swami

## Keśava Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisance at your lotus feet on this most auspicious day of your appearance.

You have descended in this world to propagate the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the fallen people. You are directly descending from Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and should be respected as much as Kṛṣṇa Himself:

*sākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair*  
*uktas tathā bhāvyata eva sadbhiḥ*  
*kintu prabhor yaḥ priya eva tasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**”The spiritual master is to be honored as much as the Supreme Lord because he is the most confidential servitor of the Lord. This is acknowledged in all revealed scriptures and is followed by all authorities. Therefore I offer my respectful obeisance unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is a bona fide representative of Śrī Kṛṣṇa.”**

You are eternally liberated inhabitant of Goloka Vṛndāvana. You have entered into the most delicate pastimes of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava. That is the opinion of the Vaiṣṇava ācāryas: śrī-rādhikā-mādhavayor apāra...

So it is the great fortune of a conditioned souls when he can attain Kṛṣṇa’s mercy and come in contact with such a pure devotee who is always engaged in devotional service. In this way, I want to associate with you. I am praying to Kṛṣṇa that He will let me hear your words with rapt attention. The more I hear you, the more I will learn how to please you.

As it is said,

*guru kṛṣṇa-rūpa hana śāstrera pramāṇe*  
*guru-rūpe kṛṣṇa kṛpā karena bhakta-gaṇe*

**”According to the deliberate opinion of all the revealed scriptures, the spiritual master is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa. Lord Kṛṣṇa in the form of the spiritual master delivers His devotees.”**

You have demonstrated to us that if we trust your basic instructions for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness – books are the basis, preaching is the essence, utility is the principle, and purity is the force – we will surely be successful.

This is the sublime mission of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. That is, one should chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* and preach the instructions of Kṛṣṇa as they are given in the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. One only has to follow your instructions, and in that way one’s life will be spiritually successful.

I bet at your divine lotus feet that I may carry out your orders as my life and soul. So, on this auspicious day I pray to become more determined in hearing your instructions and executing them so that one day, by your mercy, I may become adorned with all the qualities of the Vaiṣṇavas.

*I beg to remain your humble servant,*  
Keśava dāsa Swami

## Kṛṣṇadāsa Swami

Salutations and homage at the lotus feet of His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Mahārāja Prabhupāda.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

My earliest memories stem from our first, and probably the most momentous, meeting in 1973. I was entangled in householder life at that time, but I was trying to preach *sanātana-dharma* in southeast London. At that meeting I asked you many personal questions about your birth, parentage, education, initiation, marriage, and children, and also about your spiritual master. You also asked me the same questions. We discussed my being initiated into the Brahma-sampradāya in my youth. After that, you started to preach to me very strongly and went on preaching for almost two hours, convincing me to join your movement. At first I was very much surprised to hear your suggestion. But you answered all my questions and allayed all my doubts. At last I had to surrender, and I requested you to become my *sannyāsa-guru*. Being merciful, you immediately accepted my request. Since then, I have been trying to serve Your Divine Grace in ISKCON under your guidance and blessings.

*ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān*  
*nāvamanyeta karhicit*  
*na martya-buddhyāsūyeta*  
*sarva-deva-mayo guruḥ*

**”One should know the** ***ācāryas*** **as Myself and never disrespect him in any way. One should not envy him, thinking him an ordinary man, for he is the representative of all the demigods.” (*****Bhāg*****. 11.17.27)**

Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī comments that a pure devotee observes the spiritual master as being one with the Personality of Godhead in the sense that he is very dear to the Lord, not identical to Him in all respects. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura confirms that all the revealed scriptures accept the spiritual master to be identical with the Personality of Godhead because he is a very dear and confidential servant of the Lord.

The *ācārya* is the representative of all the demigods. He is considered as follows:

*gurur brahmā guror viṣṇur*  
*gurur devo maheśvaraḥ*  
*guruḥ sākṣāt paraṁ brahma*  
*tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**”The spiritual master is respected as good as Lord Brahmā, Lord Śiva, and Lord Viṣṇu, for he is considered to be directly representing the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Therefore I offer my respectful obeisance unto him.”**

Considering the miraculous changes that I witnessed within myself from my fortuitous meeting with Your Divine Grace, and seeing the transformations you have inspired in so many other devotees the world over, I would also like to describe you in terms of the powers of the three *guṇāvatāras.*

Just as Lord Śiva accepts all fallen souls and delivers them making them Vaiṣṇava, so you very mercifully accepted as your disciples those born in Western countries and addicted to all types of abominable activities. Through your preaching, you destroyed their demonic mentality.

As Lord Brahmā is responsible for creation within the material universe, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, created divine qualities in your sincere followers.

You not only destroy the demonic mentality and create divine qualities, but through your books, instructions, and Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, you maintain our spiritual lives, just as Lord Viṣṇu maintains the cosmic manifestation.

So by your marvelous achievements, you have proven yourself to be as good as Lord Śiva, as good as Lord Brahmā, and as good as Lord Śrī Hari. But of all your achievements, the fact that you have picked me up from rotting in material life remains the most miraculous to me.

Please curb my demoniac mentality, create the qualities of a devotee in me, and maintain me in my humble efforts to serve you as you requested me.

*Your worthless servant,*  
Kṛṣṇadāsa Swami, Bhaktivedanta Manor

## Lokanātha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisance at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Even while the parent bird is flying high in the sky, it is always thinking of the welfare of its fledgling in the nest. In the same way, I feel that wherever you are, you are always thinking of us and are concerned for our welfare. Dialogue between yourself and us is continuous. We have so many questions, and you answer these questions in so many ways – especially through your books, and sometimes even through dreams. But Vyāsa- pūjā day is extra special because that dialogue becomes more formal and a meeting of hearts is made possible. That is why devotees all over the world anxiously await this special opportunity to communicate with you.

While you were present on this planet I had more opportunity to talk to you and write to you, but now that is only possible for me once a year. So, now I want to convey to you my heartfelt feelings and patiently wait for you to get back to me in one of your many inconceivable ways.

**Shooting for the Rhinoceros**

It started in the year 1976 with a simple bullock-cart program of village-to-village preaching. Then, in 1984, we set out from Dvārakā and returned there in 1988, having successfully completed our circumambulation of the Indian subcontinent. Devotees in Guyana are performing *pada-yātrā* in their own country, and earlier this year we inaugurated “Pada-yātrā America.” And this is just the beginning. We have become *pada-yātrā* addicts, with no desire to kick the habit. Engaged in this tireless preaching campaign of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti, pada-yātrīs* have walked faithfully, year after year, kilometer after kilometer. *Pada-yātrā* is not a new idea. It is not a speculation or concoction. It is by you desire that this *pada-yātrā* program was established, and it is by your mercy that it is continuing.

In 1996 we will be celebrating your centennial – the one hundredth anniversary of your appearance – and in honor of that most auspicious occasion, we are contemplating organizing *pada-yātrās* in one hundred different countries around the world. During the next seven years, we hope to have completed at least 100,000 kilometers on foot as our special offering unto you. This may sound like “shooting for the rhinoceros,” but isn’t that how you trained us, to think big? In a letter dated March 16, 1977, you inspired us to “shoot for the stars.” You wrote, “We can introduce many millions of such carts all around the world.”

We fallen, conditioned souls need full-time engagement to keep us out of *māyā,* and this one-hundred-country *pada-yātrā* should be just what we need to keep us busy. An easy-going life means it is easy for *māyā* to enter, so we need the toughness of *pada-yātrā* life to keep māyā at bay. Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day, please bless us to continue this program and achieve our 100,000 km goal.

**Don’t get packed up in the center**

I find myself in a dilemma. In a letter dated December 14, 1976, you wrote to me, “You go to the villages for preaching and come back. But if you become packed up in the center, that is not wanted.” As I take on more and more managerial responsibilities, it seems that I am in danger of becoming just that – packed up. My heart is in *pada-yātrā.* Pada-yātrā is the perfect situation for a *sannyāsī* or *brāhmaṇa,* whereas management is the work of a *kṣatriya*. I do not understand what my *varṇa* is – *brāhmaṇa, kṣatriya,* or both. What is my specific service? Or should I be a “jack of all trades and master of none”? Sooner or later I will have to make this decision, and the sooner the better.

I consulted my God brothers in this regard, but their advice has been contradictory. Many say I should just “do the needful,” but my energies are limited, and those limited energies need to be conserved and cautiously invested in those areas of practical involvement most suitable for me. In other words, you authorized me to do *pada-yātrā,* but what about the other areas of service I am gradually becoming more and more involved in? Do they meet with your approval?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were perfect in all respects. You did everything perfectly, so please, please give me direction. Please reveal to me the correct path to follow so I can proceed with full confidence, in the knowledge that what I am doing is authorized by you and pleasing to you.

**I am happy that I am no young anymore**

This year I will be forty years old. I am happy that I am no young anymore. I was fed up and frustrated with the bad qualities of youth: frivolousness, foolishness, inexperience, and immaturity. I want them to be replaced with the qualities of old age: gravity and wisdom. Most of us, your disciples, are now coming into our forties and fifties, and we are hoping that we will become more and more effective in our preaching as we mature in age.

It is seen – even in this Kali-yuga, where respect is not a prominent quality – that elderly people, especially elderly saintly persons, are generally afforded more respect. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu accepted *sannyāsa* in order that people would offer Him due respect and would not commit offenses against Him. Similarly, we are hoping that our preaching efforts will be more effective in older age because people will naturally respect our senior years.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you old age was one of your opulences. Remembering your pastimes, we see that your greatest preaching achievements came in your old age. In your latter years you journeyed to the West, made so many disciples, established so many centers, and printed so many books, all to help us fallen, conditioned souls.

My own years on this planet are numbered. With the passing of each year, this life’s journey is nearing its end. May my old age give me further impetus to take to spiritual life one thousand times more seriously. This is certainly not a time to sit back and become complacent. I had better get my act together if I am to accomplish something tangible for your pleasure in the time that is left to me.

**We are doing fine now, Śrīla Prabhupāda**

At last it looks like the turbulent storm is finally over and the ISKCON boat is sailing smoothly once again. We see that stalwart devotees are passing the test of time, although, unfortunately, a few have at least temporarily become mice again. It is a victory of divine over demoniac. You are back in the center again, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Devotees are more friendly towards each other, and even big devotees are accessible to small-timers like myself.

I remember how you often quoted the example of boiling down milk. There has been a boiling down in ISKCON in the sense that there have been many reforms and changes in the way of thinking. Our Society has passed through a lot, and time has stirred up and changed many things. This process of condensation has evaporated our false egos, thus purifying our hearts and drawing us closer to you. I can see that the spirit of cooperation that you so much desired to see between your disciples has at last begun to emerge. We love you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! It is a time for great jubilation.

This spirit of cooperation was very evident at the recent Kumbha-mela festival at Allahabad and at the last few Māyāpura-Vṛndāvana festivals. The general mood among devotees participating in these festivals definitely took a much more positive turn. The *prajalpa,* politics, etc., were drastically minimized, and in their place came lots of interest and opportunity to engage in *kṛṣṇa-kathā*. What a relief!

If we can now become truly united and stand together to fight and conquer the enemy, *māyā,* then victory is assured for us, our soldiers, on all fronts. We recently witnessed the power of unity and purity with the success of the campaign to free the Soviet devotees. In the beginning the authorities in the Soviet Union, one of the world’s “superpowers,” felt threatened. With all their might they tried to stop your movement. However, they ultimately just had to surrender at the lotus feet of the power of the holy name.

Soviet Hare Kṛṣṇa devotees-kī jaya! Śrīla Prabhupāda-kī jaya! Caitanya Mahāprabhu-kī jaya!

**Prayer for the year**

It’s there. Yes, yes, it’s still there. It’s so subtle, but just because others don’t see it, that doesn’t mean it doesn’t exist. Śrīla Prabhupāda, lust in its very fine, residual form is lurking within my heart. Its presence makes me feel so bad. Lust, the sinful enemy of the world, has covered my pure consciousness, thus making me an unalloyed devotee of māyā.

During the past seventeen years I have chanted my daily sixteen rounds more than five thousand times; I have performed austerity by circumambulating the entire Indian subcontinent on foot; I have read your books and lectured in twenty different countries – but still my enemy doesn’t leave me alone. How gradual is this process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness? When will lust’s rule come to an end? Or, rather, will it ever come to an end?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, only you can help me. Kṛṣṇa, Madhusūdana, the killer of the Madhu demon, is yours. Please appeal to Him on my behalf. That powerful Lord can very easily drive away this all-devouring enemy.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless me so that I may continue my onward journey, back to the lotus feet of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

*Your servant,*  
Lokanātha Swami

## Mahānidhi Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Your greatness, mercy, and beauty cannot be compared to anything produced within this material world. Being completely pure and transcendental, your universally attractive qualities originate in the spiritual world. The Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Kṛṣṇa, has personally created and decorated you with these wonderful and amazing attributes.

In attempting to glorify you on this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, we will cite a few Sanskrit verses praising Śrī Kṛṣṇa by comparing Him to the lotus flower. The lotus flower is the symbol of beauty in the material world. This celestial flower begins humbly from the muddy bottom of a clear, placid lake. It rises quickly through the dark and watery depths to blossom under the sun’s brilliant encouragement. The fabled and famous thousand-petal lotus, dressed in red, yellow, blue, or white, explodes in waves of beauty cresting with saffron-hued pollen particles.

The lotus flower is a traditional object of meditation for the *yogīs,* an alluring subject for poets, painters, and photographers, and a perennial symbol of purity and beauty, peace, and fulfillment. And ironically, a lotus flower once caused a heavenly war.

In the original *Vaiṣṇava Songbook* translation of the “Śrī Guru-vandana” prayer, the spiritual master is likened to a lotus flower five times in four stanzas. “The lotus feet of the spiritual master are the only way to attain pure devotional service.” The whole process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is based on faithfully following a pure devotee of the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa. While taking *darśana* of Govinda, Brahmā said, “Kṛṣṇa stands before me with His two small lotus feet.” Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your small feet, barely size eight, are like two huge lotus flowers spreading their reddish rays throughout this darkened planet. By strictly following in your footsteps without trying to change your pattern or surpass your lead, the disciple will follow you back home, back to Godhead.

Kṛṣṇa used to leave the imprint of His lotus feet on the land of Vṛndāvana. Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your constant traveling you left your lotus footprints all over the earth. People everywhere are engaging in Kṛṣṇa’s service by contacting your lotus feet in the form of books, videos, and tapes. *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* states that “the waters of the Ganges flow from the tip of the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa, and by bathing in that water everyone, including fruitive actors and sages, can wash the dirty things from the mind.” Purification of one’s material desires occurs gradually by repeated bathing in the Ganges. However, when one serves a pure devotee like you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, one’s consciousness becomes rapidly cleansed, and as the *gopīs* said of Lord Kṛṣṇa, “Your lotus feet certainly kill all kinds of sinful activities of the devotees who have taken shelter there.”

”We bow to your lotus feet with great awe and reverence.” Lord Kṛṣṇa’s Vāmanadeva incarnation astounded Bali Mahārāja by poking His toe through the universal shell. Thus the Ganges River surged into this universe. We are overwhelmed by the oceanic mercy contained in your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda. This flow of kindness is so intense and expansive that wherever conditioned souls bathe in it, they become sinless, satisfied, and saintly devotees of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. The Ganges is restricted to India, but your mercy is available in dozens of languages in hundreds of countries and to thousands of sincere souls.

By the benediction of the dust of your pure lotus feet, one can touch the lotus feet of the Supreme Lord. No one can become a pure devotee unless another pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa favors him. Uddhava prayed for a birth as a blade of grass in Vṛndāvana so as to receive the dust from the *gopī’s* feet. We pray to forever hold your lotus feet upon our heads. According to *Teachings of Lord Caitanya*, unless one surrenders unto the lotus feet of the Supreme Lord, certainly even great thinkers will become bewildered. For us, surrender means to follow your instructions, adhere to the four regulative principles, always chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, and benefit others by preaching. Bewilderment means forgetting one’s spiritual identity and falsely enjoying this temporary world.

Lord Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental body resembles the lotus flower in eight parts. Queen Kuntī glorifies the lotus navel, garland, eyes, and feet of Kṛṣṇa in the *Bhāgavatam.* Perceiving Kṛṣṇa’s lotus beauty is somewhat difficult, but one can easily see how your body, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is as enchanting as the lotus flower.

From the navel (*namaḥ paṅkaja-nābhāya*) of Garbhodakaśāyī Viṣṇu, a lotus flower grows, upon which Brahmā, the creator of the universe, becomes manifest. *Paṅkaja* is one of the many Sanskrit words for lotus flower (*paṅka* means “mud”; *ja* means “generated”). Although the lotus flower is generated from the mud, it is a most beautiful and important flower, very much liked by Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your mind, which is like a lotus flower, has produced a simply wonderful lotus like creation called ISKCON. This creation further expands and expands as the opening of new temples adds petal after petal. Actually, before the end of Lord Caitanya’s golden age, you will manifest a new variety of lotus flower far surpassing the fabled thousand-petal lotus. With ISKCON temples in every town and village, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, will introduce the “million-petal lotus”! ISKCON, although founded in the muddiest and darkest of ages, Kali, will grow and spread into a most pleasing and significant society to save conditioned living entities from suffering.

As the dust on the lotus flower exhibits exquisite beauty, so the millions of future ISKCON devotees will magnify the incomparable magnificence of ISKCON’s founder*-ācārya,* Śrīla Prabhupāda.

The lotus is a friend of the sun and thrives by his glance. Śrīla Prabhupāda, proclaimed “the Kṛṣṇa sun” as your Back to Godhead magazine motto – *kṛṣṇa sūrya-sama māyā haya andhakāra.* Please help us keep the ISKCON lotus flower bathing in the energizing and purifying beams of the “Kṛṣṇa sun.”

The gopīs once prayed, “Dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, please come before us and bless us with the lotus flower in Your hand.” We too always hanker for a blessing from your lotus hands, Śrīla Prabhupāda. When playing *karatālas, mṛdaṅga,* or harmonium, your lotus hands were as sweet and soft as a flower. But like Lord Nṛsiṁha’s lotus hand tipped with steel nails, your soft lotus hands could be as hard as thunderbolts when writing a letter to chastise a nonsense disciple or pounding your low desk while condemning the rascal atheistic scientists.

In New Dvārakā your lotus hands blessed us with “Prabhupāda Cookies,” like a mother giving sweets to elicit good behavior from a rebellious child. Then we were young and wild, down on authority and distrusting anyone over thirty years old, except you. Now the cookies are gone and we are way over thirty, but we still need and pray for the fortunate blessings of your lotus hand.

Queen Kuntī praises Kṛṣṇa for having a gorgeous garland of lotuses: *namaḥ paṅkaja-māline.* He wears this garland even when fighting demons like Kālayavana. In appreciation for His killing Balvalāsura, the *brāhmaṇas* presented Lord Balarāma with the lotus garland of victory. Śrīla Prabhupāda, without compromising your principles or losing any purity, you came to the demoniac Western cities to fight and defeat the atheists, agnostics, and hedonists. You single-handedly and successfully conquered, collected, and transformed “the flowers of your generation” into a wonderful victory garland of five thousand loving lotus-flower disciples.

Your attractive eyes are very large and expressive, and when you glorify Kṛṣṇa they open wide like an unfolding lotus flower drinking in the scorching sunshine. Hearing and speaking about the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, tremendously enlivens you, whereas our owl-like eyes remain shut in the sun of Kṛṣṇa’s presence. Please bless us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with your cooling lotus glance, and deliver us from the raging fire of material existence.

”My only wish is to have my consciousness purified by the words emanating from your lotus mouth.” Sometimes drones hover over the honey in the lotus flower. Śrīla Prabhupāda, when you stopped during your daily morning walks, you appeared like the whorl of a lotus flower surrounded by your beelike devotees. We clustered around you, hoping to catch some Kṛṣṇa nectar cascading from your lotus mouth. In Vṛndāvana’s lakes, various kinds of lotus flowers bloom, such as the *kahlāra, kañja*, and *utpala,* and the air carries their aromatic pollen. You gave personal instructions, showed us Kṛṣṇa’s superexcellent beauty, and taught us how to live and work for Kṛṣṇa, love Him, and how to go home, back to Godhead. Your words on every subject were authoritative and perfect because you related everything to Kṛṣṇa. Wherever the air transports your words, that place will become pure and pleasing.

The *parijāta* is a kind of lotus flower grown on the heavenly planets. To obtain this flower for Queen Satyabhāma, Lord Kṛṣṇa and the Pāṇḍavas once went to war with Indra and the other demigods. By the causeless mercy of Lord Caitanya and Kṛṣṇa, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, descended to this mundane world. Despite all our pleading and fighting, Kṛṣṇa and His eternal associates like the Pāṇḍavas have taken you back to their kingdom in Goloka Vṛndāvana. As Lakṣmī-devī attained perfection by serving Viṣṇu’s lotus feet, we pray for attachment to your lotus feet, which is the perfection that fulfills all spiritual desires.

The spiritual abode is shaped like a thousand-petaled lotus, and the petals are the abodes of the *gopīs,* who are part and parcel of Kṛṣṇa, to whom they are most lovingly devoted. Śrīla Prabhupāda, since you are always serving the *gopīs,* surely you must be residing now on one of those lotus petals. But as Mother Yaśodā had a scheme to capture Kṛṣṇa, we take solace from *Bhāgavatam’s* Eleventh Canto, which says, “If a person’s heart is always tied to the lotus feet of the Supreme Lord with the ropes of love, the Lord doesn’t leave him.” The secret to keeping your lotus like association, then, is to love you, beginning with always thinking of you. First, Ambarīṣa Mahārāja fixed his mind on Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet: *sa vai manaḥ kṛṣṇa-padāravindayoḥ.* Then all other, subordinate senses engaged in suitable services for Kṛṣṇa’s pleasure.

According to the verse beginning *samāśritā ye pada-pallava-plavaṁ mahat-padaṁ punya-yaśo murāreḥ,* by taking shelter of Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet one crosses the ocean of repeated birth and death. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please allow us to take full shelter under the shade of your lotus feet. King Kulaśekhara prayed that the swan of his mind would become entangled in the stems of Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. And quoting a verse from his *Mukunda-mālā-stotra,* we close our offering to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda –

”My only prayer is that at the time of my death I may simply remember Your two beautiful feet, which are just like lotus flowers blooming during the autumn season.”

*Your servant,*  
Mahānidhi Swami

## Mahāśṛṅga Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisance unto your lotus feet eternally.

Who you are I’m not really sure, but what you’ve done for the living entities in Kali, there is no doubt. You are the key, gateway, and passport to the spiritual world. By following your divine instructions, guidelines, and lessons in your books, one is assured of the perfection in life, to return back home. Therefore, I and all the jīvas in this miserable age fall in the dust of your footsteps and pray that we can engage in your service forever.

To me, you are a direct incarnation of Lord Viṣṇu’s mercy potency who has descended on this earth to give an opportunity to the most fallen creatures for upliftment toward the most intimate form of love of God. Therefore, it is not possible to glorify your exalted activities adequately with our insignificant words.

Anyone who had the great good fortune of meeting Your Divine Grace was immediately directed on the spiritual path. And for those most fortunate souls who seriously took up the pursuit of religious principles, their lethargic lives were transformed into ones with direction and purpose. Thousands of personalities blossomed like lotus flowers with their hands outstretched, singing and dancing in ecstasy.

Although none of us could ever understand your personal relationship with Lord Kṛṣṇa, the directions He gave were always perfect. Every endeavor and all your activities were glorious and successful, with your unlimited good qualities increasing at every moment. The impact and influence of the knowledge in your books on the religious systems of today will continue for the next ten thousand years, exactly as the sun-god expands his shining rays throughout the universe.

You were always firmly determined, even in the midst of undisciplined, weak-minded disciples, and because you were a lover of brahminical culture, always situated in truth and merciful to the poor and innocent, you gave shelter to all surrendered souls.

You were invincible in your travels around the globe, being uniquely powerful and heroic. No one could defeat your logic and reasoning, which was backed up by the Vedic literature, and your reputation as the ultimate champion who changed the direction of Kali-yuga is continuing to expand through the universe.

Thus I can only conclude that you are a *nitya-mukta* living entity who has been directly empowered by the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Being a liberated soul, without change and completely learned, you are representing the Supersoul as His expansion.

It is only by the mercy of Lord Caitanya that I am striving to become your worthy disciple. If by chance I don’t succeed in this life, I only pray for the opportunity to have you as my spiritual master in my next life.

All glories to your *vapur* and *vāṇī* forms forever!

*Your eternal servant*  
Mahāśṛṅga Swami

## Nava-yogendra Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisance at the dust of your lotus-like feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who are very kind upon the fallen souls like myself.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, today is your divine appearance day. Yesterday was the appearance day of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and today is your appearance day. The Lord’s mission was *paritrāṇāya sādhūnām,* “to annihilate the demons and establish religious principles,” and your mission is the same. People were forgetful of the Lord’s instructions, so Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa sent you to revive those instructions and help us avoid falling down to a hellish condition of life. You saved many souls from that kind of life and gave them the real meaning of life.

You gave us the highest understanding of life, but I am so unfortunate and dull that I am not able to follow your instructions. Please give me the strength and intelligence to follow your instructions so that I can make a little advancement before the end of my life comes. This life is short, and I am wasting my time in useless thinking.

Please protect your great Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement from bad people – those who have joined your movement for some material benefit. Your movement is purely a spiritual movement. There is no room for materialism. I remember once, one person wanted to come to Africa with me, and Your Divine Grace inquired, “What for he wants to go there? To do some business?” You were very particular about this. You used to say, “My temples are not hotels where people can come to eat and sleep. My temples are for spiritual seekers, those who want to make advancement in spiritual life.”

There is so much materialism all over, which is the cause of our suffering. You gave us the alternative: spiritual life, which alone can relieve our suffering. But we are so unfortunate that we are not so serious about what you gave us. Please give us intelligence so that we will behave ourselves.

In the end, I beg for your mercy and blessings. Haribol.

*Your humble servant,*  
Nava-yogendra Swami

## Raghunātha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisance at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Burning in the fire of conditional life, we cry out for your mercy. Without the association and blessings of one such as you, our lives are wretched failures. You light the way, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and guide us on the path to reach you. What greater goal can exist beyond the shade of your lotus feet?

To please guru is to please Kṛṣṇa, and by your causeless mercy all things can be done. Therefore please turn your glance upon us that the age of Kali cannot impede our efforts to serve you, thereby satisfying the Supreme Lord. Make us dance, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Make us dance. Make us dance as you like.

Your fallen servant,  
Raghunātha Swami

## Rāma-Govinda B.G. Goswami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this holy day of your appearance, let me prostrate myself at your divine lotus-like feet to offer this humble homage:

*bhaktisiddhānta-śiśyāya*  
*bhaktivedānta-nāmine*  
*prasannāya praśāntāya*  
*tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**”Let me offer my obeisance unto my Guru Mahārāja, who is a disciple of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, who is always calm and joyful, and who bears the name Bhaktivedanta.”**

You have departed, but at the same time you remain with me in the form of your teachings and books. You have left these behind as your transcendental inheritance and instructed us to distribute your teachings to everyone throughout the whole world. But without your mercy, I am helpless and without the strength to carry on your mission. As Lord Kṛṣṇa explains in the *Bhagavad-gītā,*

*manaḥ-ṣaṣṭhānīdriyāṇī*  
*prakṛti-sthāni karṣati*

The mind and the senses want to overcome the intelligence, and in this way the material nature is trying to cover the living entity. Thus we risk forgetting the real mission of life, which is to spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu, as taught by Your Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Let me therefore prostrate myself before you and beg for your mercy, so I may be able to push on the *saṅkīrtana* movement steadily and faithfully, thus helping to fulfill your mission.

*kabe heno kṛpā, labhiyā e jana,*  
*kṛtārtha hoibe, nātha*  
*śakti-buddhi-hīna, āmi ati dīna*  
*koro’ more ātma-sātha*

**”O lord and master! When will this devotee be blessed by obtaining your mercy? I am low, fallen, and devoid of all strength and intelligence. Please make me your beloved servant.” (*****Śaraṇāgati)***

Everything around me is gradually disappearing – father, wife, sons, and so on. Everything was so real, but gradually they are all vanishing.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please do not let me fall into the mistake of rejecting your society and the wonderful devotees in it and at the same time trying to continue calling you “Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.” I know that would be offensive and meaningless.

You are eternally related with all the devotees in ISKCON – whether they be your direct disciples or your grand disciples or simply members who like to serve you ISKCON. Your title is “Śrīla Prabhupāda” because, at your lotus feet, many conditioned souls have found refuge by means of following your instructions. You gave them the holy name of the Lord – the *mahā-mantra,* Hare Kṛṣṇa. Your Divine Grace has given new hope in life by your books, such as the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, The Nectar of Devotion,* and *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta.* Your Divine Grace has given strength and determination by your example and conviction, as shown by how you lived your life and how you departed from this planet.

Some are dead and gone, while others have simply left me. But in the middle of all this illusion...

You appear, coming from the eternal abode of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa,  
to relieve our lamentation and suffering condition.  
You arise within my mind when I remember your teachings.  
You appear in front of me when I study your books.  
You appear before me in form of your disciples – my God brothers –  
to correct me and chastise me.  
You appear before me to give me protection from the illusory energy of  
Kṛṣṇa by keeping me within your ISKCON society and guiding me by  
your GBC.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I never was a good disciple as a *brahmacārī*, a *gṛhastha*, or a *sannyaṣī.* I do not want to be the best devotee. My only request is that you let me be steady and faithful to your mission.

Four years ago, on the appearance day of my *param-guru* – your Guru Mahārāja – I took *sannyāsa,* and the definitive way of my life was given. At that time I received the *sannyāsa* name of Rāma-Govinda Bhakti Gauḍīya Goswami Mahārāja. I took this name as definitive confirmation that my life should be dedicated to spreading the mission of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya, as taught by Your Divine Grace. I came to that conclusion by reading a very nice portion of Your Divine Grace’s translation of the *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi-līlā* 1.19), which runs as follows:

*ei tina ṭhākura gauḍīyāke kariyāchena ātmasāt*  
*e tinera caraṇa vandoṅ, tine mora nātha*

**”These three Deities of Vṛndāvana \[Madana-mohana, Govinda, and Gopīnātha\] have absorbed the heart and soul of the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas \[followers of Lord Caitanya\]. I worship Their lotus feet, for They are the Lords of my heart.”**

And you write in your purport: “These three Deities are the life and soul of the Bengal Vaiṣṇavas, or Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas, who have a natural aptitude for residing in Vṛndāvana.”

So, I remember your voice when you initiated me: “Your name is Rāma-Govinda dāsa,” which means Balarāma and Kṛṣṇa. And years later, another name was added: Bhakti Gauḍīya Goswami Mahārāja, a name which I took as an indication that I have to assist you in the service of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma in Vṛndāvana. Also, because you are a Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava and have accepted initiation into the Brahma-Gauḍīya *sampradāya,* I have to assist you in spreading the teachings of devotional service to Śrī Kṛṣṇa in the way of the Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana, as you have presented in your *Nectar of Devotion,* which is a summery study of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī’s *Bhakti-raṣamṛta-sindhu.*

Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, my humble prayer on this Vyāsa-pūjā *tithi* is that you let me help you in the service of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma by spreading the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrī Caitanya and Prabhu Nityānanda, and the way will be by the distribution of your transcendental books.

Your divine lotus feet are my only shelter and refuge. I have no real interest in devotional service, and I am a poor and lowly *sannyāsī* named  
Rāma-Govinda B.G. Goswami

## Romapāda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear master and saving grace, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

On this day of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, huddled happily at your holy feet, with assembled voices wholeheartedly heralding your most auspicious appearance, we are mindful with a singular concentration: your glorification and your pleasure. My chorus or refrain, added to the orchestration of celebrating voices, is quite simple: I am most grateful to experience, lifelong, your loving service.

As the *mahā-bhāgavata* Vaiṣṇava, while traversing the entire face of the earth’s surface, your raised to the platform of *utsahān-bhakti* so many amazingly qualified souls. These inspired servitors accomplished, under your expert guidance and titanic inspiration, miraculous expressions of devotion.

To cite one typical example: I very recently met one gray-haired, prim-and-proper University of Texas administrator in Austin who asked about her former art department chairmen and his wife. Many years ago they both moved into the Dallas temple, completely inspired by your brief personal association and the all-attractive devotion of your loving servants. Professor Garland (I believe that was his name) was quite the charismatic and talented fellow himself, who had built up a big name, a big following amongst students, and had a well-respected post and profile on campus. “Who could imagine,” the administrator reasoned, “how great was the person unto whom Professor Garland rededicated his life!”

This one example is but a drop of your manifested mercy. Your mere presence on this earth, as a preacher of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, created a riot, a spiritual sensation in the camp of *māyā.* You moved the hearts, minds, and intelligence of millions. This is the impact of a *mahā-bhāgavata* preacher.

I am not to be counted among those who possessed some great talent. Nor have I ever performed something extraordinary to win your special attention or the attention of the many admirers of your great movement. Nonetheless, like thousands of others, both ordinary and extraordinary, I became swept away by your mere movements across the face of this planet. I consciously decided to become your surrendered servant, specifically in acknowledgement of your feature of unlimited self-sacrificing compassion. This feature of yours alone was so overwhelmingly powerful that I felt compelled to reciprocate. It was not enough to offer my private respects. It was not enough to study and try to enact your perfect words. I felt so obliged to you for the gift of the path of perfection that I submitted myself to work within the ranks of your created temples. My stubborn independence was overwhelmed by your purity and its attendant mission. I had taken advantage, to my unlimited fortune, of your sublime teachings; and you were looking to us, the fresh crop of newly fortunate souls, to help carry this fortune to others. O illustrious assembly of worshipers, can you hear the call?

Even the smallest selflessly rendered service to Kṛṣṇa gives the greatest satisfaction. Who can *imagine* the depth of your endearment to Kṛṣṇa? “Gallons of blood I have shed,” you once described, “for each soul brought to this great movement!” You, our eternal spiritual father, have toiled on Mahāprabhu’s behalf to provide for all your spiritual children whatever we needed and *more,* to achieve the perfection of life, love of Godhead. I am most appreciative of these priceless gifts. Please accept these words of praise, lovingly offered, and my lifelong service, as an incomplete expression of my gratitude.

The great pause, contemplating your call  
to rescue all.  
Who can even think this broadly? “All humanity?”  
”In all lands?”  
This only a mahā-bhagavata can do, and that is you.  
”Deliver them!”  
Staggering duties. “Write transcendental literature,  
become guru, be GBC’s, guide my  
worldwide society’s affairs”  
And more. This requires the liberated soul!  
Irresponsible stagnancy,  
\[“Hold still the hands of time!”\]  
Nor charades hold the clue.  
O mahā-bhāgavata, Your Divine Grace,  
Your vāṇī, architect’s blueprint, tells all.

I. “Become moonlike,  
Each, by pracāra and ācāra,  
to carry Mahāprabhu’s mission.”

Turning crows to swans, faint stars to moons,  
This magic is your mission.

“In love-and-trust exchanges,  
Work together” –  
The test of our love for you.

As an army command passes over a suspension bridge, marching in time, it will smash to pieces a structure that otherwise can hold tons upon tons of no synchronized weight. So moves a well-coordinated *saṅkīrtana* army, to accomplish the impossible.

Your “*saṅkīrtana* army” was filled with diversity, according to our personal capacity. Individual expression was never suppressed; to the contrary, unique contributions were maximally encouraged. Your “*saṅkīrtana* army” was most joyful, all inviting, magical.

Healthy signs abound once again: sublime preaching aplenty reappearing, old members returning, managers extending inter-temple and cross-national helping hands. Please, our founder*-ācārya,* empower our Vaiṣṇava leaders with all intelligence to combine the swelling tide of auspicious elements. ”A preacher is one who can detect a small spark of interest in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and turn the spark into a flame. An expert preacher can turn a small flame into a big blaze!” you once said. As we dedicate ourselves to become expert *sādhakas*, please help us glorify your name in this world.

And please count me as one who wants to assist, despite my many shortcomings.

*Your humble servant,*  
Romapāda Swami

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

O Prabhupāda, you are the saintly person guiding the lives of thousands of devotees in the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, and you will continue to be the central person because of your Bhaktivedanta purports and your unexcelled achievements as a preacher and pure devotee;

O Prabhupāda, who sometimes appear very dimly in the memory, because our Kali-yuga brains are so limited, and who are therefore a Nārada Muni of the modern age, available in retrieval systems like books, *mūrtis* of exact likeness, and taped lectures, movies, and photos – in which we find you strongly present;

O Prabhupāda, you are the early-morning *Bhāgavata* writer, the witty commentator, the blasting attacker of demons who misinterpret the *Bhagavad-gītā,* and the enjoyer of a simple meal of *kṛṣṇa-prasādam;*

O Lord Kṛṣṇa, please reveal Prabhupāda to our minds in many ways throughout the day and night, and let him appear in our speech; and as we scan a page of a letter or essay that we have written, let the name “Prabhupāda” appear a few times on each page along with “Kṛṣṇa” and “Caitanya,” thus assuring literary excellence and substance to our writings;

O Prabhupāda, whom we forget, please let us remember that Śrī Kṛṣṇa will reveal Himself to us only when we please His pure devotee;

O Prabhupāda, who sit at the end of the *kīrtana* hall facing Rādhā-Govinda, and who smile as the devotees promenade toward the Deity and then turn and promenade toward you;

O Prabhupāda, who pull the devotees through tough times, as when you told us to expect stiff resistance to the *saṅkīrtana* movement from those people who are like dogs, hogs, camels, and asses, and who warned us not to expect anything auspicious from nondevotees, even if they be scholars or altruists;

O Prabhupāda, whom we neglect and offend, but who take us back like a kind father accepting his children who commit many mistakes;

O Prabhupāda, who are the topmost theologian not because you invent new theories of God, but because you stay faithful to the conclusion of the richest theology of devotion, and who are never a mere theorizer but who asks everyone to directly surrender to God, and who explain why, by pointing out the foolishness of materialism and the many attractive qualities of the Personality of Godhead;

O Prabhupāda, you are very close to Kṛṣṇa, but I am very far from Him. Kindly tell us again how we also are actually close to Kṛṣṇa because He is within our hearts; please remind us of these eternal truths, and never give up on us, despite our persistent faults.

*Your servant,*  
Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Smita Kṛṣṇa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I am just a speck of dust at your divine lotus feet.  
Seated there, I am free from anxiety.  
Remembering your transcendental feats,  
I am hoping for ISKCON’s longevity.

What use is there in a life like a tree?  
Life is short and breathing counted.  
Is my breathing like a blacksmith’s bellows?  
Soon my life will be accounted.

The mind wants to deviate:  
It won’t read your books.  
It makes an endless debate;  
Finally it gets hooked.

Great hope is there for me  
Because of your transcendental ecstasy.  
Let me surrender mind, body and words  
To preserve your transcendental legacy.

No hope for me to be like you,  
But let me try to carry your shoe.  
Being limited in time and space,  
I cannot help keep your pace.

With faith and conviction  
We repeat you edict.  
The future looks bright –  
We try to predict.

To vanquish the rule of demons,  
You preached, not caring for the hedonists.  
Thus whatever breath we have left,  
Let us use it to defeat repeated birth and death.

You wasted your time.  
It is already too late:  
The demons are shaken.  
That is their fate.

I am caught by attachment and aversion.  
You are cutting the bondage of sinful life.  
The age of Kali in bankruptcy is found  
When your glories are chanted all around.

To write an offering to you is not an easy thing  
Because my heart is full of sin.  
But in the process my heart gets purged,  
And thus new hopes emerge.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are my only shelter.  
Please accept this as an offering.  
I hope it is not offending.  
Please have patience with this fledgling.

*I beg to remain a humble servant of your divine lotus feet,*  
Smita Kṛṣṇa Swami

## Somaka Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisance at your lotus feet.

You once said, “My *guru-mahārāja* used to say that our *mṛdaṅga* can be heard for a few blocks’ distance but our books are the great *mṛdaṅga* because they can go thousands of miles distant. So I ask all of you: Please continue to print and distribute the books, and in that way Kṛṣṇa consciousness will spread all over the world, and the people will wonder that this old man keeps beating his drum.”

Yes, the sound of your drum arrived thousands of miles distant: In 1972 there was no temple in Italy – at least it was not yet manifested – but there was a temple in France, a country thousands of miles away. But through one of my friends your drumbeat reached me in the form of a copy of *Back to Godhead* magazine. I started to read, and from thousands of miles away you started to beat the drum:

”*Ta-ge* – Kṛṣṇa is God. *Ge-ta –* you are His servant. *Jere-ghena-tare-kheta* – please chant the holy name.”

Your drum was so strong that my consciousness miraculously changed. I sent my little savings to the Los Angeles BBT and ordered books and a record. The day the package came was very special. I ran to the post office to claim it and then ran back home. I opened it with trembling hands, turned on the record player, and began playing the record. My heart was beating fast as I heard your grave voice sing, “*Cintāmaṇi*...” An ocean of peace and bliss came over me. I flipped the record over several times – over and over – so sweet, so sweet. Before I had realized how the time had passed, it was already dark.

I picked up the books – Kṛṣṇa books 1, 2, and 3; *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is; Easy Journey to Other Planets;* and *Kṛṣṇa Consciousness, the Topmost Yoga System*. I looked at the books and touched them. I was so fulfilled; everything was so magical. I picked up a small one – *Kṛṣṇa Consciousness, the Topmost Yoga System* – and as I sat on my bed I started to read. It was so nice, I could not stop. Your drum was beating so strong, so strong: “*Ta-ge* – stop your nonsense. *Ge-ta* – become Kṛṣṇa conscious.” At the end of the last page of your *kīrtana* I was floating in an ocean of nectar, so sweetly had your words entered my heart. I looked at my watch: two in the morning.

I woke my wife and said,  
“We should give up all nonsense.”  
”But some of that ‘nonsense’ is nice.”  
”No, read here: Prabhupāda says, ‘All sense gratification is illusion, *māyā*.’”  
Your drumbeat entered her heart: “Yes, we should give up our nonsense.”  
”Everything, promise?”  
”Yes, everything.” We slept.

On the following day we continued to read the other books, chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, and listen to your grave voice singing: “*Cintāmaṇi.*..” Your drum started to speed up: “*Tere-kheta-tere-kheta* – surrender, surrender.”

It was the end of September ’72 when we rolled up our sleeping bags, packed our few belongings into two shoulder bags, and headed for the temple. With our little savings we got two one-way tickets to Paris. We got off at the Paris Gare de Lion, floating in nectar. We got into the metro; when we changed to a different line, and finally we got to the station named Fontaney o Rose. We asked directions and learned that the temple was very near – three or four blocks. We walked fast. When we rang the bell, a shaven-headed, smiling devotee appeared: “Hare Kṛṣṇa,” he said. “Please come in.”

I entered the temple room, went to the *vyāsāsana,* and fell like a stick: “Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.” In my heart I felt, “I have finally made it home.”

Thank you, thank you, for beating you drum, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please go on beating it, and when needed, please beat my head.

Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda! Go on beating, go on beating! You are saving the whole world.

Please always maintain this useless soul at your lotus feet, and despite the fact that I am such a fool, please engage me in continually carrying to everybody the sound of your sweet drum.

*A useless servant at your lotus feet,*  
Somaka Swami

## Suhotra Swami

**It’s not enough**

It’s not enough to eulogize, emotionalize, and with wet eyes  
Apologize for all the tries that didn’t quite materialize.

It’s not enough to theorize, fantasize, and finally minimize  
What Prabhupāda institutionalized to make us wise and realized.

It’s not enough to, eagle-eyed, legal-tie and constitutionalize,  
Proclaiming, “Spontaneity must be sanitized, sterilized and  
classified.”

It’s not enough to be weary-wise, bleary-eyed, on the dreary side,  
And shrug my shoulders and take no sides when another God brother  
glides down the slide.

It’s not enough to find out why, expose the lie, and testify,  
And politicize to get glorified, revolutionize to aggrandize.

It’s not enough to get analyzed, de-fanaticized, and normalized,  
Nationalized and rationalize, “I’m more inspired to live outside.”

It’s not enough to synthesize, harmonize, and compromise,  
And justify with the alibi that we need allies to increase our size.

It’s not enough to be internalized, self-hypnotized, and petrified,  
and quietly hide in a bubble of pride, opposition safely neutralized.

It’s not enough to be mystified, Christified, and blisstified,  
An acolyte who’s quite contrite, a glowing mite in Kali’s night.

It’s kind of tough cause I’m so demonized, but I’ll make my try  
to be utilized  
By Prabhupāda, my eternal guide, and leave aside these unfortunate  
plights.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisance at your lotus feet, and thank you for giving me shelter and service at your lotus feet for one more year of my life in this body. I pray that I may not become attracted to any position, conception, ideology or activity that deviates from this shelter.

As my spiritual master, and as founder-ācārya of ISKCON, you so mercifully make yourself readily available to those, like myself, who are not Kṛṣṇa conscious but who cannot bear the thought of further entanglement in the material whirlpool. I really whish I was a better disciple; my problem is that I am so very lazy and immature, but I am most grateful to you for keeping me in the incubator of the shade of your lotus feet so that I might gradually develop enthusiasm to serve you.

You once remarked, “Think how busy Kṛṣṇa is. He is in the heart of every living entity. And yet, Kṛṣṇa is with Rādhārāṇī and all the *gopīs* simultaneously with His queens in Dvārakā. This is God.” You are the supreme personality of servitor Godhead; you are completely busy eternally in Lord Kṛṣṇa’s service – like Kṛṣṇa, on many levels and in many places simultaneously. Thus you are always busy with your ISKCON movement even now, though I’m not able to see you directly. I hope that someday I can cast off this lethargy that dogs me and, with your kind permission and assistance, render some substantial service. Really, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to fight for you and thus contribute in some way to your glories, as you fought for your spiritual master and glorified him throughout the world. If I could simply die in battle for you, bleeding, gasping, exhausted, but with your name on my lips, I would finally have confidence that I have developed real love for you.

Even when you were physically present before our eyes, I did not have much opportunity to directly see and serve you; I take that as your kindness also, because I was so immature then that I would have made offenses. Apart from a few personal meetings with you (which I treasure in my heart as *sūtras* codifying the full meaning of the disciple’s eternal connection with his spiritual master), I have served you in separation, guided by your literatures and lectures, and also by your representatives, your disciples, who are like *śikṣā*- and *vartma-pradārśika-gurus* to me. Though I certainly consider myself unfortunate for not having had more personal association with Your Divine Grace, in another way I find that the way I served you when you were among us prepared me for the way we all are serving you now.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it distresses me how so many of my God brothers, who did so much substantial service and who were personally favored by you in so many ways, and who were thus most respectable for a distant “peon” like me, have fallen into various traps of *māyā,* some of which I’ve indicated in the above poem. But this poem is not meant to criticize them; in a way, they are still teaching me, for it is by their grace that I can see the dangers of these traps of illusion. Thus I am praying that you please guide me in such a way that I may not also become victimized. I am sure that, despite whatever difficulties my wayward God brothers may have come to, in the end they will all be rectified by Kṛṣṇa at your pleas.

I am praying for this help because it appears that I have yet so far to go before I can shake off all the stubborn contaminations that plague my heart. I continually need your protection. I deeply yearn for the day when I can begin to repay you for all you’ve done for me. It’s not enough, but it’s all I can do for now.

*From an embryonic servant of the servants of Śrīla Prabhupāda,*  
Sohotra Swami

## Vipramukhya Swami

**It’s That Time of Year**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

At writing poetry I’m not very good;  
At anything for you I’m just no good.  
You are our shelter, you are our hope,  
You are the one who took us off dope.

Save me, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Save me, Gurudeva!  
I’m lost and confused and I’m crying some, too.  
It doesn’t even rhyme and I don’t care a bit,  
But if I don’t get some mercy, I’m gonna have a fit.

I’m so fallen and wretched and degraded and low;  
I try to hide away, but with you I will show.  
I have no value, no knowledge, no hope.  
Only with you can I have any scope.

It’s that time again, when words fail,  
And all our past works seems to no avail.  
It’s that time again, when many a great soul  
Will come together to glorify your role.

It’s that time again, when tears will be shed  
By grown men whom you all led.  
It’s that time of year when I start to shake  
As I see grown men break down – no fake.

It’s that time of year that I witness with awe  
The great love that my God brothers saw.  
It’s that time of year when that I try to catch  
The love they feel, which I can’t match.

It’s that time of year when a great abyss  
Of emptiness haunts us as you we miss.  
It’s that time of year when we stay up all night,  
Yet come in the morning – what a fight.

It’s when great men come in this time of year,  
Chant your glories – have no fear.  
Have you come to this world to save all  
But leave this poor wretch to rot and fall?

Have you given your mercy to those who served,  
But not cast your glance towards me, undeserved?  
Will you call all your disciples to be with you,  
But forget this one, for he was so new?

O Śrīla Prabhupāda! My heart breaks apart!  
Help me to serve you and with Māyā to part.  
This Vipramukhya weeps in the dust of your feet,  
But with serving you I will never retreat.

*Your wretched disciple,*  
Vipramukhya Swami

## Śrī Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Abeokuta

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisance at your lotus feet on this most blessed occasion of your appearance in this mortal world.

Just for the sake of delivering the conditioned souls, you took the trouble to come down.

Just for our sake, you bore the pains and botherations of material existence.

Just for our sake, you forsook the comforts and peaceful atmosphere of Vṛndāvana.

Just for our sake, you left home, wife, children, and relatives.

And just for our sake, you spent sleepless nights writing transcendental literatures, which are now the savior of the conditioned souls.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can’t enumerate the sacrifices you made on our behalf. By these sacrifices we are learning devotional service to Kṛṣṇa. And by your sacrifices we are becoming real human beings.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are so much indebted to you. We can’t glorify you enough, for your greatness is unlimited. You took the whole world by surprise, launching a worldwide spiritual movement at the age of seventy and achieving a feat no one had ever achieved before or will ever achieve in the future.

In one of your lectures you mention that the *śakty-āveṣa avatāras* are known by their extraordinary potency and great achievements. By examining your activities, one cannot but conclude that the following statement holds true for you: “You are a very empowered incarnation.”

The scholars who are appreciating your greatness say of you: “To say that he was ‘one in a million’ is an understatement.” Indeed, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are one in a hundred million or hundred billion. In fact, our conclusion is that your greatness cannot be measured.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your grand disciples in Abeokuta, Nigeria,

Guru dāsa / Parameśvara dāsa / Syamantaka dāsa / Abhaya-caraṇāravinda dāsa / Caraṇāravinda dāsa / Vāmanadeva dāsa / Bhakta Moses / Bhakta Shankara / Bhakta Joel / Bhakta Francis / Bhakta Douglas / Bhakta Lawrence / Bhakta Christopher / Bhakta Okon / Bhakta Ferri / Bhakta George / Bhakta Boniface

## Amsterdam

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitam yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

All glories to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who has carefully elucidated the most comprehensive instructions on the science of Śrī Śrī Rādha and Kṛṣṇa! His words are pure liquid nectar just meant for my parched, desert like heart. He is nondifferent from his instructions, and he is ever conscious of his disciples’ progress. With extreme humility and reverence we offer our respectful obeisance unto his lotus feet, which are the shelter for the countless souls of Kali seeking peace from the storm.

O blessed hero! You are friend and guide. What fortune is it that gives one your touchstone association? O loving patriarch, O ocean of sweet qualities, please show us your compassion. Guide us to the far shore of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti.* That rarest of shelters is so far away; who but you can captain this precious human vessel to the safe harbor of Govinda’s lotus feet. In desperation, with extreme humility and reverence, we offer our respectful obeisance unto your lotus feet, which are the shelter for the bitter, wandering souls of Kali seeking peace from the storm.

Jagat-guru! Jagat-guru! Jagat-guru! We pray that your gentle countenance be firmly fixed within our minds. Your lotus form reflects that golden hue of Nīlācala-candra; by seeing you, we remember Him. O potent envoy of Śrī Nitāi-Gaura, catch us up in Their *bhakti* net. Deliver us and protect us. Your clear instructions leave no room for doubt. O master, may we take your loving directions and thus become free from the ghosts of unsatisfying speculations. By your blessing we will gain a taste for chanting the Lord’s holy names. O abundant and merciful rain cloud of compassion, shower us! Inundate us! Overwhelm us with a desire to chant offenselessly! With outstretched arms, falling like a rod with extreme humility and reverence, we offer our respectful obeisance unto your lotus feet, which are the shelter for the confounded souls of Kali seeking peace from the storm.

In any history, who is your equal! There is none. Nor will there ever be such as you! O emissary of effulgent Nityānanda! Following His lead, you place in our vacant hearts a wish to succeed: “Go on chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare! Who could imagine that, possessed of so many disqualifications, I, the lowest of vile sinners, would be taken as your disciple? O servant of the servant of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, did you see me as representing his lamentations for the lost souls of Kali? I thank you countless millions of millions of times for letting me be the fool redeemed. Let every man and woman desirous of good fortune know that a small glance laden with your tender mercy will invest them with the highest of good fortune. We pray to be counted among the millions you will save. With extreme humility and reverence we offer our respectful obeisance unto your lotus feet, which are the shelter for the repentant souls of Kali seeking peace from the storm.

How much are we in awe of your unassailable stature? When will our minds find composure from your stunning achievements? Just once more may we offer you a seat, *madhuparka,* and a soothing foot wash? Take our offerings of garlands while we fan you with peacock fan and *cāmara*. May we just once more crave the *candana* remnants from you regal brow or covet with greedy passion some *prasādam* morsel that has touched your lips? Let us please, O mighty *guru*, sit just one more time at your feet to hear your expositions of the highest truth, spoken from the perfect platform of realization. To hear those sweet phrases again and again, we would accept any extreme condition! Śrīla Prabhupāda, could we hear just one more time your gentle request to share your teachings with others? Could we again feel the pride of showing others the writings of our perfect guru – the likes of whom no one has ever dreamed? What o joyous day it would be to invite some curious guest or ardent seeker to sit at your feet to hear you sing, “*Vande ‘haṁ śrī-guruḥ...”* May I die young, before insistent old age robs me of these remembrances, as sweet as a mild lotus-scented breeze. With extreme humility and reverence, meditating on your spotless remembrance, we offer our respectful obeisance unto your lotus feet, which are the shelter for the forgetful souls of Kali seeking peace from the storm.

O champion son of Gaura-deśa, O dearest inmate of the Radha-Damodara *mandira,* O resolute mariner of Śrī *Jaladuta,* O pristine charitable Vaiṣṇava, O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help us to hear you! Continue to be our guide and our soul and bless us to “simply follow without changing anything.” Do not neglect a dependent waif such as me, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Show us your mercy. Continue to miracle by making this sinner steady in spiritual life. In doing so, convince those ever more qualified than me of your continued ever-presence. With extreme humility and reverence, desirous for the dust of your lotus feet, we offer our respectful obeisance unto your lotus feet, which are the only shelter for the hopeful souls of Kali seeking peace from the storm.

(written by Bhūdhara dāsa)

*Your servants in Kṛṣṇa-dhāma,*

Bhadrāṅga dāsa / Brahmarāta dāsa / Bhūdhara dāsa / Gaura-Gadādhara dāsa / Gaurāṅga-prema dāsa / Naṭavara dāsa / Nirāmaya dāsa / Rasācārya dāsa / Śāstra-siddhi dāsa / Vātsalya dāsa / Bhakta Albert / Bhakta George / Bhakta Jarek / Bhakta John / Bhakta Stephan / Bhakta Thillai / Bhaja Rādhe-devī dāsī / Paṇḍitā-devī dāsī / Rādhā-Gopīnātha-devī dāsī / Sarasvatī-devī dāsī / Vṛndāvana-tulaṣi-devī dāsī / Yadurāṇī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Carla / Bhaktin Cecile / Bhaktin Claudette / Bhaktin Flor / Bhaktin Lucette / Bhaktin Thea
