## Copenhagen

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept our humble obeisance prostrated in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Lord Gaurāṅga predicted that the holy name would be spread to every town and village. Some took prediction allegorically, but you set out to fulfill His mission. Lord Caitanya must have had you in mind when He made that statement. All glories, all glories to Your Divine Grace, the bestower of Gaurāṅga’s grace!

Śrīla Vyāsadeva wrote about literatures that are improperly composed but yet cause a revolution within the hearts of men. Your mother tongue was Bengali, and yet you translated transcendental literatures from Sanskrit into English. That *Bhāgavatam* verse seems to predict your mission. The transcendental glories of the Lord you profusely churned, with millions of millions of more glories to come, the revolution in the hearts of men has begun. All glories, all glories to Your Divine Grace, the deliverer of Vyāsa’s cream!

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, from his house, saw a light shining from Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s place. He foresaw that a transcendental city would appear, where English, American, French, and Russian devotees would chant “Jaya Śacīnandana!” with their Bengali brothers and dance with upraised hands. By your desire and the sincere efforts of your followers, that transcendental city will certainly rise to fulfill Śrīla Bhaktivinoda’s vision. All glories, all glories to Your Divine Grace for instilling in us that lifelong mission!

Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, your divine master, gave you the mission to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the English-speaking world. You brought it to America to fulfill that order, and from there you spread it all over the world, to every continent. “If you ever get money, print books,” said your spiritual master. Those words deeply entered your heart, and you set out to flood the world with transcendental literatures. All glories, all glories to Your Divine Grace, the foremost and dearmost servant of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, who once predicted, “When the time comes, he will do everything himself.”

You are the great personality predicted by previous *ācāryas* and the scriptures, the one who will bring hope to the desolate inhabitants of this dark dungeon called Kali-yuga. To simply declare your divinity, however, does not do you full credit. Your life was full of struggles to establish the mission of the Lord. Your unflinching faith in and devotion to your Guru Mahārāja and your constant endeavor and intense desire to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness serve as illustrious examples for us who are on the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You never claimed that your success was in your being a *śaktyāveśa-avatāra.* Rather, you said that you never expected all your successes: “I am just trying to fulfill the order of my spiritual master.” This is your exemplary teaching. There is no doubt you are that empowered personality predicted by the previous *ācāryas,* but your life is full of examples to teach and guide us in our humble attempts to serve you.

Every day we can gain deep inspiration by remembering how you struggled alone in India to print and distribute Back to Godhead magazines and translate and print Fist Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, how you came to America and suffered two heart attacks on the way, how you went through hardships the first year without any success, how you checked with the Scindia Steamship Company to find out when the boat would be returning to India, how you considered going back but kept on struggling, trying to fulfill the order of your spiritual master. There are unlimited examples in your life to learn from and emulate, to give us strength and inspiration. You are certainly the empowered devotee descending from the spiritual world, but your endeavors, determination, humility, devotion, etc., are very real, and they are something we can learn to follow.

We pray that we may not fall into the trap of laziness by claiming, “Prabhupāda can do what he did because he was an empowered devotee sent by Kṛṣṇa to fulfill His mission. But we’re not Prabhupāda, so we can’t do anything.” Rather, let us remember and emulate your very exemplary life and accept the challenge to follow in your footsteps. May you please bless us with your mercy.

*Begging to remain faithfully your humble servants,*

Cirañjīva dāsa / Daśāvatāra dāsa / Jaya Nṛsiṁha dāsa / Kaṁsahantā dāsa / Lalitānātha dāsa / Laulyajit dāsa / Manuśreṣṭha dāsa / Nārāyaṇa dāsa / Parajñāna dāsa / Trayūmaya dāsa / Utkala dāsa / Yadunandana dāsa / Bhakta Jan / Bhakta Lars / Bhakta Martin / Bhakta Peer / Bhakta Robbie / Bhakta Soren / Aprameyā-devī dāsī / Ayatī-devī dāsī / Candrāvalī-devī dāsī / Kaṁsāri-devī dāsī / Mahimā-bhūṣaṇā-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Nicolai / Bhaktin Sonya

## Costa Rica

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisance at your divine lotus feet.

It is very difficult for us to address such a great personality as yourself. But casting aside all inhibitions in our heart, we want to express our feelings of awe and respect.

You are our *parama-guru,* our eternal master for the eternity of your teachings and for the eternity of our gratitude. By your marvelous books we were able to awaken from illusion and give up ignorance. By your divine teachings we have attained the freedom to be able to serve Kṛṣṇa.

Although we never met your personally, you are always present in our heart as the permanent light by which we understand the real nature of things. Your untiring enthusiasm for distributing Kṛṣṇa consciousness around the world is our source of inspiration. The entire world is suffering due to lack of spiritual knowledge and faith in Lord Kṛṣṇa. Anyone who reads your books carefully will automatically receive that knowledge and develop all the best qualities.

Your words are transcendental vehicles by which the Supreme Absolute Truth, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, has been pleased to come to us. We feel indebted to you for your kindly saving us.

This country is filled with obstacles to the spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, so it is difficult to offer you acceptable results from our preaching work. Therefore we invoke your blessings so that we may not become discouraged and may be able to follow your example of perseverance and seriousness.

*Your humble servants in Costa Rica,*

Gopīnātha dāsa / Caitanyadeva dāsa / Yamunā-jīvana dāsa / Jagannātha dāsa / Viṣṇu dāsa / Ṛṣabha dāsa / Dhana dāsa / Jaya dāsa / Nārada Muni dāsa / Maheśvara dāsa / Sad-vigraha dāsa / Bhakta Andre / Rādhā-Govinda-devī dāsī / Līlā-śakti-devī dāsī / Indranīlā-devī dāsī / Sītā-devī dāsī / Murāri-devī dāsī / Devahūtī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Dolores / Bhaktin Judith

## Czechoslovakia

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisance at your lotus feet.

You so mercifully delivered to us your books, which are full of real life. If one reads your books, he will find a storehouse of transcendental literature. Even people who do not understand the philosophy can glorify your work. In our country, where it is still difficult to come in regular contact with your representatives, it is possible to receive instruction from your books and start to develop love for Lord Kṛṣṇa. One man who saw the transformation of a person practicing just a little bit of Kṛṣṇa consciousness admitted with full wonder, “What is this book after reading which one will completely change one’s life? What is this wonderful book?” Even if he did not understand much about our philosophy, he was impressed and admired what you have done.

From our low position, it is almost an offense to try to glorify you, since we can hardly perceive your real greatness. Your glory is beyond our conception, but we can clearly see your pure compassion, because of which you are so kindly delivering to us the teachings of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Thank you for saving us from religions and philosophies that bewildered the poor people of Kali-yuga. Thank you for engaging us in devotional service and giving us the nectar of life. Thank you for giving us the directions and inspirations contained in your writings. Thank you over and over for staying with us through your books.

You are giving us so much that it will be impossible to repay our debt. If we, however, try our best to give your matchless gifts to others in this country, we hope we will please you and that you will shower upon us even more of your mercy, which will give us more strength to continue.

*Your servants in the Czechoslovakian yātrā,*

Turiya dāsa / Alica / Fredy / Karel / Lubos 1 / Lubos 2 / Mirka / Peter / Radek / Roman / Tomas / Visra

## Dallas

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisance at your divine lotus feet. All glories to your wonderful, transcendental books, which are bringing to this darkened world the light of true knowledge. All glories to your wonderful, transcendental pastimes, which bring joy into the hearts of all those who read about them.

We offer our humble obeisance unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for you alone in this world are worthy of them. Unto you, a most humble and perfect servant of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, we offer all respect. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have come to us garbed as a wandering mendicant, but instead of begging something from us, you filled our lives with a wealth of knowledge and virtue. Your words, so sweetly spoken, give meaning and purpose to our misdirected lives. Because your words are filled with beauty, grace, and wisdom, they encourage and arouse within us a desire to serve you beyond our own abilities and personal ambitions. They turn us away from a sinful path, placing us on the road to spiritual perfection. Grant us the ability and courage to continue to receive your words faithfully and gratefully into our hearts, for without them the substance of progressive human life will be lost. Because you are the founder*-ācārya* of this great spiritual movement called ISKCON, your words are law.

We offer our humble obeisance unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and pray to always remain at your lotus feet. You are eternally situated as a pure associate of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, dedicating your life to fulfilling His desire to have the Hare Kṛṣṇa mahā-mantra chanted in every town and village. You are freely giving us the opportunity to reestablish our eternal relationship with Kṛṣṇa, which is filled with eternity, knowledge, and bliss. How shall we serve you? We humbly pray and beg for your instructions. How shall we lay down our lives for you? Let us bear all tribulations if doing so increases our service to you. Let us be proper in all thoughts, words, and deeds so that by properly representing you, your glories will become more and more expanded in human society. How sinful we must be that we do not yearn more ardently to surrender our hearts unto you.

Unto you, most dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, the most surrendered servant of His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, we offer our most humble obeisance. It was you who came to us in 1965, drenching our thirsty souls with Kṛṣṇa’s wonderful pastimes. You gave us yourself, and by doing so you gave us Kṛṣṇa and the whole spiritual world. We beg and pray without cessation to be able to serve you without deviation, lifetime after lifetime. Your books are the stepping stones back home, back to Godhead, for all mankind for the next ten thousand years. Your charity floods the hearts of all living entities in this universe, therefore let your glories be continuously sung and preached to all who have ears. Your humility commands the hearts of mighty men, and your virtues are standards for all generations to follow. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, perpetual praise is insufficient reciprocation for your kindness towards us. Therefore allow us to find no peace until we have glorified your name and pastimes to all people in this universe.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us permission to offer unto your great self our humble obeisance as long as we have the strength to do so. A long duration of life and vast material opulence prove to be enemies if not employed in your service. You are our life and strength. Through your desire we may come to know Kṛṣṇa. You are the cause of all success that may come our way. By your consolation, our troubled hearts are given freedom. You’ve lifted us from the bondage of material happiness and distress. How is that we do not shed tears of gratitude for all you’ve done? And surely none of us could honestly say that we were qualified to receive your unmotivated blessings. By you, our faith in Kṛṣṇa was made firm and our hope strengthened. By you, temptations are conquered and evil passions subdued. You invited us into your house, in which the whole world can live. Let us know no other pleasure than that pleasure which comes from serving you. By your causeless mercy alone are we privileged to be called “servants.” Your goodness exceeds the total goodness of all this world, and therefore your name is worthy of all praise. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have truly fulfilled the meanings of “spiritual master.” Let us now, as your menial disciples and grand disciples, learn and fulfill the meaning of “servant.”

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, most beloved spiritual master, kindly accept our humble respects. Grant us the ability to feel and express our heartfelt gratitude for all that you have kindly bestowed upon us. What reverence, honor, and praise are due you, let we sit idle, lacking enthusiasm. Twelve years ago you passed beyond our vision, but as you did you entered deeply into the hearts of your surrendered disciples, securing an inerasable position in the core of their lives. New men and women have come since then, your grand disciples, and more are still coming. We join together, hearing your instructions on tapes and in your books. Even today, though not physically present, you have the potency to inspire the conditioned souls to take up spiritual life. Please accept our lives as instruments in your great preaching work.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, glorious Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept this humble offering, whose words fall short of expressing and glorifying your ever-increasingly wonderful activities and virtues. We pray to continually realize that we have a long way to go before we become your perfect servants. With all affection of heart that a disciple may be able to offer let us now offer unto you our deepest awe and veneration on this most holy of days, your appearance day.

(written by Jatāyu dāsa)

*Your humble servants at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kālacandjī-dhāma.*

## Denver

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisance at your lotus feet. All glories to your beloved appearance day.

Almost ten years after your arrival in the U.S.A., you arrived in Denver. At that time, you spoke from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* Sixth Canto. Now, in 1989, we need to hear those instructions again and realize that you are always with us at every moment:

”So, the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is giving this benefit to the human society. It is very easy, not difficult, without any condition. You simply chant and remember Kṛṣṇa. It is not at all difficult. Everyone can. Here you’re seeing the Deity of Kṛṣṇa. So, while chanting, if you remember this Deity, is it very difficult? We are opening these centers, giving this education, and from *śāstras* all information is given. So you take advantage of it. Don’t spoil life... And in this life we can hear about Kṛṣṇa, think of Kṛṣṇa, and see Kṛṣṇa’s Deity. So many opportunities, and if you take this opportunity you will become accustomed to think about Kṛṣṇa... So, Kṛṣṇa has very kindly agreed to come to your city, Denver city, and this temple. Take advantage. Come daily. See Kṛṣṇa. Think of Kṛṣṇa. Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. Take Kṛṣṇa’s *prasād*am and be happy. That is the Vedic mission... That is Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission – to do other’s something auspicious... So, if we go on with this habit – Kṛṣṇa consciousness – then our position is very secure. Keep yourself in that secure position very rigidly; then your life is successful. Thank you very much.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are trying to carry on in such a way that will please you, by always distributing the holy names, *prasādam,* and your books. Our mood is to try “to do other’s something auspicious.” We pray that we can act on the desire to follow your temple programs, to worship the Deities as purely as possible, and to distribute more books. Please help us to become humble enough to always beg for your mercy to accomplish these goals. We want to desire your pleasure and satisfaction, despite the fact that we are falling short of your expectations. Please empower us to push on your mission the way you want it to be done.

Thank you for coming to Denver then, and may we always live in such a way that you are always with us. You live forever in your books. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your servants at ISKCON Denver.*

## Detroit

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this most significant day of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā, celebrating your appearance, we have no qualifications to describe your glories, but simply by looking around this temple and the preaching project of Devasadan Mandir here in Detroit, we cannot help but recognize your potency, wisdom, spiritual vision, and the many blessings you have given us. After all, only by your mercy do we now have such a facility as The Fisher Mansion to help spread the message of our dear Lord Kṛṣṇa.

When you originally wanted to purchase this building, no one else could understand why, and we were reluctant to favor the idea. Yet you said it would be a perfect facility for combining the spiritual knowledge of the Vedas with the material advancement of the West. Now we can see that this has actually been accomplished. So many people regularly visit this preaching project who otherwise would never even consider visiting a temple of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. And many times they say that they like visiting this mansion much better than any of the others in the Detroit area. While they are here, they also get the opportunity to hear the message of Lord Kṛṣṇa through your books that we distribute and have on display, as well as by experiencing the Kṛṣṇa conscious diorama and multimedia exhibit that you long ago wanted to build to fulfill the desires of your own spiritual master.

Visitors also like and praise the taste of the *prasādam* they get at Govinda’s restaurant in the luxurious dining room. Across the hall from the restaurant, they often have the opportunity to look into the temple room and see the beautiful Deities of Śrī Śrī Gaura- Nitāi, Śrī Śrī Rādhā Kuñjabihārī, and Lord Jagannātha, Lord Balarāma, and Lady Subhadrā. On tours of the mansion, visitors also hear about your pastimes of how you translated many important spiritual books and how you came to America with practically no money but yet, by Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, were able to establish such nice temples as this mansion. Where else can the conditioned souls enjoy such a barrage of spiritual experiences? Indeed, many visitors have described it as an oasis in the middle of hell.

We also have to remember that at the time when you wanted to purchase this building, many devotees thought that the neighborhood was so decrepit and dirty that no one would want to visit. You predicted that as soon as we brought the Deities here, the condition of the neighborhood would begin to improve. Though the devotees may have doubted you at first, we have seen that the neighborhood has improved so much that they are building new town houses all around us, with Devasadana Mandira as the jewel in the center of it all.

You also originally instructed the devotees to distribute *prasādam* to the local people, and that has also been established with a growing Hare Krishna Food for Life program that has gained much respect and popularity with the people of the area. You also wanted the devotees to build a dock on the canal in back of the temple so that people could sail in from the Detroit River and dock their boats and come in for some tasty *prasādam.* This summer that will also become manifest.

So, what does all this prove? It explicitly shows how very rare and special a person you are. Though we could not at first see the possibilities of this project, you saw it all. It also shows that though it may seem difficult to carry out all of your instructions now that we no longer have your personal association and guidance the way we did a few years ago, still, we are sincere and continue to work together and cooperate by following your orders, in spite of whatever attacks or distractions māyā may try to give us, in the end success is guaranteed. Therefore let us never forget the personal sacrifice you made in order to bring Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the West, and let us pray that you always allow us to remain in your service.

The only way we can ever begin to slightly repay you is to beg to always remain humble servants of your lotus feet and to continue to carry out your instructions. Then not only will we benefit, but also anyone who comes in contact with this project will make untold spiritual advancement.

(written by Śrī Nandanandana dāsa)

Your humble servants at Devasadana Mandira, ISKCON Detroit,

Ambarīṣa dāsa / Svāhā-devī dāsī, Amṛta / Ādi-rāja dāsa, Mahā-medhā-devī dāsī / Atulānanda dāsa / Ayodhyā dāsa, Kṛṣṇa-kathā-devī dāsī / Bhagavad-gītā dāsa, Kālindī-devī dāsī, Vanamālī / Bhakti-latā-devī dāsī / Bhaumadeva dāsa, Bhāvanī-devī dāsī, Gītā, Govinda, Varuṇī / Bhūśāya dāsa, Lekhaśravantī-devī dāsī, Prahlāda, Mohinī, Rāma / Caitanya-rūpa / Dakṣiṇa dāsa, Dhūmrakeśā-devī dāsī, Dhirodatta / Dean, Karuṇikā-devī dāsī / Gopī-kānta dāsa, Bhava-bhāvanī-devī dāsī, Sumaṅgala, Rādhikā / Gopikā-suta dāsa, Rāma / Ikṣvāku dāsa / Indrabhart dāsa, Vaikuṇṭha-sundarī-devī dāsī, Kiśorī-priya, Prema-bhakti, Rādhikā / Jagat-guru dāsa, Puru / Jaya dāsa / Kalalapa dāsa, Sarasvatī-devī dāsī, Rūpa-mañjarī / Kamlesh, Promila, Anjan, Asu / Kiśora-kiśorī and family / Lokanātha dāsa, Diane, Ānanda, Cintāmaṇi / Kṛṣṇa-kathānanda dāsa, Sarva / Kṛṣṇa-darśana dāsa / Lālasamayī-devī dāsī / Līlānanda dāsa, Kānta, Abhay Charan, Hṛdayānanda, Līlā-rāja / Mukunda dāsa / Nāma-rūpa dāsa / Pañca-tattva dāsa, Yauvananda, Caraṇam / Paṇḍu dāsa / Prabhānu dāsa, Pañcajanī-devī dāsī, Gaurāṅga / Prabhāsa dāsa, Viśālākṣi-devī dāsī / Pratyatoṣa dāsa, Urmilā-devī dāsī, Mādhava, Yamunā, Keśava / Rādhā-sundarī-devī dāsī / Rāma-rāja dāsa / Ravi dāsa, Yaśogamī-devī dāsī, Śakuntalā, Nārāyaṇa / Sudhīra dāsa / Sukṛti dāsa / Surabhi dāsa / Śravaṇānanda dāsa, Ānanda-gopī-devī dāsī / Triśakti dāsa / Udayānanda dāsa and family / Vamśi dāsa, Brenda, Janakī / Viśālakṣa dāsa / Vṛndāpati dāsa, Kāryātīṭa-devī dāsī, Baladeva, Amṛta / Bhakta Barrie / Bhakta Bob Roberts / Bhakta Bob and Bhaktin Cheryl / Bhaktin Deborah and Stephanie / Bhakta Eric / Bhakta John and Bhaktin Colleen / Bhaktin Kathy / Bhaktin Kim / Bhakta Tim

## Durban

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept our humble obeisance at your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Trying to offer you suitable words of praise is seemingly impossible, for it is very difficult to express in English what you mean to us. But still, as Vaiṣṇava tradition dictates, an attempt has been made.

It appears from scripture that even to take birth as a human being on this particular planet is rare: *aho amīṣāṁ kim akāri śobhaṁ / prasanna eṣāṁ svid uta svayaṁ hariḥ.* “Since the human life is the sublime position for spiritual realization, all the demigods in heaven speak in this way: ‘How wonderful it is for these human beings to have been born in the land of Bhārata-varṣa. They must have executed pious acts of austerity in the past, or the Supreme Personality of Godhead Himself must have been pleased with them. Otherwise, how could they engage in devotional service in so many ways? We demigods can only aspire to achieve human births in Bhārata-varṣa to execute devotional service, but these human beings are already engaged there.” (*Bhāg.* 5.19.21)

Later on having had the good fortune of taking birth on this earth planet, we are even more fortunate in that we have taken birth a human birth. Certainly we must be counted amongst the lucky few. But wait – even more good fortune awaits us: *yo ‘sau bhagavati sarva-bhūtātmany anātmye ‘nirukte ‘nilayane paramātmani vāsudeve.* “After many, many births, when the results of one’s pious activities mature, one gets an opportunity to associate with pure devotees. Then one is able to cut the knot of bondage to ignorance, which bound him because of varied fruitive activities.” (*Bhāg*. 5.19.20) And as stated elsewhere in the *Bhagavatam* (1.18.13), *tulayāma lavenāpi na svargaṁ nāpunar-bhavam / bhagavat-saṅgi-saṅgasya martyānāṁ kim utāśiṣaḥ.* “The value of a moment’s association with the devotee of the Lord cannot be compared to the achievement of heavenly planets or liberation from matter, and what to speak of worldly benedictions in the form of material prosperity, which are for those who are meant for death.”

Somehow we are getting the association of a pure devotee in the person of your good self. How is this possible? What must we have done to achieve this? But there is yet more *bhāgya:* You have graciously agreed to accept us as your disciples, and thus we are eternally connected with you through the *paramparā* (disciplic succession).

We find ourselves connected to the personal, confidential representative of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. You are in every respect the representative of all the demigods (*sarva-devamayo guruḥ*). Then, to allow us to maintain and increase our *bhāgya,* you have created a transcendental society, a family of Vaiṣṇavas. Your *guru-mahārāja* referred to such a society as the “Śṛi Viśva Vaiṣṇava-rāja-sabhā,” a society composed of the foremost Vaiṣṇavas in the world.

Somehow or other we forgotten and misguided souls have been elevated to the position of associating with you. After having wandered throughout this universe for trillions of lifetimes, we are now on the threshold of actually going back to Godhead. Within our grasp, by your mercy, we can very shortly see the Supreme Lord.

You write in a purport, “If one meets a saintly person completely engaged in transcendental duties and achieves his favor, then one’s life mission becomes complete. What is not possible to achieve in thousands of lives can be achieved in one moment if there is an opportunity to meet a saintly person.” (*Bhāg*. 3.22.5, purport)

With all this mercy, our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, how are we repaying you? What are we doing to reciprocate with you?

To be honest, we are simply pursuing *upśākhā-gaṇas,* the unwanted creepers of *kuṭi-nāṭi* (diplomacy), *jīva-hiṁsā* (envy of other living entities), pūjā (adoration achieved by satisfying mundane people), and *pratiṣṭhāśā* (desire for name, fame, and high position).

Our wicked minds (*duṣṭa-mana*) are forcing us *jīva* souls to be unfortunate. In all humility we beg that you may forgive us for being so ungrateful and self-centered. We pray that one day in the future we may at least begin to honor the eternal debt that we owe you and become responsible disciples and followers of Your Divine Grace.

For years we struggled to get you a visa;  
Finally the government relented.  
There were only six devotees and  
A single-story house we had rented.

From the moment you came you preached like a lion  
To Boers, whites, and blacks – hearts changed from iron.

*Intimate morning walks on the golf course,*  
*Talks of India before the war.*  
*We organized seven large programs, and you said,*  
”I’ll stay longer if you organize more.”

In a Durban meeting with professors and leaders,  
You said a temple would be nice.  
Ten years and two million dollars later  
It is opened. Does it suffice?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you spent three weeks in this forgotten place,  
But now more than ever we need Your Divine Grace.

Māyā is attacking, in this world  
We are put to test and trial.  
Ambition and party spirit, exploiting  
The body, which is nothing but bile.

In the soothing rays of your presence  
Such misty illusions disappear.  
Guide us, direct us, and instruct us, please,  
We just want to hear.

(written by Pārtha-sārathi dāsa)

*Your servants in Durban, South Africa,*

## Edinburgh

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our worthless obeisance in the dust of your lotus feet. Please be kind to us and look over our humble offering.

You came to the West just a few years ago  
To give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to us lost and fallen souls.  
You are the spiritual master who extinguishes the forest fire  
Of material lust – the spell by the witch of māyā.

You came as Kṛṣṇa’s pioneer, fearless and bold,  
Giving light from the Bhāgavatam, as the scriptures foretold,  
Taking the saṅkīrtana to every town and village,  
Rescuing fallen jīvas from their senses’ rape and pillage.

”I am God! I am God!” one madman did propose;  
Roaring like a lion, you would kick on face with shoes;  
”You are dog!” In appreciation the audience arose.  
”Kṛṣṇa is God!” The imposter you exposed.

You preached, “You are eternal, blissful servants of Kṛṣṇa, God.”  
Yet reluctant and unappreciative, we’re in need of a prod.  
To please you by mass transcendental book distribution,  
The cure for Kali’s ills – the only solution.

We’re lacerated by desire for distinction and gain;  
Our inherent Kṛṣṇa consciousness is temporarily lame.  
Release from this labyrinth will come only when  
We surrender to the needful again and again.

We are lame men and have many mountains to cross,  
But in this endeavor – no diminution or loss.  
Please help us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to attain life’s only goal:  
To serve and chant the name of Kṛṣṇa, and be surrendered souls.

You did give us everything – every breath of your life  
Was selflessly directed to free us from all strife.  
Prasādam, Deity worship, and śāstra to read,  
The chanting of the holy name with which to succeed.

Although there is no question of paying back the debt  
We owe you for saving us from māyā’s entangling net,  
Please accept our service, ‘though devoid of all devotion.  
All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the ever-increasing  
merciful ocean.

Begging your causeless mercy and kindness so that we may eternally serve you to your heart’s content,  
*Your insignificant servants in Edinburgh, Scotland,*

## Fiji

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our humble obeisance at your feet. All glories to you. All glories to your cause of benedicting the world with Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Every year we are remembering this special day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, when you made your divine appearance into this material world. Throughout your life you lived in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. When the time was opportune, by Kṛṣṇa’s will you brought the message of your beloved Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is the soul of all souls, into our lives. Your position as an extraordinary representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva is unquestionable. All saintly devotees accept this fact. All honest religionists, learned philosophers, and professors cannot but acknowledge this as a fact. Therefore, each year we are not trying to establish your honored position but to collectively present your ever-increasing glories.

Disciples express their heartfelt thanks. Most cannot verbally express their full feeling, nor can they write it down. Therefore they simply surrender and quietly go on serving your order. Educationists, welfare workers, and people in general are awe-struck by your knowledge, compassion, genuine humility, and dedication to uplifting the degraded state of mankind. Any person with even a speck of intelligence who encounters you in any of your forms, such as books, audio tapes, video tapes, magnificent temples, society of devotees, etc., is forcibly attracted to you and begins to ask, “Who has done this?”

Personally, we always feel that you gave so much purpose to life and have relieved and are continuing to relieve the society at large of so much suffering. You have immersed your dedicated servants in the nectar of Lord Caitanya’s service. If someone tries to measure your works, it is not possible, nor can your work be put into quantifiable terms. Therefore the entire planet earth and even the whole universe has become indebted to you. Your worship is now a 24-hour program throughout the world. This is most benefiting, for you are actually worthy of such worship and glory. Only by Kṛṣṇa’s sanction, the appreciation of his pure devotee’s glories fill our hearts. May Kṛṣṇa allow more of you to fill our lives so that we can go on serving to greater and greater capacity for your pleasure. May your mercy be always with us in the form of ISKCON, which you have established. We remain eternally indebted to you – O our dear and beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants at ISKCON Fiji,

## Finland

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisance at your lotus feet. Those lotus feet are our only shelter within the three worlds.

We are hoping that one day we will sincerely render service to your lotus feet so that your mercy may wash away the unwanted things in our hearts. You have so often told us that our hearts can be purified only by following the simple and sublime process of chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa

But it is our stone-cold hearts that cannot perceive this sublime message. Only the fire of your preaching can burn away these obstacles. Yes, the storehouse of transcendental knowledge in your books, which you have so kindly left us, will protect us from our illusory, darkened vision covering our hearts.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are always full of compassion for the fallen, conditioned souls. We are the most fallen and need your mercy constantly, but now that we are beginning to taste the nectar from following your perfect instructions, we must also follow your perfect example.

You were always preaching while you were here in your physical form. You never stopped preaching. Now that you have left, you are turning the tide of history in a way that it has never been turned before. Your books are effulgent jewels spreading knowledge in all directions. We must become transcendental mirrors and reflect your mercy on the hearts of all the conditioned souls. You are the perfect example, never stopping for one second. Therefore your fame is spread all over the three worlds. Thus by your desire the true glories of Vedic knowledge and culture are being recognized for the first time throughout the entire universe, and we are simply running behind you. We cannot catch up, nor can we ever repay you. We are simply crying out from behind, begging for your causeless mercy.

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Dhīra-śānta dāsa

## Gainesville

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisance at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Of all the auspicious days throughout the year, the day of your appearance is the highest of all, because if you had not come to teach us the message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, then every other Vaiṣṇava festival day would have come and gone without our knowing it. But by your coming, now hundreds of thousands – actually millions – of people all over the world are engaged in celebrating the glorious pastimes of the Supreme Lord. We can understand by this that it is you whom Lord Caitanya sent to fulfill His prediction that the glories of the Lord would be heard in every town and village all over the world.

So all of your disciples and followers here in Gainsville, Florida, a place you once called “a remote corner of the world,” are praying to you to please give us your kind blessings so that we may be able to follow the instructions you so kindly gave us and to fulfill your desires to preach this Kṛṣṇa consciousness as strongly and as purely as you are.

*Your fallen servants in Gainsville, Florida.*

## Ghana

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisance in the dust of your lotus feet.

Had you not come, where would we be?  
What would have been our end?  
A struggle without purpose and  
No way we could transcend.

You gave us all the vital keys  
To open every gate,  
To leave this mundane prison and  
Transcend our dismal fate.

You trained us up as soldiers  
In the mission of the Lord,  
Where purity’s the armor  
And knowledge is the sword.

You gave our life a purpose,  
Gave us each a sacred role:  
To follow in your footsteps;  
To waken every soul.

Though Māyā shoots her weapons,  
To try to knock us back,  
We cling to your instructions to  
Stay firmly on the track.

Throughout each town and village  
We will march behind your feet,  
To share this priceless jewel you gave  
With every soul we meet.

O foremost general in Lord  
Caitanya’s force divine,  
Please guide us as your instruments  
To make this mission shine.

*Your servants in Ghana, Africa.*

## Gītā-nāgarī

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our humble obeisance in the dust of your lotus feet.

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ‘pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**”By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement. Therefore I should offer my respectful obeisance unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master.”**

When you visited Gītā-nāgarī in July 1976, you asked us “to be satisfied with simple village life.” You have already given us everything. The holy *dhāma* of Gītā-nāgarī is here, the cows are here, and your instructions are here. In your lecture of July 15, 1976, you said, “Live village life, simple life, and be satisfied with the bare necessities. There is no need of luxuries. Save time and chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. This is ideal life.”

The simple fact that you were headed here to Gītā-nāgarī *dhāma* to establish *varṇāśrama-dharma* just prior to your departing this planet in 1977 emphasizes to us your desire for us to live a simple village life based on agriculture, cow protection, and cottage industry, with Kṛṣṇa as the center. For us to live in this simple village way will please you very much.

Wherever you traveled, one of your main themes of preaching was cow protection. You have emphasized the importance of distributing your books. We are making a humble attempt to push on this important task showing how one can live a simple village life based on cow protection and centered on the pastimes and instructions of Lord Kṛṣṇa, as you have asked us to. Please forgive all our mistakes and bless us with the intelligence of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to execute your orders.

You are Kṛṣṇa’s dearmost friend and our ever well-wisher. We beg to remain eternally your menial servants.

The residents of Gītā-nāgarī village (Port Royal, Pennsylvania).

## Greece

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

O master and savior of the world! Please accept our humble obeisance at your lotus feet.

We can never thank you enough or repay Your Divine Grace for saving us from the painful materialistic life. You came into this world of distress and brought real happiness and hope. You showed to everyone the path of perfection. Your philosophy is so practical and so easy to follow when someone is simple, and at the same time difficult when he is not. But for everyone there is great hope, for to both the simple and the crooked you gave the knowledge and practical philosophy by which they can overcome all doubts and problems. You could do this because you are the viceroy of the great monarch of all the material and spiritual worlds, Śrī Kṛṣṇa

Yes, there is no doubt in any one of your disciples, grand disciples, or indeed any of those who came in contact with your lotus feet that your ISKCON movement and the sublime, pure, and practical knowledge and philosophy you gave us comprises the path of perfection for all the souls who are locked in this prison of the material world. The problem is that instead of trying to follow your instructions perfectly, we tend to forget and to interfere with our own imperfect ideas. But then we learn the hard way, from our mistakes and sufferings. Certainly you know our shortcomings and weaknesses. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you deserve pure, selfless love and devotion, but our hearts are dirty and selfish.

Spiritual life is so nice and real, but we are conditioned to so many false ideas and ways. Sometimes it seems that we will never become free from all these *anarthas.* But no, as long as do not give up the fight and keep on serving you faithfully, the Supreme Lord certainly is cleansing our hearts from within. As he promises, “As they surrender unto Me, I reward them accordingly.” Therefore all of us ISKCON devotees must learn to cooperate and work together to please you. You said many times in your letters that “personal ambition is the poison.” So please kindly and patiently keep on teaching us how to become qualified, to spread this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement in this material world. First we ourselves have to practice unmotivated, selfless devotional service, just as you have shown us with your pure example. And then, when we become more and more purified of this perverted desire for profit, adoration, and distinction, we will without a doubt see Lord Caitanya’s movement take over the whole world and millions come to take shelter. Why don’t they come now? Because our hearts are not free from selfishness. Most of us still struggle to control our minds and senses.

Please, therefore, let us learn to be humble servants of those who have been serving you steadily, progressively, without falling down, year after year, with the hope that someday we can also be free from all *anarthas* and then offer you real loving service, free from any conditioning.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us the strength and enthusiasm to go on in our service to your lotus feet without becoming discouraged or losing sight of the goal you have given us.

Please empower us to become pure preachers and to help others become free from this hellish material life.

(written by Nanda Gopa dāsa)

*Your servants in Greece,*

## Guatemala

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisance at your lotus feet. With all respect and gratitude, we offer our incompetent service on the day of your glorious appearance. Surrounded by inconveniences, we struggle day after day to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness with our meager means. Neophytes as we are, we still make many mistakes. But we are confident that by Your Divine Grace we will one day become qualified to be useful instruments in your mission of propagating Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world.

The inspiration we derive from your example is the great hope that keeps our faith alive. By reading your books, we have understood that this material world is illusion. Please give us your blessings so that we may never forget this and may have the capacity to make others understand it.

## Guyana

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisance in the dust of your divine lotus feet.

Today, on the occasion of this most blessed event, Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā, we are all gathered to offer our homages to you and sing your unlimited glories, reminding ourselves that we can truly worship Your Divine Grace only by striving constantly to serve your message – that very message which has enabled us to understand the truth of the sublime teachings of Lord Caitanya. By your mercy we have all been delivered from nescience and saved from the most dangerous type of fear – that of having to remain for lives and ages in the darkness of helpless captivity.

Your dearmost desire is that we should unite together and preach this message of Lord Caitanya by broadcasting the glories of the holy names of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa in every town and village of this land. As you once wrote to an aspiring disciple, “So, do something wonderful there in Gainsville. Wonderful means simply you chant loudly and distribute enthusiastically and sincerely; then success will be there.” The highest perfection of our lives will be to be able to give up all desires that conflict with your desires.

You have personally demonstrated how, simply by maintaining firm faith and conviction in the orders of your Guru Mahārāja, you have been successful, even beyond your own expectations, in spreading Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement all over the world. We pray that our lives be completely dedicated to the spreading of your mission. Please bless us so that we may become one-pointed in our determination to make your movement the most prominent and famous throughout the length and breadth of this land.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your servants in Guyana.*

## The Hague

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our humble obeisance at your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

For five thousand years now, Kali has been victoriously directing the universe’s affairs. Although previously he trembled, being threatened by Parīkṣit Mahārāja, today he stands proudly, claiming to be without a competitor. He doesn’t know that with the appearance of Abhay Charan, “the fearless one at the Lord’s lotus feet,” later known as His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, his power will be greatly diminished, and that for ten thousand years his position will be reduced to that of ruling a few unfortunate souls who cannot surrender to *harināma saṅkīrtana.*

For the greatest part of his life, Abhay Charan did not reveal his strength; he was disguised as a simple Vaiṣṇava pharmacist householder. But soon after he took *sannyāsa* on the instruction of his *guru-mahārāja,* Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, the real truth about his personality was revealed. He prepared his weapons in the form of books, which were filled with spiritual *śakti,* or transcendental knowledge. No ignorance or illusion, which are Kali’s *astras,* can withstand these *kṛṣṇāstras.*

At the age of seventy he proceeded to the enemy’s headquarters and recruited some of Kali’s men for his purpose. He trained them to shoot his *astras* and empowered them to maintain the golden empire after he left to conquer other places. Although he will not be physically present, for thousands of years he will continue to purify and direct the affairs on this planet through his transcendental instructions. During this time, the glories of his Lord will be heard in every nook and corner of this globe:

*Jaya Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya, Prabhu Nityānanda,*  
*Śrī Advaita, Gadādhara, Śrīvāsādi-gaura-bhakta-vṛnda*

*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare*  
*Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare*

Śrīla Prabhupāda will enter history as one who has saved the world in its darkest hour. He will be the most beloved revolutionary known. His teachings will be law, and by his mercy everyone who takes shelter of his teachings will be able to go back to the spiritual world, the kingdom of God.

Śrīla Prabhupāda-kī Jaya!

*Your servants from The Hague yātrā, The Netherlands,*

## Hare Krishna Land, Mauritius

**Confessions of a Blind and Deaf Disciple**

**Invocation**

It is the duty of the disciple to appear as a fool before his spiritual master and pray for his mercy to eliminate his faults. Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, I will confess all my faults before you and pray that my inability to actually hear your instructions and follow your example will vanish. Your mercy is so vast and powerful that if I can obtain it, I am certain I can conquer my seemingly insurmountable problems.

**Confessions**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have no attraction for the *kīrtana* of the holy name. So often during temple *kīrtanas,* I leave to do something I consider to be more important, even though it really could wait. I’ve attended many *kīrtanas* in which you were present, but I never once saw you leave in the middle, and obviously you were the busiest, with the most important work. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* you have enlightened us about the absolute importance of *kīrtana* by saying that the more we chant and dance in the association of devotees, the more we awaken our love of God. Although my eyes seem to see your example and my ears apparently hear your words, factually I don’t really see or hear you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, now that I think I have become a so-called big devotee and am doing so much important work for the movement, I have become more careless in following the details of devotional service. Thinking it is not so important to attend the full morning program, I don’t always attend all the functions. Often I come late to the programs I do attend, or I leave early. The tendency to be lax in my devotional practices is setting on me. I forget to pay obeisance to you when I take rest in the evening and wake in the morning. I’ve become slack in maintaining proper standards of brahminical behavior and cleanliness. I fall asleep regularly when reading your books or lie down and read them. And although I manage the temple, I overlook so many of the details which you emphasized. The list of details I neglect can go on indefinitely. You told us that “big devotee” means one who follows all the big rules and little rules. You showed us by your example that your eyes were everywhere and saw everything. No important detail was ever neglected by you. Unfortunately, your words and example have not changed my heart. My eyes and ears are blinded by *māyā.*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when I speak during *Bhāgavatam* class, I am at a great loss when it comes to referring to *śāstra* and quoting Sanskrit verses. I find myself wasting time referring to so many mundane facts and figures, and in general representing the philosophy in a way very unlike your presentation. I cannot manage to quote a verse from *Bhāgavatam,* but I manage to elaborately explain the latest current events of this world. You said devotees do not read newspapers and magazines. You always supported your points with a profuse supply of appropriate verses quoted in both Sanskrit and English. And your presentations followed exactly in line of the previous *ācāryas.* I’ve heard and seen you give hundreds of classes like this, but did I really see and hear? The problem is that I have no taste for following your example.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have developed a mania for pushing papers. This mania influences me at all hours of the morning, day, and night. I push papers from one side of my desk to other, and this service keeps me so busy that I can’t manage to find much time for preaching. The more I push papers, the more the paper-pushing work increases. When the devotees complain that I don’t preach enough, I tell them that they cannot understand how important my work is and how busy I am. But the problem is that although I am more expert in paper-pushing than I was in the past, the preaching work in the temple is not expanding and the devotees are not enlivened. You instructed your leading men in Los Angeles to not manage in the evening because the evening was for preaching programs and attending the temple *kīrtanas* and class. You personally wrote to me that good management follows preaching like a shadow and that the best thing is that the managers should always be preaching. I think I am suffering from a severe case of obstinacy which had damaged my hearing abilities. As a result of my hearing loss, I have become lost.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when a devotee not so learned in the *śāstras* gives class, I immediately think of avoiding class, as I feel he will have nothing to say that can enliven or enlighten me. I think I know whatever he’ll say better than he, and I’ll just be bored. My intelligence tells me that I must attend class because it is your order, so if I do, I bring other work with me so I won’t waste my time. Whenever you were too ill to give class, you would still attend and happily sit and hear your disciples speak. You wrote to one devotee to encourage him to attend the evening program by saying that you were attending the evening classes given by your disciples and were enjoying them. You then used your transcendental sarcasm by saying that by avoiding the class, he was trying to become a greater *paramahaṁsa* than you. I have no taste for hearing, coupled with the fact that I am proud. On top of that, I’m not afraid of māyā, and therefore I’m not afraid to miss class. Once, just to teach us how cautious we must be, you told us that you were praying to Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa that you wouldn’t fall into māyā. Your life was the perfect example to us of how to be properly situated in devotional service. I personally witnessed that perfect example again and again, but in your absence my memory has faded.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I bahave in ways that disrupt the devotees and are not beneficial for spreading the movement. I tell myself and others that I act in this way because this is just the way I am and it won’t change, so everyone will have to just live with it. I am so obstinate that instead of removing these problems from my heart, I publicly defend these *anarthas.* Instead of appreciating the devotees who have mercifully pointed out my shortcomings, I condemn them, thinking that they have no right to criticize because they don’t to even a fraction of the service I do. Instead of uprooting these weeds, I keep them firmly implanted within my heart as if they were part of my essential nature. You taught us to be humble. Indeed, once you begged your leading devotees with tears in your eyes, saying, “Please don’t get puffed up.” You showed us how to be humble by showing the utmost respect for your God brothers when you visited them or vice versa, even though you in fact deserved their full respect. My heart is so full of pride that I cannot even relate to the concept of humility. I write if off by saying “Of course Śrīla Prabhupāda was perfectly humble and could act in any way required for Kṛṣṇa’s service, but how can you expect a devotee like me to act in that way?” In this way, I foolishly write off my future hopes for progress and consider your example impossible to follow. If I don’t think I am meant to follow your example, then your example will have no effect upon me. What is the use of a torch for a blind man?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my book bag and street shoes are now hanging in the saṅkīrtana hall of fame. Now that I’ve become an office general, I send the troops out to the front lines while I lounge in the background. “Do as I say, not as I do” has become my motto because I no longer set the example that will inspire the men. In my middle age I think I’ve become too old for the front lines. I’ve become too sophisticated to go out and approach the public on a one-on-one basis. I’ve become too full of good ideas to execute to “waste time” on *saṅkīrtana.* I’ve done the austerity for many years, so I prefer to let this job be done by the new men. As a *vānaprastha*, you were personally distributing your Back to Godhead magazine and later selling *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* First Canto, sets, both in India and America. You also personally sold all the Kṛṣṇa books that newly arrived in time for the 1970 Ratha-yātrā festival in San Francisco. You were always preaching on the front lines, practically until your body stopped working. You told us, “I want the privilege of fighting for Kṛṣṇa until my last breath,” and you did exactly that. With your example before me, there is no question of giving up the *saṅkīrtana* movement. Yet even though this example is before me, I fail to take notice of it and remain in illusion.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I avoid the association of devotees because I prefer to be alone, eat alone, do service alone, read alone, live alone, travel alone, and remain alone, even when I could and should perform these activities with other devotees. You were always surrounded by devotees. You taught us that our advancement is mainly dependent on the association of devotees. When I first joined the movement, we embraced this principle with such earnestness that devotees wouldn’t even go to the corner stone alone. It is by your mercy only that there are devotees all over the world, but my appreciation of them is practically nil. It is obvious that my vision and hearing are critically impaired.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, sometimes I do not attend *maṅgala-ārati* when I take rest late or I’m ill. You told us that as long as we have enough strength to crawl, we must attend *maṅgala-ārati.* You rose early despite ill health, late-night programs, and other inconveniences. I am so fallen that I rationalize your instructions and example based on mundane logic and so-called practicality and convince myself that I can miss *maṅgala-ārati* now and then and it is not important. The later I take rest and the sicker I become, the more your instructions seem to fade away. What is the use of the thousands of letters you wrote telling us we must attend *maṅgala-ārati,* when they fall on deaf ears like mine? What is the value of this life if I cannot hear your instructions?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I spend money on my sense gratification, just to pamper my body and satisfy my tongue. I always find money to develop a program to satisfy my personal desire for service, but I can’t always find a single cent for your BBT. When you request us to spend 50% of our income for book distribution, my rebellious mind thinks that we’ve already distributed so many books and nothing has come of it, so let’s launch a more important program. You said you blindly follow the order of your spiritual master to distribute books, but now I think I have the eyes to see better ways to preach. With this perverted vision, I am capable of destroying any one of your temples I am allowed to manage. My eyesight will only get myself and others into trouble.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am shameless because I take advantage of my service and your movement to attain some fame and prestige for myself. This becomes the main source of my determination and enthusiasm to remain in the movement. I delight in having many people serve me, but I never consider that I am meant to be their servant. When opulence and sense gratification come to me as a result of devotional service, I hoard these pleasures instead of renouncing them because I think I deserve them. I use preaching as an excuse for my *māyā* when in actuality I’ve lost my desire for austerity, renunciation, and simple living. You had palaces throughout the world, but you preferred your simple hut in Māyāpura. Millions of dollars passed through your hands, but hardly more than a few cents were for your personal use. You had thousands of disciples who would do anything you asked, yet you never exhibited a trace of desire to exploit or control them. You taught us that the highest position in the spiritual world is achieved by one who feels himself the lowest. I can’t understand this now because I’m too busy trying to become number one. The only difference between myself and a deaf man is that I am more offensive because I hear but do not act on what I have heard.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I commit many offenses when I chant my rounds. Sometimes I don’t even want to finish my rounds. You told us concentrate on every syllable of the *mantra,* but my mind is preoccupied with other thoughts. Sometimes when I chant I’m apathetic, bored, distracted, sleepy, or restless, and I’m always without devotion. I’m the same when I chant the Gāyatrī *mantra*. Because of these offenses, my progress has almost stopped, and thus I do not develop a taste for the holy name. You said that chanting our sixteen rounds is our ticket back to Godhead. My ticket has become invalid due to offenses. You trained us not to commit offenses. Why can’t I understand that I must stop? The instructions of the spiritual master are the life and soul of the disciple. Obviously I have no life and soul.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am attached to women despite all the warnings you have given about the dangers of associating with them. I associate too much and too closely with them in the name of service. Then on the other hand I criticize and condemn them only because I’m actually attached. A think I’m an attractive man, even though you’ve been trying to convince me for the past twenty years that I’m not. Still I think I can associate with this dangerous type of fire and not get burnt. You said there are problems in this movement because the *brahmacārīs* and *sannyāsīs* associate too much with women. Because I’m in illusion, I contribute to the problems in our movement by associating too much with women. If I were not deaf, I could save myself and others from so much suffering.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I’ve conveniently forgotten so many of your instructions that seem to me to be impractical or impossible. Even though I saw you do the impossible, as if the laws of nature were operating according to your desires, still I have no faith in the spiritual nature and power of your orders. You did everything in a transcendental way and achieved spiritual success. My material vision dictates another approach, but unfortunately the results in the long run prove to be detrimental. “Do not doubt the words of your spiritual master,” you often exclaimed, but fool that I am, I look for a so-called better way of doing things. This ends up proving only to be a way of undoing things – the things that you lived to establish. I am struggling just to keep the ship afloat in your absence, and even if I keep it floating, I cannot guide it properly because I do not listen to the instructions of the captain.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am a faultfinder despite your warning that *vaiṣṇava-aparādha* can uproot the creeper of devotional service. I especially find fault with leaders, GBC men, and ISKCON itself. I think the movement is misdirected, and I know exactly how to rectify the problems and direct it to success. Actually, indirectly I’m envious of you because you once remarked that those who don’t want to cooperate with the society’s leaders are really saying they don’t want to follow your instructions. You also told us, “No matter what happens, don’t leave ISKCON, but remain within and work for its rectification.” But I doubt whether this movement will succeed, even though you told us that our movement would go down in history as saving the world in its darkest hour. Foolishly I place this *anartha* of independence and faultfinding on an altar of so-called preaching and worship it as a sacred deity. The problem is that I feel this deity as Kṛṣṇa consciousness, but you certainly don’t.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am hungry for power and control. Instead of giving the younger devotees position and responsibility and training them for the job, I keep them down by thinking that only I am qualified to do these services. You told us that Kṛṣṇa consciousness means training. You further instructed us to create challenging services to enthuse the devotees to voluntarily take more responsibility and service. You said we should enliven, advise, and train them, not control them. You were actually the personification of giving others time, guidance, encouragement, and responsibility. Why can’t I see your brilliant example even though it is shining in my face? Why can’t I see that your system of dealing with others brought out enthusiasm and surrender, while my system only brings frustration to those who work with me?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I don’t read your books enough, I eat and sleep too much, I waste time in frivolous sports in the name of exercise, I often dress and act like a *karmī* for no reason, I don’t do things the way you did, I don’t display proper Vaiṣṇava etiquette, I think I’m always right... and the list goes on without limit. Above all, I don’t even try to rectify my faults. I just think all I have to do is just work hard (sounds familiar to your often-quoted example of the ass).

Śrīla Prabhupāda, despite all of these *anarthas,* I have taken disciples. On your last days you said, “They can’t even follow, and they want to become guru.” And what is most amazing is that after taking up this service, I believe that I have the right to imitate you. I think I can do whatever I want, whenever I want, and I consider it to be transcendental. You said a perfect follower (not imitator) becomes the next leader, and that an *ācārya* teaches by example. Setting a good example is too troublesome for me. I prefer to use my position for other purposes, namely my false prestige. Why can’t I see that you never became illusioned by pride, no matter how much fame came to you (you are honored all over the three worlds). I can’t see because I have no desire to see.

**Prayers of Hope**

I actually have no hope, but because you are *patita-pāvana,* I have immediately become qualified to receive your mercy. You once said that your instructions are your mercy. Presently I am rejecting this mercy and am thus entangled with innumerable *anarthas.* But I am praying that some day I will be able to follow your instructions and actually follow your example. Then, by your mercy, love of Kṛṣṇa will awaken in my heart. In this position I can become a useful member or your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Otherwise, I will continue to remain only a thorn in your side. My real happiness in life will come when I become your bona fide servant.

After your departure, my feelings of separation were not yet unbearable. But as I saw serious problems taking birth in our movement, the pain of your separation increased. At times it became almost intolerable. But now I see that your sincere followers are taking up your instructions as their life and soul, and finally my pangs of separation are beginning to subside because I see you clearly wherever your instructions are being followed and implemented more carefully in your temples. By your mercy, I pray to also become a sincere follower of yours and thus qualified to render some genuine service at your lotus feet.

(written by Mahātmā dāsa)

*Your servants at Hare Krishna Land Project, Mauritius,*

## Heidelberg

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**”Like the sun, your goodness can travel everywhere in the three worlds, and like the air you can penetrate the internal region of everyone. As such you are as good as the all-pervasive Supersoul.” (*****Bhāg.*** **1.5.7)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisance in the dust of your lotus feet, and like a loving father please accept our well-intentioned but inadequate attempt to glorify you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are staying in the distant spiritual realm with the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, at your side, but you are readily attainable by the powerful vehicle of your vāṇī, which you have left for all those who want to associate with you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are an inexhaustible rain cloud bringing water from the Supreme Personality of Godhead to extinguish the forest fire of misery, which would otherwise burn up the whole world. In the form of your transcendental movement you are giving a constant pleasant shower of mercy. Your dedicated followers can see this clearly.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your gifts are so wonderful that intelligent, rich men’s sons are putting aside their comforts and riches to lay down on the bare floor and accept the austere life of a *brahmacārī.*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you actively and persistently expand yourself in the lives of those who come in contact with you, if they are only a little sincere. Your transcendental book distribution *kī jaya!*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the only bona fide leader of the living entities giving shelter to all. Who – except those who are determined to keep on suffering – can fail to recognize this fact?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, everything you ever said and say is completely true, since Śrī Kṛṣṇa is personally taking care that your words will never fail.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have arranged our lives in such a way that we are constantly induced to remember Kṛṣṇa. This is your expertise and unfathomable mercy. Who could ever express our gratitude for this?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please always stay in our temple, listen to the *saṅkīrtana* results and come to our preaching programs. Please also inspect the Higher Taste shop, the Spiritual Skyliner, and the preaching parties. Please check our lectures wherever we go and watch our “multi-vision” shows. We want to do everything right, so that you are satisfied.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are still immature in Vaiṣṇava dealings. Please enter our hearts so that we can cooperate nicely.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you expect so much from us. Thank you! But we beg to inform you that we have only one qualification: we are beggars at your feet. Please be so kind as to give us strength, enthusiasm, intelligence, detachment, and whatever else is required to serve you eternally.

*Your humble servants in Heidelberg, West Germany.*

## Hillsborough

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,All glories to you on this occasion of the ninety-fourth celebration of your appearance!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You are our most perfect master. In this much-less-than-perfect world, everyone except for us – your most perfectly fortunate disciples and grand disciples – has to live under the control of some far-from-perfect parent, employer, or overlord. But how perfectly willing we will always be to submit to your authority as our eternal father, director, and lord!

Just as the Supreme Personality of Godhead is the infinite spiritual being while the *jīvas* are all his minute expansions, so we are just tiny fragments of your measureless power of mercy. But in the algebra of infinity, boundless perfection still remains without limit even after being divided and distributed again and again. At your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda, there is more than enough mercy to satisfy the entire world. Why should we ever be so greedy, so unconvinced of the magnitude of your compassion, that we have to claim your favor as our personal property or worry that there will not be enough for ourselves if others take first?

Please smile on our humble endeavors, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to help spread your mercy in this unhappy world. All glories to you on your appearance day!

*Your servants in New-Goloka-dhāma (Hillsborough, North Carolina),*

## Hong Kong

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisance at your lotus feet. After a few years of book distribution in this area, people still may not recognize the devotees. But when they receive your books and see your pictures, they are stunned by your transcendental personality as manifested in your picture, and begin to listen to our philosophy. It is not difficult for anyone to imagine how great your divine personality manifested in the picture of Your Divine Grace can preach. We are from a background of voidism, and you are coming to annihilate the *śūnya-vādī* philosophy.

Most of us took up Kṛṣṇa consciousness just wanting to end our suffering. But you, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, are so merciful that you left your beloved Vṛndāvana *dhāma* and underwent various austerities to go to the hellish cities, fulfilling His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta’s desire. We are begging for the blessing to have the ability to face all difficulties so as to fulfill pure desires. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave us these blessings for the Chinese devotees: “The last race to join, but the best.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our ever well-wisher.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you say books are the basis and book distribution creates a field for preaching. By your divine grace, this bare and futile field, which is the farthest from the horizon of Gauḍa, is being cultivated, and fruits of *kṛṣṇa-prema* are growing. Your books are described as the law books of the next ten thousand years, and one who takes shelter of them is sure to be freed from the judgment of Yamarāja. According to our previous sinful activities, we took the lowest birth by the arrangement of the material nature, but somehow, by your causeless mercy to us, we got the most valuable gift of all – *Śrī Harināma,* which frees us from heaps and heaps of karmic reaction. Therefore, since we are actually unfathomably indebted to you, how can we repay this debt? You gave us the answer in your last words: “Your love for me will be shown by how well you cooperate to spread this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.”

On this most auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day, we pray for your mercy to accept us as your servants eternally.

(written by Lakṣmaṇa dāsa)

*Your eternal servants in Khāsadesa (Hong Kong).*

## Honolulu

**Memories of a Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya**

It seems like a lost dream, but sometimes it seems like yesterday. It was in Calcutta, back at the end of ’74. There was one advocate, and he wouldn’t become a Life Member. But he insisted that Śrīla Prabhupāda come to his home. Finally, somehow, it was arranged, and he sent two cars to pick up Śrīla Prabhupāda and his party for breakfast. It was all the way across Calcutta. A few good kīrtana men were there at that time, and I was invited to squeeze in with them in the second car.

When we arrived at the man’s home, one of his children blew a conch shell, and then we were led up a few flights of stairs. We were all seated in a room around Śrīla Prabhupāda, who sat in a chair while we sat with our instruments on the floor. We were between Prabhupāda and the man’s father, who was tilted up in bed, near death. Śrīla Prabhupāda began conversing with the old man in Bengali, cordially asking his age and commenting on the temporal nature of life. But soon Śrīla Prabhupāda began unsparingly describing the havoc that Ramakrishna and Vivekananda had wrought in Bengal and throughout the world with their Māyāvāda conception of *yata mat, tata path:* “Whatever you think, that’s your path.”

We performed *kīrtana* for awhile, and then we were led downstairs for breakfast. To our surprise, the man had simply purchased a few boxes of sweets and *samosās* from the marketplace, along with some *miṣṭī dahī* in cups. We sat still, wondering whether to eat or not; then Śrīla Prabhupāda began to eat a small portion, and we followed in our typically less restrained manner. While eating, Śrīla Prabhupāda lightly said, “This quarter is famous for *miṣṭī dahī”.* In a short time we were finished, and while walking out we divided Śrīla Prabhupāda’s remnants and then headed for the cars. After returning, Śrīla Prabhupāda’s secretary said Śrīla Prabhupāda had spoken to the man’s son, who had accompanied him on the way back. He was about 9 or 10, and Prabhupāda asked if they ate fish at home. He said that they did, but that he did not like it himself.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, whose pastimes are so bright with pure goodness! Illuminate the dense darkness or our wicked minds! The splendor of your memory is our only hope in life. With your words, please “Kick out the influence of hundreds of thousands of Rāhu planets!”

O Śrīla Prabhupāda! O great wise man! Kindly descendant of Vyāsa, Śuka, Mahāprabhu, Rūpa, Sanātana, Śrīla Bhaktivinoda, and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta! You once said that your Guru Mahārāja’s Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Gaurakiśora dāsa Bābajī, did not preach, seeing the hopelessness of the rascal world. He thought, “Let them go to hell; I have no power to stop them.” But then you said, “My *guru* asked me to preach.” But you lamented, seeing us so degraded. Let the ocean of your pastimes remain forever in our hearts, and help us to dive and surface in its waves. Pick us up! Give us one more chance, though we do not deserve it.!

(written by Puṣkara dāsa)

*Your insignificant servants in Hawaii.*

## Houston

Dear Prabhupāda,  
Lover of God,  
We worship two,  
Both Him and you.  
Our faith at your feet,  
You will never cheat,  
While pretenders pose,  
Your mission grows.  
Intent to teach  
Us how to preach,  
You gave your heart,  
ISKCON to start.  
You made your soul  
A begging bowl,  
Chanting the Name,  
Beseeching the same.  
Now you’ve left,  
We are bereft,  
Our lives wane,  
All is in vain.  
But you still live  
With those who give  
Other souls a chance  
To chant and dance.  
Preaching gives us life,  
Solves all strife,  
Pleases you,  
WE ALL MUST DO.

(written by Gopālācārya dāsa)

*From your servants at ISKCON Houston.*

## Inishrath

*nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate devam gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisance at your lotus feet on this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā. All glories unto you! Within the short time of your stay with us you have taught us what it means to live a life according to Kṛṣṇa’s desire: simply to follow the instructions of the spiritual master minutely – the secret of spiritual life. Minutely you have carried out the instructions of your spiritual master and “fought like Arjuna did” – the rest is history.

Please bless us on this day to be able to also follow perfectly in your footsteps. “We want Arjuna,” you said, “or we want none!”

How many times we have been victimized by our concoctions and fantasies in spite of your words: “Don’t add anything; don’t subtract – I have given you everything.”

O let us today take the opportunity to renew our commitments to your instructions, to your ISKCON. Let us arm ourselves with your commands, stand and fight like Arjuna did: “Just distribute Kṛṣṇa everywhere. Where is the problem? *Yāre dekha tāre kaha kṛṣṇa-upadeśa – that is our business.”*

Emerging from the trouble of the past, we fall at your feet and pray. Let us fulfill your desires to preach and distribute the most precious literatures – your life and soul – everywhere. This is the only hope for human society at large, as well as for ourselves. Only then can we, by your divine grace, be part of the great movement of Lord Caitanya.

Books are the basis. Preaching is the essence. Purity is the force. Utility is the principle.

Having fallen short in so many ways, we fall at your feet and pray for your mercy, promising to make amends.

*Your servants at Rādhā-Govinda Dhāma, the Inishrath Island Farm Community, Ireland.*
