## Vienna

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble and respectful obeisances in the dust of your outstanding lotus feet.

Thinking of you, one is unavoidably drowned in the ocean of your unlimited transcendental qualities, which are so relishable that one simply wants to be eternally attached to you.

As soon as one gets the first glimpse of understanding of who you are, one desires something unique in this world: to become a totally surrendered soul. How is this? No doubt it is so because you are the embodiment of the perfect spiritual master, who is so empowered that he can lead the whole universe back to Godhead. You did everything possible to please the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Kṛṣṇa, and He did everything to make it clear that you are His most surrendered servant in this world. Although He showed it clearly to everyone, and in this way made it easy to surrender unto you, no one can get away with being a sentimental follower: You arranged it in a way that certain principles are so much connected to being called "your man" that no one can cheaply adore you. In this way we can easily detect someone's relationship to you.

How can one be your follower without following the four regulative principles of abstaining from meat, gambling, illicit sex, and intoxication?

How can one be called your follower without surrendering to and cooperating with your pure followers, who assist you in keeping this ISKCON going?

How can one be called your follower without developing wonderful Vaiṣṇava qualities and behavior, which are unparalleled in this world?

A handful of checkpoints provide a sure sign—and we see those devotees displaying them who have implicit faith in you and simply accept your orders.

Please let us become more and more attached to your instructions and remain humble servants of those who understand how to serve you seriously.

(written by Sadhusangananda dāsa)

*Your servants in Vienna,*

**Disciple of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Hanuman dāsa

**Granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Vṛndāvana

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
On this most holy, blessed day,  
Along with those around the world we pray,  
Falling humbly at your feet,  
Your sacred glories we repeat,  
Like lamp lit unto morning sun  
Or Gaṅgā's worship with water done.  
Our mind is stretched beyond the sky;  
How can we ever glorify  
That mercy that you’ve given?  
But still today we're driven  
To offer words as tiny measure,  
In vain hope it may give you pleasure.

At three score years and ten,  
You left this holy land,  
Where endless waves of bliss  
Are flowing due to this:  
Śrī Rādhikā and Śyāma  
Sport in this sacred dhāma.  
Set sail for foreign land  
With your divine command  
To spread the holy name  
As freely as the rain  
To those who are not fit,  
Entrapped in Kali's grip.

*Pṛithivīte āche yata*  
*Nagarādi-grāma*  
*Sarvatra pracāra*  
*Hoibe mora nāma.*

Five hundred years have flown  
Since Caitanya made this known.  
But some of them had guessed  
The ones that would be blessed  
Were each and ev'ry town  
In Nadiā around.  
Or maybe in Bengal—  
That certainly was all.  
Orissa, too, they said,  
Is where the name might spread.  
In Bhārata-varṣa, maybe,  
But not across the sea.  
The Ṭhākura did not agree:  
"Why not beyond the sea?  
If Caitanya's Lord of all,  
Let all lands hear his call!"  
And so he got a son,  
To get the service done.  
A lion among the saints;  
The māyāvādī faints;  
In you he marked the sign:  
"He'll do the work in time."  
He made you this request:  
To get the whole world blessed.

‘Twas you, our Prabhupāda,  
Who crossed the ocean broad.  
To every continent  
The holy name was sent,  
Giving joy to all,  
With Lord Gaurāṅga’s call.  
“ ‘Tis very great indeed,"  
Some of them agreed.  
“The name has crossed the sea,  
To the world that's free.  
But in the Soviet,  
They’ll surely say, 'Nyet.'"

That day I'll not forget—  
Remembering, my eyes are wet:  
The Russians came—sixty—  
And danced in your samādhi,  
Beyond their wildest dream,  
Beside Yamunā’s stream.  
We are so very low;  
The Lord we do not know.  
The Vaiṣṇava, we have heard,  
Is he who should be served.  
But to such a one as you,  
How can we give what's due?  
We only hope and pray,  
Our debt we can repay,  
That when your servants come  
To your home, Vṛndāvana-dhāma,  
By service of their feet,  
Your mercy we will meet.

(written by Dina-bandhu dāsa)

*Your most lowly of servants, the devotees at the Krishna-Balarām Mandir.*

## Walla Walla

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Lord Caitanya is glorified as the most merciful incarnation of the Lord. Lord Caitanya preached Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over India, but you preached Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. Therefore we think you are more merciful than even Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

*Yāre dekhe tāre kāhā kṛṣṇa-upadeśa:* "Wherever you go, whomever you meet, you must tell them about Kṛṣṇa." Here is one small place in the world. Please help us to make the people here in eastern Washington Kṛṣṇa conscious.

*Your servants at ISKCON Walla Walla, Washington,*

Janārdana dāsa / Bhakta "Trino" / Bhakta "Turtle" / Lakṣmī-priyā-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Lucie / Joshua

## Warri

My dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our insignificant obeisances at your divine lotus feet. We have no *bhakti,* the prerequisite for offering you a Vyāsa-pūjā offering and presentation, but by the mercy of your transcendental utterances, we have a chance to write in commemoration of your appearance.

The world is at your lotus feet. We are just fools in a dark well. All glories, all glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious day, your Vyāsa-pūjā!

*Your servants the Warri, Nigeria, ISKCON temple.*

## Washington, D.C.

A poem for Śrīla Prabhupāda

While others slept, you wrote the Vedic imports,  
Alone in Vraja s early-morning chill,  
Sanskrit verse, translation, and your purports,  
With steady hand and mind of iron will.  
While others woke, you rested, unaffected,  
Your mind unfettered by their worldly cares.  
How foolishly they rushed in, unprotected,  
To all-devouring death's relentless snare.  
While others cried in anguish, you decided  
Kṛṣṇa consciousness would change their lives.  
Mahāprabhu’s prediction, once derided,  
Could come to pass if preaching were revived.  
While others stayed, secure in Bhārata-varṣa,  
Forsaking all the world as wasted men,  
You ventured forth to give your holy darshan,  
Reminding us of Kṛṣṇa once again.

While others pushed their way into the spotlight,  
You worked behind the scenes to serve the Lord,  
‘Till Kṛṣṇa’s name was known in every household,  
Though to the world your name remained ignored.

While others didn’t know or understand you,  
To those who asked, you gave yourself in full.  
The urgent senses failing to command you,  
You showed your men how to resist their pull.

While others sought the public’s satisfaction,  
You stuck to Kṛṣṇa’s message “As It Is,”  
Turning iron to gold by touchstone action,  
Mahāprabhu’s most expert alchemist.

While others stood in line to get their pension,  
You struck out ‘round the world, and yet again!  
Preaching with no trace of apprehension,  
Your guru’s words your ever-guiding friend.

While others crept, with eyes closer  
You looked at death as if life were the same.  
While others spent for mortal reconstruction,  
Your medicine was Kṛṣṇa’s holy name.  
While others' debts are paid in pounds and shillings,  
Our debt to you cannot be so retired.  
Accept us as your slaves, if you are willing,  
And make us dance to fill your heart’s desire.

Your servants in Washington, D.C.

## Wellington

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances on this most auspicious day of the year, Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā.

Why is today the most auspicious day of the year?

Because YOU have given us the opportunity to join Lord Caitanya's *saṅkīrtana* party, to chant and dance and take *prasādam.* Śrīla Prabhupāda, YOU have given us the chance to develop our love for Kṛṣṇa and go back home, back to Godhead. Therefore you deserve the most glory.

By your purity and personal example you have changed our hearts, and you have also taught us how to try to change the hearts of others. In fact, the most wonderful thing about your pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness is that everything you do—both by your preaching and your personal example—is aimed at giving Kṛṣṇa to others. As stated by Sanātana Gosvāmī in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,*

"Some behave very well but do not preach the cult of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, whereas others preach but do not behave properly. You simultaneously perform both duties in relation to the holy name by your personal behavior and by your preaching. Therefore, you are the spiritual master of the entire would, for you are the most advanced devotee in the world." (Cc. *Antya* 4.102-03)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please protect us from *māyā’s* onslaughts and allow us to remain your humble servants eternally.

*Your fallen servants in Wellington, New Zealand,*

Kānu Paṇḍita dāsa / Rādhā-priya-devī dāsī / Bhakta Robin / Bhaktin Frances / Bhakta Marcus

**Congregation**

Bhakta Steve / Kerin / Maggie / Dayal Govan and family / Raja Ram and family / Jagdish / Mohan / Prema / Sneh / Sashi / Miriam / Karen

## Zambia

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We offer our respectful obeisances at your lotus feet, which are acknowledged by the advanced devotees as the appropriate shelter for those who wish to escape from the cycle of repeated birth and death. Your lotus feet have purified and saved many souls who would be classified as *yavanas* and *mlecchas;* all over the world, you have saved and elevated all categories of fallen souls to Lord Kṛṣṇa's devotional service. Although there were many *ācāryas* before you, you were the one particularly empowered by Lord Kṛṣṇa to accomplish this mission worldwide. Thus you are glorious, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

We were not fortunate enough to meet you when you were present on the planet. But we have heard about your glorious activities from others. We have heard how, in your old age you gave up your comfort in Vṛndāvana to go to the West and preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We have heard of the heart attacks you suffered aboard the *Jaladuta* on your journey to America. We have heard how you went to reside with meat-eaters at Butler, then with a Māyāvādī in New York. All about the external poverty and loneliness that you suffered during this time, we have heard. Your going to reside in the Bowery, your founding of ISKCON, and the first temple, at 26 Second Avenue, the second temple, in San Francisco, the heart attack and the first return to India—all these activities we have heard with great wonder, seeing your great determination.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, there is no doubt that you are one of the greatest stalwarts in the disciplic succession. You created a big wave that has rippled throughout the seven continents of this planet. You brought in light where there was great darkness. Thus you have saved so many people from great danger. That light is still spreading all over the world and is increasing in its intensity. We thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for saving us from the darkness of this world, and we pray at your lotus feet that we may be able to spread this light of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others.

*All your grandchildren struggling here in Zambia, on the continent of Africa.*

## Śrī Vyāsa-Pūjā homages from the Publishers

## Australian BBT

To our beloved and eternal father, Śrīla Prabhupāda:

On this day we celebrate the wonderful event of your appearance—an event so significant that it has had a deep effect on humanity at large already and will continue to do so in the future, for the next ten thousand years.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, how can a blind man describe the brilliance of the sun or the subtleties of a many-faceted jewel? How can a man without the sense of smell try to glorify the fragrance of the blue lotus? With our conditioned nature and covered understanding, we are in similar darkness in trying to comprehend your deep, transcendental qualities. Although we have expressed what little vision we have of your position by following the path you have prescribed, still your real stature remains, in large part, beyond our perception.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we owe our very lives to you, we owe our happiness and our realizations to you. The very structure and strength of our being is due to your mercy and compassion on the fallen souls of Kali-yuga. You are the king of devotees, the humble, ever-tolerant *sevaka-bhagavān* (servitor Godhead) who has complied with Lord Kṛṣṇa's request that you descend to save us.

Just as Kṛṣṇa desired that Arjuna be given the credit for their victory on the Battlefield of Kurukṣetra, so He has also wished you to be eulogized for your victory over *māyā* in this Age of Kali. Just as the father is satisfied when his son does even better in life than himself, so Kṛṣṇa is content that you have delivered this Kṛṣṇa consciousness in all different countries, whereas He Himself limited the display of His wonderful pastimes.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this sanctified day we pray with fervent emotion that we will make you proud of us. We want to show you, by your grace (for it is only by the mercy of the spiritual master that one can succeed in his spiritual endeavors), that your sons and daughters are dedicated to fulfilling your mission. Please allow us to keep your desires carved deep into the altar of our hearts and our desires for petty enjoyment cast far away.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the whole world should be a monument to your greatness, and all literature should be inspired to glorify your activities and that of your beloved Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. On this day of your appearance, we offer our *daṇḍavats* without cessation, and we pray again and again to remain always firmly fixed to your service and to your divine vision, qualifying ourselves in some small way as recipients of your mercy.

(written by Kṛṣṇarūpā-devī dāsī)

*Your servants at the BBT in Sydney, Australia,*

Nareśvara dāsa / Jyotirmaya dāsa / Mahāpuruṣa dāsa / Gopamātā-devī dāsī / Kṛṣṇarūpā-devī dāsī

## Bombay BBT

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at our lotus feet. We are very fortunate to be able to offer homage to Your Divine Grace on this most blessed day.

Suffering in this world is all we knew  
*until the day that we met you.*

Suffering in this world is all there is  
*until the day we pass the quiz.*

Suffering in this world was complete defeat  
*until the day we touched your feet.*

Suffering in this world is all we deserve  
*until the day we begin to serve.*

Suffering in this world is finished with ease  
*until the day your shelter we leave.*

The living entity comes into this material world and wanders in the different species of life. Due to the power of Māyā, the living entity tries to enjoy but only undergoes frustration. He foolishly considers everything finished at death and races towards illusory pleasures, only to trip and fall on the rough reality. Sense enjoyment brings so much distress.

By good fortune, one comes in contact with a pure devotee of the Lord and stops the cycle of birth and death. It's a miracle that at one moment the living entity is locked up in the modes of material nature, suffering under an eternal sentence, and the next moment, by contact with the pure devotee, he is unshackled.

Although freed by association with the spiritual master, the devotee must always be careful to follow his instructions carefully. For that devotee who has firm faith, the only fortune worth having is service to the feet of the bona fide spiritual master. Such a devotee will be successful. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the priceless gift, and loss of it is the price for material success. What is the value of comfort and luxury if they cause us to be less Kṛṣṇa conscious?

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, our beloved spiritual master, on this auspicious day of your appearance, we pray for the determination to endure the sufferings of our past misdeeds so that we may claim a place at your feet in the service of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

*Your servants at the Bombay BBT.*

## North American BBT

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

We all came to this movement to give up this material world,  
Burned by the association of intoxication, meat-eating, and unchaste girls.  
We vowed to you to never pursue again a mundane career,  
We appreciated your books as the lawbooks for the next ten thousand years.  
You told us that we'd please you if we please the temple president and GBC.  
Distributing your books, harinām, and prasād till beyond eternity—  
That we can become the most fortunate and help others to become seers,  
That your books will be the lawbooks for the next ten thousand years.  
We want to push on your mission, we want to be good sons,  
We won't rest till we pay back our debt, a fulfillment that won’t ever be done.  
We want that every man and woman on the planet in their arms will wear  
One of your books, the lawbooks for mankind for the next ten thousand years.

You instructed all your disciples to write and keep a diary.  
Some have expanded that order to publish their own books from A to Z.  
The books are being reviewed by a council of GBC peers,  
Who see your books as the lawbooks for mankind for the next ten thousand years.  
At times we may be rascals, at times we may act like fools,  
When we forget the difference between us and you, transgressing who is who.  
But that heavy dirt is easily cleansed off the eternal shining mirror  
When we take shelter of your books, the lawbooks for the next ten thousand years.

*We've struggled since you left us, though your vāṇī still oils the gears.*  
*Some temples have closed, and a few others seem to be quite a bit in arrears.*  
*But we know that we can please you, we know you are with us here,*  
*When we profusely distribute your books, the lawbooks for mankind for the next ten thousand years.*  
*Someone must give dīkṣā, it says so in the CC.*  
*Everyone's supposed to give śikṣā, distributing the holy name without a fee.*  
*But the real śikṣā is given to one who submissively hears*  
*Your books, which are the lawbooks for mankind for the next ten thousand years.*

*Somehow the movement goes on and on, by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa and you,*  
*Although people have come and people have gone and do what people do.*  
*Those who have stayed have cooperated, though sometimes filled with tears,*  
*To see that your books will be the lawbooks for mankind for the next ten thousand years!*

Śrīla Prabhupāda-kī Jaya!

*Your servants at the North American BBT,*

Śeṣa dāsa / Praghoṣa dāsa / Ekanātha dāsa / Raṇajit dāsa / Śrī Rāma dāsa / Rājendranātha dāsa / Sura dāsa / Gaṇeśa dāsa / Dhṛtarāṣṭra dāsa / Ravīndranātha dāsa / Śubhrā-devī dāsī / Kṣamā-devī dāsī

## Portuguese BBT

Most dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

When we first came in contact with your message, who among ourselves could conceive of such a deep and broad personality as yourself in this material world? Who knew Śrī Kṛṣṇa's plan to send you to this tiny part of His creation just to rescue us?

Nothing happens by chance, what to speak of the appearance of a pure devotee—such an intimate associate of Kṛṣṇa. How the Lord arranged for you to enact your pastimes in this world is far too inconceivable for our very tiny brains to understand. Since the beginning of creation, the Supreme Lord has planned all the possible ways to reclaim the fallen souls back home, back to Godhead. It is for this purpose that He reveals everything to Lord Brahma, and it is for this purpose that He Himself descends, assuming innumerable incarnations. Among them, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is the most munificent of all. To help the Supreme Lord in this work, His pure devotees also come.

Therefore, that is the reason for your divine appearance, O most munificent Śrīla Prabhupāda! Which words can really describe all your qualities? Only other pure devotees can glorify you appropriately. Only Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, whose intimate associate you are, can really understand your personality and mission. As for ourselves, we can simply take advantage of this opportunity to beg your mercy and offer our prostrated obeisances unto you so that we might attain the position of a tiny atom at your lotus feet.

*From your servants engaged in producing your books in Portuguese.*

## Spain BBT

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet!

In the Third Canto of *Snmad-Bhagavatam* (3.9.5), Brahma, praying for creative energy, states,

*ye tu tvadīya-caraṇāmbuja-kośa-gandhaṁ*  
*jighranti karṇa-vivaraiḥ śruti-vāta-nītam*  
*bhaktyā gṛhīta-caraṇaḥ parayā ca teṣāṁ*  
*nāpaiṣi nātha hṛdayāmburuhāt sva-puṁsām*

**"O my Lord, persons who smell the aroma of Your lotus feet, carried by the air of Vedic sound through the holes of the ears, accept your devotional service. For them You are never separated from the lotus of their hearts."**

The world will be eternally grateful to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for filling its atmosphere with the transcendental fragrance of Lord Kṛṣṇa's lotus feet by showering upon it millions of volumes of transcendental literature, such as *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* The Lord, never being separated from the lotus of your heart, empowered you to deliver Him in His literary incarnation to the fallen souls of Kali-yuga, thus soothing their feverish condition of material existence with the cooling air of Vedic sounds.

In the purport to the above-mentioned verse, you explain, *"Parā-bhakti,* or spontaneous love of God,... can be attained simply by hearing about Him (His name, form, quality, etc.) from authentic sources like *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* recited by pure, unalloyed devotees of the Lord." How fortunate, then, are those who receive your books, open their ears to receive the message of *kṛṣṇa-kathā* and accept Kṛṣṇa's devotional service. We know you as one of those very rare pure devotees whose unalloyed "as it is" presentation of Kṛṣṇa's name, form, qualities, etc., can evoke spontaneous love of God in those willing to listen with due attention. And although most of us may not be able to claim that we have reached that cherished goal of spontaneous love of God, we nevertheless can testify that by our contacting Kṛṣṇa through His bona fide representative, our lives have been transformed miraculously from a doglike existence to a godlike existence.

Every day presents another opportunity to advance on the path back home, back to Godhead. How quickly one wants to go depends on each one of us. You have given us everything to reach the goal within this one lifetime, and hopefully all of your faithful followers will join you soon in the spiritual realm. But even for those who go astray, there can only be good fortune, ultimately, because anyone who once has smelled the fragrance of the Lord's lotus feet can never forget this experience, and sooner or later Kṛṣṇa, who never forgets His servant, will give him another chance to perfect his spiritual life.

Thus nothing can compare to the unparalleled beauty and potency of devotional service, and therefore it is our duty to make it available to others. One of your basic instructions in this regard was "Do as I do," and although it would be presumptuous to think that we can do what you have done, we can at least try to follow your example. For us members of one of your BBT divisions, this undoubtedly means to publish and print your books as quickly and profusely as possible. We therefore pray on this auspicious day of your appearance for creative energy (just as Brahmā prayed to Viṣṇu) in order to help fulfill your desire to see all of your books published in all of the languages of the world—or, as far as we are concerned, at least in Spanish.

*Your menial servants at the BBT Spain,*

**Disciple of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Veda-vyāsa dāsa

**Disciple of B. R. Śrīdhara Mahārāja**

Ācārya Swami

**Granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Bhāgavata-siddhānta dāsa / Hanumān dāsa / Sucandra dāsa / Yadu dāsa

## Śrī Vyāsa-Pūjā homages from Other Sources

## Back to Godhead Magazine

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

On the ninety-third anniversary of your appearance in this world, we are recalling with awe your compassionate vision and divine intelligence in starting *Back to Godhead* magazine for the deliverance of the innumerable souls rotting in ignorance in this horrible age. As the empowered representative of Lord Caitanya, you knew exactly what was necessary to awaken Kali's victims from their slumber and attract them to the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa. You began your mission by establishing the backbone of your merciful worldwide movement:

From his front room at 6 Sita Kanta Banerjee, Abhay conceived, wrote, edited, and typed the manuscript for a magazine. He designed a logo, a long rectangle across the top of the page. In the upper left-hand comer was a figure of Lord Caitanya, effulgent with rays of light like rays from the sun. In the lower right were silhouettes of a crowd of people, in darkness but groping to receive light from Lord Caitanya. And between Lord Caitanya and the people, the title unfurled like a banner—BACK TO GODHEAD. In the lower right comer was a picture of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī seated at his writing, looking up thoughtfully as he composed. Above the logo ran the motto "Godhead is Light. Nescience is darkness. Where there is Godhead there is no Nescience."

That was 1944. And now, forty-five years later, we at *Back to Godhead* are trying to maintain the transcendental magazine you have given us, which you described in the first issue as "a feeble attempt... to bring up a real relation of humanity with central relation of the Supreme Personality of Godhead." While your attempt was magnificent, our attempts are certainly feeble. Yet because we are trying to keep *Back to Godhead* the way you wanted it, we are confident that it can still bring people into their relationship with Kṛṣṇa. And the mail we receive from readers strengthens this conviction. One young man recently wrote, "I am eternally indebted to all of you for bringing this useless fool to Kṛṣṇa consciousness by way *of Back to Godhead."*

We at *Back to Godhead* are eternally indebted to you for saving us and for entrusting us with the responsibility to continue the publication of your magazine. We also feel ourselves to be useless fools, yet we know that our success lies only in our ability to become instruments of your will. If you direct our feeble affairs, the weapon *of Back to Godhead* will find its mark. It will break *māyā’s* grip and change people's lives.

We have lately been calling *Back to Godhead* "Śrīla Prabhupāda's favorite magazine." This is an understatement, of course, like calling ISKCON your favorite movement. More precisely, you once wrote, *"Back to Godhead* is my life and soul." And you wrote to an early BTG editor that he should make it his life and soul.

We want to *make Back to Godhead* our life and soul. Please grant us a drop of your realization of the importance *of Back to Godhead.* And please grant us a drop of our faith in the inestimable transcendental value of the mass distribution *of Back to Godhead.*

(written by Nṛgarāja dāsa)

*Your unworthy servants at Back to Godhead magazine.*

## Bala Books

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Since beginning Bala Books under your direction in 1977, we have been guided by the standards you set. "If it is worth doing for Kṛṣṇa," you said, "it is worth doing right."

We are incapable of doing great quantities of service. Our prayer is that whatever we do, you guide us to do it in the very best way for Kṛṣṇa: with devotion.

In Europe in 1974, you showed how devotion makes the service perfect. Traveling by plane, you pinned a drawing to the back of the seat in front of you and began chanting *japa.* That drawing was a simple, awkward crayon rendering of Kṛṣṇa done by one of the *gurukula* children. It was a crude drawing, but it was done with whatever devotion a small child had to offer. And you accepted it as your object of meditation.

We see our service as being like a child's awkward attempt to draw. But by your mercy, even our fumbling efforts can be made perfect.

We pray that you allow us to continue our contribution to Lord Caitanya's *saṅkīrtana* movement through spiritual publications. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for having given us this lifeline. Our hope lies solely in pleasing you.

*Your servants,*

Yogeśvara dāsa / Prahlāda-priya dāsa / Satyarāja dāsa / Māyāpura dāsa / Kathāmṛta dāsa / Yamunā-devī dāsī / Dinatāriṇī-devī dāsī / Vṛndā-devī dāsī

## Benin

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Your appearance is the only hope of our being freed from material existence. Who is more merciful than Your Divine Grace? You are expert in making the materialists accept Kṛṣṇa.

Your messages of love of God make us know who we are, where we are, and what our eternal relationship with the Supreme Personality of Godhead is.

Alone you built a house that all the world can live in. We all come from the north, south, east, and west, yet by your mercy we all live together with the same goal: to be Kṛṣṇa conscious and to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your message clearly reveals you to us as a pure and perfect devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Please bless us so that we can unlimitedly increase our service.

*Your insignificant servants,*  
*The devotees of the Hare Kṛṣṇa temple in Benin, Nigeria.*

## Birmingham

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet.

On this day we would like to thank you for the transcendental gift you have given us in the form of service in your ISKCON movement. When we look back on our own activities before coming in contact with your movement, we begin to realize what a nightmarish existence we lived—performing activities which need not be mentioned here, ruining our lives and ruining the lives of others as well. In our darkest hour, when we were hoping against hope to somehow or other get out of this abyss of material suffering. Your Divine Grace mercifully appeared in full brilliance. You appeared in the form of your books and your disciples, who were transparent via media of your transcendental, effulgent self.

Through the mercy of these two media—your books and your disciples—we are getting a glimpse of what it is like to live a life free from the tribulations of material existence.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your mercy we are beginning to understand that the qualities of a Vaiṣṇava are not fictitious but real. The qualities of love, compassion, humility, magnanimity, and numerous others are fully manifest in you. You are the embodiment of all these qualities. In your person you perfectly synthesize all the good qualities of the demigods.

So on this day and, by your mercy, during every second of every day for the rest of our lives, we fully dedicate ourselves to hearing, assimilating, and teaching your instructions for the benefit of the conditioned souls of this planet.

We pray, beg, and cry that somehow or other, by your inconceivable causeless mercy, we will be placed as insignificant specks of dust in the shade of the most merciful lotus feet to have set foot in this creation: the lotus feet of Your Divine Grace, Śrīla A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

*Your servants at the Birmingham Preaching Center, U.K.,*

Govinda-bhāṣya dāsa / Rāma-rāja dāsa / Bhaktin Karen

## Calabar

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this day of your appearance. Please excuse me for my rascaldom and inadequacy in the way of my expression in representing the devotees of the Calabar *yātrā.*

It is said that pure devotees and Kṛṣṇa are identical in that one cannot understand them by direct perception of the mundane senses. Still, I will try to mention a few of your glories that even a layman can appreciate.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have saved the whole world from the imminent danger of total faithlessness in Kṛṣṇa (God) and given the world true spiritual activity. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Although we may not take the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness very seriously, we can now feel relief from the fear of the present and future trends of the world's misdirected civilization.

It is only the dearmost devotees of Kṛṣṇa who can revive God consciousness and effect a rapid evolution in the dormant consciousness of the conditioned souls. Who can imagine the condition of the present world without you, Śrīla Prabhupāda?

I know nothing about you but for the little association I have had with some of your bona fide representatives, the present *ācāryas.* I am begging to have a little insight concerning your great mercy. Our dearmost father, you have become a household word in every town and among all the truly learned personalities of the world.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, your broad smile in your pictures gives reassurance to your devotees, while your frowning expression in some of your pictures creates fear in the minds of those who oppose devotional activities.

By the ISKCON institution you have set in motion by your great will, we are still being sustained in your service. Our dear *guru,* there is no need to make a great endeavor now to narrate your glories. In the future we will be able to narrate them more completely, if we become purified of all sinful habits.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have become so indebted to your lotus feet that we will cling to them eternally. We are so degraded that our lifestyle is quite contrary to our professed principles. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, excuse and forgive us. All glories to you.

*Your servants in Calabar, Nigeria.*

## Fort Myers

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

As the moments turn to days, and the days turn to years, my heart feels more the loss of your personal association and guidance.

Your name, "Śrīla Prabhupāda," removes all fear. Your gravity helps me fix my mind on the goal of life—Kṛṣṇa. Your glance frees my soul from all illusion.

You are still present in the form of all your instructions—to direct me if I am sincere and to encourage me if I am despondent.

We love you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please give us the shade of your lotus feet.

*Your devotees in Fort Myers Beach (Southwest Florida),*

## Goteborg

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories unto you.

Only once in a day of Brahma does Lord Caitanya appear in this world to spread love of Kṛṣṇa through His *saṅkīrtana* movement. While here, He made a prediction that His *saṅkīrtana* movement would spread to every town and village all over the world.

Only once in a day for Brahma does the Lord need a servant to fulfill that mission of spreading the *saṅkīrtana* movement. On this rare occasion He has to chose one qualified servant out of all the infinite number of living entities to accomplish this great mission.

He chose you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Here I am, sitting at my typewriter, trying to figure out something relevant to write for your glorification, but considering how exalted you actually are, no big phrases are big enough to describe your true glories.

I can only prostrate myself in obeisances before your lotus feet, hoping that one day, through your mercy, I will be purified enough to properly offer prayers to you, just as Dhruva Mahārāja was able to offer prayers to the Lord only after he had been purified by the touch of the Lord.

*Your servants in Goteborg, Sweden,*

Paramānanda dāsa / Manupriya dāsa / Mandahāsa-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Kini / Bhaktin Anette

## Hamilton

*vaiṣṇava ṭhākura doyāra sāgara,*  
*e dāse koruṇā koni*  
*diyā pāda-chāya śodho he āmāya*  
*tomāra caraṇa dhori*

**"O venerable Vaiṣṇava, O ocean of mercy, be merciful unto your servant. Give me the shade of your lotus feet and purify me. I hold on to your lotus feet."**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Nobody at this time could dare say that you have left us, for certainly anybody who thinks like that reasons ill. You will live forever with us in your books—in every word and every syllable. Now you have gone to join Lord Gaurāṅga, the great dancer. And certainly we do not find the strength to carry on alone the *saṅkīrtana* of the holy name of Hari. Please bless us by giving us just one drop of faith with which to obtain the great treasure of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa.

We, the insignificant disciples and granddisciples of Your Divine Grace, are making our humble attempts to freely distribute the message of Lord Gaurāṅga. You have so mercifully ordered us to go to every home in every town and village in New Zealand—and it is only through your grace that our preaching endeavors are successful. All this has happened since the time you first set foot on this land.

We have a very nice farming community supplying the devotees with milk and fresh vegetables, and also a *gurukula* for the young children. In the city we have two large restaurants distributing *delicious prasādam* to the masses of people. Book distribution is constantly increasing, and in the smaller towns Nāma Haṭṭa programs are being developed. All this is certainly due to your causeless mercy.

Here in Hamilton we hold regular Sunday feasts, which are maintained with the help of your devotees who regularly attend. By your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your ISKCON movement is spreading more and more. O please, by a drop of your mercy, bless us that we may work together in harmony and surrender.

We are eternally indebted to you. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray for the guidance and assistance of yourself and your representatives, so that we may always increase our service to you by always preaching your divine message. On this glorious day, we wish that the whole world would come and glorify your exalted position.

*Your humble servants at ISKCON Hamilton, New Zealand,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Aṣṭa-siddhi dāsa / Sanātana dāsa / Jñānamūrti-devī dāsī

## Ibadan

*vande ‘haṁ śrī-guroḥ śrī-yuta-pada-kamalaṁ śrī-gurun vaiṣṇavāṁś ca*  
*śrī-rūpaṁ sāgrajātaṁ saha-gaṇa-raghunāthānvitaṁ taṁ sa jīvam*  
*sādvaitaṁ sāvadhūtaṁ parijana-sahitaṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanya-devaṁ*  
*śrī-rādhā-kṛṣṇa-pādān saha-gaṇa-lalitā- śrī-viśākhānvitāṁś ca*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We were at the crossroads, and we could neither leave nor come with you. We lingered over the choice, but in the darkness of our doubt, we saw in your face the road that we should take.

The light of knowledge of the self that Kṛṣṇa has unveiled through you to redeem the conditioned souls of Kali-yuga is the real goal of life. It is difficult, though, to keep the devotees together, but by the unlimited mercy you are relentlessly bestowing on us, we are endeavoring.

We have no capacity of our own to surrender to the will of Kṛṣṇa, but by our remaining as instruments of this glorious engagement, we can actually reach great heights.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we take shelter of your lotus feet. All glories to Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga!

(written by Gaṅgā dāsa)

*Your servants in Ibadan, Nigeria,*

## ISKCON Vaiṣṇava Academy

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

It's early Saturday morning, and the Florida sun is still too young to manifest its scorching rays. Cool breezes adorn the tree-lined streets of Gainesville as sleeping souls, having temporarily shaken off one aspect of their ignorant covering, begin to engage in other kinds of ignorance. Forgetful of their real self-interest, some begin to play, some to work on their lawns, some to get ready to go shopping—a myriad of activities devoid of *ātma-tattva.*

Onto this scene come three smiling young boys riding bicycles, each with an conspicuously full cloth bag hanging from the handlebars. Their glances dart to and fro, as if looking intently for something. Suddenly they spy a middle-aged man reading a newspaper on his front lawn. One bike breaks formation and rapidly zeroes in on this "target." Slamming on his breaks as he leaps from the bike, the boy approaches the man, holding something he took from his bag. The spiritual potency of untold eons, as delivered by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is about to manifest.

"Good morning, sir, how are you today?" Startled, but pleasantly so, the man looks up and grunts a positive response. "Here's a gift from our school. It's called *Easy Journey to Other Planets,* and it tells about the special kind of *yoga* we study."

After a short conversation, the man takes the book and gives the boy a dollar. They wish each other well, and the young man, mounting his trusty bike, begins pedaling furiously to join up with his friends. Meanwhile, the man has opened the book and begun to read about the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

A variation on the theme: That afternoon a housewife in her late twenties leaves her kitchen to respond to a knock on the front door. Opening it, she finds three young girls, *tilaka* adorning their bright faces. A few minutes later, the door closed and the girls on their way, she sits down on her sofa and begins to read her newly purchased magazine, *Back to Godhead.*

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, even Ananta Śeṣa, with His unlimited mouths, cannot properly describe your amazing accomplishments: hundreds of transcendental literatures produced, millions distributed; hundreds of temples opened, in each one of which the Supreme Lord has agreed, at your request, to reside in His *arca-vigraha* form; farms; restaurants; schools; devotees serving millions upon millions of plates of spiritual food, *prasādam,* accepted by Kṛṣṇa because we offer it to you, and you, in turn, offer it to Him; men and women of all races, nationalities, and faiths, voices joined together in chanting the holy names; Ph.D's producing papers and books, organizing lectures and conferences, with a one- pointed goal, as you instructed them: to use modem science to prove the existence of your beloved Lord; a transcendental city rising in the holy *dhāma* of Māyāpura, the fulfillment of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda's vision, that Vaiṣṇavas from all over the world will come and worship Lord Gaurāṅga.

The list of your accomplishments is endless, all testifying to the reality of your empowerment, proclaiming that you are a *śaktyāveśa avatāra* come to spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement to every town and village on this planet.

But on this Saturday, in northern Florida, by your grace, I watch an amazing transcendental drama unfold, a miracle bearing witness, perhaps more than anything else, to your potency as Lord Kṛṣṇa's pure representative. A second generation, most born not in Bhārata-varṣa but rather all over this condemned world; many born after you ended your manifest physical presence on this earth; most never having seen you personally or heard you speak; all foregoing their "natural" childish propensities to always play and engage in frivolous activities; instead taking up that most exalted of all activities, that activity more dear to the Lord than any other in the three worlds: preaching the timeless message of Godhead.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious day of all, on your appearance day, I pray that all of us—your disciples, your grand-disciples, indeed, all those in the human form of life—be benedicted that we may get a glimpse of your potency, of your actual position. If only this can remain forever in our minds, then we will always beg for the shelter of your infinitely potent lotus feet, which are the only safe place and only means out of this horrible material quagmire. Please forgive our unlimited offenses and never kick us away; please always engage us in the service of your beloved Lordships, Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

On behalf of the Vaiṣṇava Academy, the teachers and students, your disciples, granddisciples, and granddisciples to be, I beg to remain

*Your eternal servant,*  
Muralī-vādaka dāsa

## Krishnafest

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. I am your wayward son who is begging for your causeless mercy upon me. Although undeserving, I pray that you will not reject this useless rascal. You have given me ample opportunity for progressing in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, but I have carefully neglected surrender of my false ego. Now I'm perplexed because I have embarked upon a monumental task, which frankly I am not qualified to perform. I know that you desire that the task be accomplished, and similarly it is obvious that you have designated the responsibility to this fallen wretch. I cannot run and hide away, because this would bring infamy and would only prolong the agony of nonsurrender.

I must stand and fight, armed with *yoga,* the weapon of detachment, and transcendental knowledge. Unfortunately, *yoga,* knowledge, and detachment are not in my arsenal, which instead consists largely of bluffs, false prestige, mundane manipulative maneuvers, lusty desires, etc. What am I to do, my spiritual master? This is no poetic exercise in humility; it is honest to the bone. I need a complete overhaul, a rebuilding of my engine or a heart transplant. It doesn't matter how I acquire the proper necessities, but if I am to be an instrument for you, I need help.

It is not fair that I've posed as a fit candidate to execute your bidding and now, just as the battle is to begin, I become filled with fear, overwhelmed with bewilderment and illusion. Arjuna experienced this, but that was due to Kṛṣṇa's *yogamāyā,* and also Arjuna was so qualified that he attentively heard the *Bhagavad-gītā* and then acted as the Lord desired. But I'm covered by the Lord's modes of ignorance and passion. Kindly pick me up, wash me off, and fix me as one of your faithful, successful servitors at your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your transcendental qualities are unlimited and indescribable. Nevertheless, we become purified by praising Your Divine Grace eternally. We shall not forget how you patiently bore, like a long pregnancy, the desire of your Guru Mahārāja to deliver the English-speaking population, and how at the appointed hour that desire gave birth to the divine ISKCON movement. We shall not forget how you began *Back to Godhead* magazine while in the *gṛhastha āśrama.* We shall not forget how you renounced the home, family, nation, and material world in order to fulfill your master's order. We shall not forget how you single-handedly wrote and published the first canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and carried those books with you to the United States for distribution.

We shall not forget that you patiently rewrote the entire *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* because the original manuscript was stolen. We shall not forget how you prayed to your Lord Kṛṣṇa during the *Jaladuta* sea journey. We shall not forget how you lived in the Bowery amidst drunkards and drug addicts. We shall not forget how you traveled continuously to protect this movement. We shall not forget that you are the transparent medium who channeled eighty volumes of Vedic literature from the spiritual realm.

O beloved Gurudeva, we shall not forget the vows we made at the time of our initiation. We shall not forget how seeing your books distributed gave you the greatest joy. We shall not forget how your transformed the hearts of the younger generation in places like Tompkins Square Park, Haight-Ashbury, Morningstar Ranch, MIT, etc. We shall not forget the gratitude you displayed toward scholars who appreciated your books.

We shall not forget to visit the pilgrimages you built for us in Vṛndāvana and Māyāpura. We shall not forget how you established *gurukula* for our children to become pure devotees. We shall not forget how you solidly kicked Darwin in the face with boots. We shall not forget how you set up the Governing Body Commission to manage your society. We shall not forget how you created *sannyāsīs* to travel widely and preach boldly on your behalf. We shall not forget the final lesson you taught on how to leave this world. Nor, Śrīla Prabhupāda, shall we forget to maintain what you have left us and, in a cooperative spirit full of love for Your Divine Grace, try to expand.

We at Krishnafest are enthusiastically traveling and presenting Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the United States. Please give your blessings that this program may increase by quantum leaps and bounds.

We beg to remain your eternal servants.  
(written by Dānavīra dāsa Vanacārī)

*The devotees of Krishnafest,*

Guṇagrāhī Goswami / Dānavīra dāsa Vanacārī / Saṅkarṣaṇa dāsa / Vālmīki dāsa / Kalki dāsa / Bhakta Dave / Bhakta Mike / Bhakta Jeffrey / Bhakta Jason / Bhakta Juntin / Bhakta Darrell

## Liberia

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We offer our humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

"We were born in the darkest region of ignorance, and our spiritual master opened our eyes with the torchlight of knowledge. We therefore offer our most respectful obeisances unto the dust of his lotus feet."

Śrīla Prabhupāda, though a father is merciful, most of the time a grandfather is much more merciful. Most often we ask our father to give us the same mercy that you bestowed upon him. And he will reply, "It can be achieved if we patiently follow the principles of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, live a lifetime spent in work for the *guru,* and never ask for anything except service."

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we thank you very much for giving us a chance to taste the nectar of devotion.

When you first came to Africa, you were involved in an accident, but still you never gave us up. When you were leaving the planet, you sent your most merciful son to carry on your mission, and he is doing that very powerfully. Presently on the continent of Africa we have temples in all the major cities and towns and are meeting with chiefs and government officials and holding television and radio programs all over Africa—all by your divine mercy. You sent a handful of devotees to the continent, but today you have hundreds of granddisciples.

You are more merciful than Lord Caitanya, as Lord Caitanya is more merciful than Kṛṣṇa. Lord Caitanya predicted that the holy name would be chanted in every town and village, but no one except you made it a reality. Because of your great devotion. Lord Caitanya is showing His mercy even to the most sinful souls.

We thank our spiritual master, Bhakti Tīrtha Swami, for serving you with such attention that your presence and compassion are flowing unlimitedly.

Paraṁ vijayate śrī-kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtana!

*Your servants in Liberia, Africa.*

## Liverpool

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet!

Every day we see the family of ISKCON growing, with more temples being opened and Nāma Haṭṭas being established—the prediction of Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura being manifested before our very eyes.

This is all a result of the fathomless compassion and mercy of Your Divine Grace.

If you had not left Vṛndāvana at the age of seventy, if you had not chanted in Tompkins Square Park, if you had not translated *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* where would we be and what would we be doing? It is a frightening thought! No Deities, no temples, no *harināma,* no books, no philosophy, no devotees, no *prasādam*!

When we think of your selfless, single-handed effort—writing, printing, distributing BTGs in Delhi, alone for one year in New York—what are we? What is our meager effort?

We are simply like an ugly duckling stumbling in the wake of a great swan.

*Your servants in Liverpool, U.K.,*

**Granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Marco Ferrini Design

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances unto your lotus feet. All glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga.

We want to thank you for having given us the opportunity to come in the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

The Kṛṣṇa consciousness philosophy is the most noble and sublime way for a worthy human life. Its literature is the most interesting reading, at the same time inspiring and pleasing to the soul. Its heroes, the Lord and His devotees, are constant reference points. Every day they inspire and purify our lives by their powerful, ever-fresh, vivid examples. I thank you for having inspired in us a very strong desire to serve more and more this *bhāgavata* philosophy and literature, both by applying them in our everyday lives and by working to help the circulation of this culture.

The Kṛṣṇa conscious devotees are the best people, the only ones able to help human beings in their spiritual life. Lord Kṛṣṇa's devotees are the true friends of all creatures. Thank you for having transformed our bad previous characters into these new ones, able to desire to be the servants of all your devotees.

Lord Kṛṣṇa's *prasādam* is the best food, nutritious and delicious, healthy for both body and soul—the only food worthy to be taken. Thank you for having transformed our tastes for material food into the hankering for pure, spiritual *kṛṣṇa-prasādam.*

Deity worship in Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the best among all the rites. The cleanliness, punctuality, and opulence help us judge the devotion involved in the worship. In our Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa temples there is a high quality of Deity worship going on. Thank you for having transformed our previous propensities to adore material paraphernalia into an ever-increasing pleasure in dancing, playing, and singing devotional songs in front of our beloved Deities.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we deeply feel ourselves very grateful towards Your Divine Grace for having given us the opportunity to know this great way of life, based on the chanting of the holy names of the Lord. This simple and great science of self-realization is powerful enough to respiritualize the people of this whole planet, with their materialistic civilization.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for having taken us into your spiritual family. We were poor, ignorant wanderers in the material world. We shall be indebted to Your Divine Grace forever.

On this very auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we are taking the opportunity to thank Your Divine Grace more and more, hoping to remain your humble servants and be able to keep the shelter of your lotus feet.

You are our worshipable Deity in the form of the spiritual master, and you are the most wonderful devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. You are a genuine, bona fide Vaiṣṇava *ācārya,* and we are hoping to become worthy servants of the servants of your devotees.

*Your eternal servants,*  
Matsyāvatāra dāsa and Manu-patnī-devī dāsī

## Marseille, Nice and Toulon Nāma-haṭṭa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who so joyfully bestowed Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu's mercy on our Western countries, which are surcharged with hate, agnosticism, hypocrisy, and voidism!

We are tiny little parts of the big ISKCON family you generated from your own faith, and we like to be so, because there is so much pleasure in it. Of course, as yours is not an ordinary family, it is not surprising that there is so much going on all the time. But everything has its purpose. Many of your sons are maturing, leading the way to Kṛṣṇaloka, where you are surely enjoying your transcendental pastimes with the Lord and at the same time waiting for those you have trained on the path of perfection. Some failed along the way, forgetful of your divine instructions, and are again entrapped in the miseries of material life. But you give your blessings to all, hoping the unreasonable ones, once tamed by the material laws of nature, will come to their senses and serve your mission faithfully. And to the serious ones you give the real taste in life—*ānandāmbudhi-vardhanaṁ prati-padaṁ pūrṇāmṛtāsvādanam.*

One thing is certain, Śrīla Prabhupāda: it is safer to stay under your spiritual protection than to go around looking for prestige and adventure in "the world of names." The only interesting names are the holy names you gave us, and the only blissful adventure in life is to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in every town and village of the world—*sarvātma-snapanaṁ param vijayate śrī-kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtanam.*

When distributing your books, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are always with you, defeating all classes of atheists. When presenting Kṛṣṇa consciousness as it is, in our devotee clothes and with shaved heads, as you showed us, we are respected by both the so-called high-class people and by the dangerous fools. When distributing *kṛṣṇa-prasādam,* we drive away all speculation, fear, and ignorance. Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for engaging us in this wonderful pastime of the Lord. There is no end to such activity, and we are certain that by your unlimited mercy, the transcendental happiness derived from it will overflow and inundate millions upon millions of conditioned souls.

SAṄKĪRTANA-YAJṢA KĪ JAYA!

*Your servants in Marseille, France,*

## Ministry of Vaiṣṇava Studies

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

There was a time when Śrīla Prabhupāda did not exist for us. The world was a finite realm of birth and dying, and despair ran like dark alleys through the city of our hearts. We spent ourselves in madness, bewildered by fleeting glimpses of identity that flickered before us as lightning fractures a summer storm. We ran fast and hard toward an invisible, undefined destination, hoping, despite frustration, that the goal and purpose of our flight would appear.

Into this chaos descended Śrīla Prabhupāda. Coming down from his ecstatic immersion in meditation on Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara, that most courageous preacher worshiped the teachings and instructions of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī in the core of his heart and came to us.

We didn't know he was coming. We didn't even know what or how we lacked or how to be students, how to listen, or whom to listen to. Still he came, unswayed by the cold technological enemy that rose like a giant on the horizon of the battlefield. Arrogant and sure, the demon of impersonalism and envy laughed contemptuously at the sky, amazed that Śrī Kṛṣṇa would send just *one* person to combat his invincible power.

Boldly, patiently, with infinite compassion, Śrīla Prabhupāda entered our confused hearts and began to soothe us with his mercy. It was painful to stand on shaking, newborn legs of faith—while the old securities of sense gratification and escapism were stripped away and gradually replaced with a higher taste. For us who had never trusted, who had never loved—for us to surrender our opinions and speculations, to which we clung so desperately, is an indication of the power of this teacher and the immense attraction of his love.

Sometimes we wonder if Śrīla Prabhupāda still exists, if his mercy disappeared with his form, if we are now abandoned, orphaned, and alone. But it is our prayer that we may recover each of his precious instructions as if they were Deities who had become lost and covered by the material forest. We must uncover them and install them on the altar of our hearts. We must worship them in celebration with *saṅkīrtana* and cooperation. We must offer to their lotus feet golden vessels of renewed dedication garnished with self-honesty. We must decorate them in silken finery shining with the richly glowing colors of enthusiasm and determination. And somehow, we must cause the world to come before their illumination, to take *darśana* of their grace and shelter, and to share freely the unlimited *prasādam* he has revealed.

(written by Aghāri dāsa)

*Your servants in the Ministry of Vaiṣṇava Studies (Washington, D.C.),*

**Washington, D.C., area members**

## Newcastle

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your ISKCON movement.

There are at the moment just four full-time devotees serving here at your new "embassy" in Newcastle-upon-Tyne, England. At present you have a rented three-floor property nicely situated in the center of the city, a little like the temple of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Londoniśvara.

Last August, a friend of your movement—Valerie from Edinburgh—donated one thousand pounds for us to construct and decorate the temple room, which has been operational since last October. There is a picture of Śrī Pañca-tattva on the altar, which was made by Dave and Harry. They also made and donated your *vyāsāsana.*

The devotees are maintaining the regular temple schedule as you have established it and chanting their prescribed number of rounds. Daily we have a few guests at the morning program. Weekly the Sunday Feast and Festival is steadily increasing, with about 20-30 guests at present. The devotees maintain the temple by distributing your books on the streets in the towns of northeastern England. About 30% of the gross income goes to the BBT.

Recently we drew up a preaching schedule to ensure progress in all departments. These include *hari-nāma saṅkīrtana,* book distribution, *prasādam* distribution, university preaching, door-to-door preaching, a Hare Kṛṣṇa festival (planned for the end of this year), locating another property (to purchase, rather than to rent), and our new *bhakta* program. We want to make at least six new devotees this year.

Normally, the staff of an embassy consists of nationals from the mother country; rarely are local residents employed. We therefore feel ourselves to be most fortunate, since you are employing us, who were very much residents of the "local" material world, and transforming us and the people we contact on your behalf into enthusiastic preachers of the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Thank you for your causeless mercy.

*Your humble servants at Newcastle-upon-Tyne, U.K.,*

**Granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Divyasiṁha dāsa / Bhakta Brian / Bhakta Bill / Hlādinī-śakti-devī dāsī

**Congregation**
